For `PROJECT_AS`, it must carry origin type, target context, bounded target function, constitutive source features, validity scope, source-change sensitivity, and alternatives including analogy or no projection.

These are prose semantics supplied by Chapter 4. The schema may require fields and flag category conflicts; it may not decide whether a formation relation is constitutive, reference identity is real, or a target function is semantically warranted.

#### Three minimal declarations from the shared source family

**COMPOSE occurrence**

```text
occurrence: C4-CMP-01
kind: COMPOSE
source: K0, E1/T1, K1, N1/T2, K2
target: P_review as bounded path composite
formation rule: selected actual traversal under review-process relation
preserved: temporal order, transition relation, bounded path identity
compressed: conversational and minor procedural detail
excluded: rival selections not constitutive of this target
uncertain: minor intervening acts
local result: admissible_with_bounded_claim
continuation: later operation requires separate occurrence
```

**DECOMPOSE occurrence**

```text
occurrence: C4-DEC-01
kind: DECOMPOSE
source: P_review
target: finer reconstruction of the same P_review
finer distinction: adoption-through-event versus persistence-through-non-realization
preserved: path reference and actual traversal
compressed: some source-document variation
excluded: rival responsibility-path formation
uncertain: boundary of minor intervening acts
local result: admissible_with_bounded_claim
continuation: projected function not yet tested
```

**PROJECT_AS occurrence**

```text
occurrence: C4-PROJ-01
kind: PROJECT_AS
source: P_review
origin type: path
target context: later process-review meeting
target function: bounded orientation for monitoring promised reconsideration and reopening burden
preserved: source reference, origin type, constitutive path features
foregrounded: event/non-event and transition relation
excluded: universal institutional function and decision authority
uncertain: validity outside the declared meeting question
local result: admissible_with_bounded_claim
continuation: no origin-type replacement
```

The three declarations share one source family but produce non-interchangeable targets:

```text
composite path object
≠ finer reconstruction of that path
≠ contextual function performed by that path
```

#### Chain declaration boundary

A chain is represented by several Minimal Operation Declarations linked through explicit handoffs. It is not represented by expanding `kind` into a list.

```text
link 1 target
→ declared as link 2 source
→ identity and availability checked
→ link 2 tested independently
```

Every link retains its own local Output Class. An integrated summary may describe the relation among links but may not flatten their results into one verdict.

#### Formal and authority boundary

Completeness of the Minimal Operation Declaration establishes only that the occurrence is explicit enough to test. It does not establish:

- empirical truth;
- actual causality;
- semantic adequacy;
- source sufficiency;
- Type Integrity in fact;
- admissibility;
- normative validity;
- application authority;
- or completeness of capture.

```text
complete declaration
≠ valid transformation
≠ true claim
≠ authority
```

#### WP3 boundary and handoff

Sections 4.8–4.10 establish:

- the three canonical non-invertibility statements;
- reverse-looking movement as a new testable occurrence;
- preservation and loss reassessment under apparent reversal;
- the integrated operation-confusion matrix;
- mandatory separation of collapsed chains;
- Non-Capture for unresolved operation identity;
- the conceptual Minimal Operation Declaration;
- mapping to existing Shared Record paths without schema duplication;
- three non-interchangeable declarations from one source family;
- formal and authority limits of declaration completeness.

WP3 does not yet perform:

- integrated Chapter 4 synchronization and completion testing;
- final case-duty assignment;
- the formal-model delta decision;
- Chapter 4 provisional lock;
- detailed procedures from Chapters 15, 20, and 30;
- the complete Shared Transformation Record from Chapter 7.

The WP3 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality attaches to the WP4 integrated audit and lock decision, not to a known local defect in Sections 4.8–4.10.

#### Chapter 4 integrated completion and provisional lock

Sections 4.1–4.10 now form one integrated operation grammar. The completed chapter fixes a closed inventory of exactly three core operations, occurrence-level exclusivity, the three source–target signatures, independent coordinate declarations, separate chain links, bounded non-invertibility, confusion routing, and the prose-bound Minimal Operation Declaration.

```text
operation identity
→ one occurrence
→ one canonical operation kind
→ one declared source–target relation
→ preservation and loss duties
→ local result
```

The integrated completion test confirms:

- `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` remain mutually distinguishable;
- no comparison, validation, translation, recontextualization, chain, audit act, output, Stop, or Non-Capture condition creates a fourth operation;
- abstract Δ–Ψ operator types remain non-decomposable;
- `PROJECT_AS` preserves origin type and assigns only a bounded contextual function;
- every chain consists of separately identified occurrences and separately retained local results;
- reverse-looking movement is a new testable claim rather than an automatic inverse;
- the Minimal Operation Declaration maps to existing Shared Transformation Record paths and creates no second schema;
- declaration completeness, formal validity, or operation classification does not establish truth, admissibility, authority, or complete capture.

The integrated Chapter 4 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 4 is provisionally locked. The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves legitimate later revision pressure from Chapter 5 continuity and context, Chapter 6 admissibility, Chapter 7 record integration, Chapter 8 non-equivalence audit, the detailed operation procedures in Chapters 15, 20, and 30, operation-chain cases, LIMITS, appendices, corpus audit, and model finalization. Reopening requires a documented contradiction, failed case burden, ownership conflict, schema pressure, or later source-supported boundary finding.

<a id="chapter-5-origin-type-target-function-and-transformation-context"></a>

## 5. Origin Type, Target Function, and Transformation Context

A transformation can remain verbally coherent while quietly changing what its source object is, what role the target is supposed to perform, or which context makes that role meaningful. The resulting analysis may preserve a name while losing the referent, preserve a referent while replacing its type, or preserve a type while assigning a function that no longer depends on the source structure. Chapter 5 establishes the continuity discipline needed to prevent those substitutions.

The governing question is:

> What must remain continuous, and what may change, when a source object is reconstructed or projected across contexts, granularities, or relative levels?

The chapter begins with three terms that must not collapse:

```text
origin type
≠ target function
≠ transformation context
```

An **origin type** identifies how the source object is analytically typed in the source reconstruction. A **target function** identifies a bounded role that the source object may perform in a declared target context. A **transformation context** declares the wider relation-space in which the source–operation–target claim possesses analytical purchase and a limited validity scope.

The distinction is especially important for `PROJECT_AS`, because projection permits a source object to perform a new bounded function without becoming a new origin type. The distinction also constrains `COMPOSE` and `DECOMPOSE`: a newly formed target object receives its own explicit typing, and a finer reconstruction may revise a prior typing claim, but neither operation may silently rewrite the historical source record.

Chapter 5 defines the foundational continuity criteria later consumed by Chapter 6, recorded by Chapter 7, operationalized by RETYPE, and systematized under LIMITS. It does not yet perform the complete Admissibility Band, serialize the full Shared Transformation Record, or supply the detailed `PROJECT_AS` procedure.

### 5.1 Origin Type

The **origin type** is the declared analytical type under which a source object is identified in its source reconstruction before a later target function is assigned or tested.

```text
origin type
= source-side analytical typing
  of an identified object
```

The origin type may be:

- an operator-typed occurrence;
- a composite structure;
- a configuration;
- an event or non-event structure;
- a transition;
- a sequence;
- a path;
- a trajectory;
- or another already warranted derived analytical object.

It is not automatically one of the abstract Δ–Ψ operator types. A review path can have origin type `path`; a transition can have origin type `transition`; a composite can have origin type `composite structure`. Only where the source object is itself an operator-typed occurrence is an operator type directly relevant to its typing.

```text
origin type
≠ operator type automatically
```

This distinction prevents the operator inventory from becoming a universal object taxonomy. Δ–Ψ remain the fixed PMS Base operator types. STRATA objects, occurrences, composites, paths, trajectories, and derived analytical objects may be typed without being promoted into additional operators or forced into an operator type that their source reconstruction does not warrant.

#### Origin type and source identity

The source object and its origin type are related but non-identical claims.

```text
source object
≠ reference identity claim
≠ origin type
```

The source object answers **what object the transformation is about**. The reference identity claim answers **how that object is identified and tracked**. The origin type answers **under which analytical type that source object enters the transformation**.

For example:

```text
source object: P_review
reference identity: the bounded review path reconstructed from K0 through K2
origin type: path
```

The name `P_review` does not establish either reference identity or origin type by itself. The path identity depends on the selected configurations and transitions, actual traversal, temporal order, formation relation, and declared losses established in Chapters 3 and 4. The origin-type declaration depends on that warranted source reconstruction.

#### Origin type is not permanent essence

Origin type is neither a metaphysical essence nor an irreversible label.

```text
origin type
≠ permanent essence
≠ immunity from revision
```

A source typing may be provisional, narrowed, or revised when new evidence or a warranted finer reconstruction changes what can responsibly be claimed. The discipline is not that every earlier typing must survive forever. The discipline is that revision must be explicit, source-supported, and temporally honest.

A later record may therefore state:

```text
earlier typing: path
later revised typing: limited sequence
reason: actual traversal no longer supported
```

What it may not do is preserve the old record’s wording while using a later target function to imply that the earlier object had always possessed a different type.

```text
later function
≠ retrospective source-type rewrite
```

A failed or revised origin claim remains part of the transformation history. A new context, granularity, or function does not erase it.

#### Origin type, target object, and target function

`COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` relate typing differently.

For `COMPOSE`, several source structures form a new composite analytical object. The composite receives its own target-object typing. The source structures retain their recorded source typings.

```text
source typings
+ formation relation
→ new target object with declared target typing
```

For `DECOMPOSE`, the target is a finer reconstruction of the same reference object. The finer reconstruction may confirm, narrow, revise, or reject the coarser typing claim, but the revision must be stated rather than smuggled in as added detail.

For `PROJECT_AS`, origin-type preservation is mandatory:

```text
origin type preserved: required
bounded target function: declared separately
origin type replaced by function: prohibited
```

The target function does not become a target-object type merely because the source object performs it successfully.

#### C5-OT-01 — origin type preserved under projection

The shared source family supplies:

```text
K0
→ E1 / T1
→ K1
→ N1 / T2
→ K2
→ P_review
```

`P_review` is the bounded review path formed in Chapter 4. Its source-side declaration is:

```text
source object: P_review
origin type: path
source frame: review-process reconstruction
source granularity: configurations, events, non-events, and transitions
source relative level: wider than one meeting turn, narrower than the whole institution
source temporal scope: K0 through K2
```

A later process-review configuration `K3` asks how promised reconsideration and accumulated reopening burden should be made visible. The path may be tested as performing a bounded frame-function in that context. Nothing in that function changes the source-side declaration:

```text
P_review remains a path
while performing one bounded function in K3
```

The positive origin-type finding is therefore local and precise:

```text
origin type: preserved
origin-type replacement: absent
```

This finding does not yet establish full reference, functional, or temporal continuity. Those dimensions receive separate criteria in Sections 5.4–5.7. It establishes only that the projection claim has not confused a performed function with the source object’s analytical type.

If the same record instead states:

```text
P_review has become Frame
```

then the reference object may still be recognizable, but the type claim has crossed the Chapter 5 boundary. A function named with operator language does not transform the source into the abstract `Frame` type.

```text
function language
≠ origin-type conversion
```

### 5.2 Target Function

A **target function** is a bounded relational role that an origin-typed source object performs within a declared target context for a specified analytical purpose and validity scope.

```text
target function
= source-carried role
  in a bounded target relation
```

The function is not an additional object hidden inside the source. It is not a permanent property discovered by moving to a higher level. It is a relation among:

- an identified source object;
- load-bearing source features;
- a declared target context;
- a specified analytical purpose;
- and a bounded validity scope.

```text
target function
≠ target object type
≠ new primitive
≠ permanent identity
≠ authority
```

A target function is therefore **contextual**, **source-sensitive**, **defeasible**, and **claim-bounded**.

#### Naming the function precisely

A function must state what the source object does in the target context. Labels such as “important,” “structural,” “memory,” “frame,” “constraint,” or “attractor” are insufficient when they do not specify the relational work being claimed.

A testable function declaration has the form:

```text
source object X
performs function F
for purpose P
within target context C
under validity scope V
because source features S1...Sn carry F
```

For `P_review`, a bounded function can be stated as:

```text
P_review performs a frame-function
for the question of promised reconsideration and reopening burden
within the later process-review configuration K3
```

The function is not “being a Frame.” It is the bounded performance of orienting attention, comparison, and issue selection for one declared review question.

#### Load-bearing source features

A function must remain traceable to specific source features. In the running case, the proposed frame-function depends on:

- the actual adoption event and transition into the operative arrangement;
- the explicit promise of later reconsideration;
- the bounded non-realization of that promised review;
- the transition into a configuration carrying unresolved commitment;
- and the accumulated procedural residue affecting reopening.

Those features are not decorative background. They explain why this path, rather than an arbitrary dated sequence, can perform the proposed function.

```text
contextual usefulness without constitutive source trace
≠ functional continuity
```

Section 5.6 later defines the full functional-continuity test. WP1 fixes the entry burden: a target function must already identify what source features are alleged to carry it and what kind of material source change would require the function claim to change.

#### Source-change sensitivity

A target function is source-sensitive when a material alteration to its load-bearing source features weakens, alters, or defeats the function.

Consider a counterfactual source variant in which `N1`, the source-supported non-realization of promised reconsideration, is removed. Suppose instead that the reconsideration was completed within the promised window.

The original function cannot remain unchanged:

```text
remove bounded non-realization N1
→ promised-reconsideration residue changes
→ reopening-burden function must weaken, alter, or fail
```

A broader path may still perform another function. But a function centered on unresolved promised reconsideration cannot remain identical when the unresolved non-realization disappears.

```text
material source change
→ target-function change required
```

If the same function survives every relevant source alteration, the function is likely carried by the analyst’s preferred label or by the target context alone rather than by the declared source object.

#### Target function is not analogy

An analogy may remain useful below the threshold of a target function.

```text
P_review is like a frame
```

This statement may identify resemblance: the path helps foreground some matters and background others. But resemblance alone does not state the target context, purpose, validity scope, constitutive source features, or source-change sensitivity.

The weaker result can remain:

```text
analogy_only
```

The stronger `PROJECT_AS` claim requires the full bounded function relation. Chapter 5 does not force every useful resemblance either upward into projection or downward into uselessness.

#### C5-TF-01 — bounded frame-function

The positive function claim is:

```text
source object: P_review
origin type: path
target context: process-review configuration K3
target function: bounded frame-function
purpose: orient one review question toward promised reconsideration,
         bounded non-realization, and reopening burden
validity scope: the named review question in K3
load-bearing source features: E1/T1, promise, N1/T2, accumulated residue
excluded claims: universal institutional framing, decision authority,
                 person evaluation, permanence
```

The function foregrounds the difference between completed adoption and non-realized review. It does not determine what decision participants should make. It does not prove a general institutional pattern. It does not convert the path into `Frame` as an origin type.

At the WP1 level, the local candidate is:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

This is a provisional chapter-local candidate because the full continuity matrix and contextual-boundedness test remain to be completed in WP2 and WP3.

#### C5-TF-02 — same source, different compatible functions

The same source object may perform different compatible functions in different target contexts.

In `K3`, the review question concerns whether promised reconsideration and reopening burden are being adequately tracked:

```text
P_review
+ K3
→ bounded frame-function
```

In a distinct target context `K4`, the question concerns why a later reopening request carries accumulated procedural residue. The same path may perform a bounded memory-function:

```text
P_review
+ K4
→ bounded memory-function
```

The memory-function is not a claim that the path has become a universal `Memory` operator occurrence. It is a limited role: preserving a source-traceable account of why current procedural burden cannot be understood from the present configuration alone.

The two functions require separate claims:

| Declaration | `K3` frame-function | `K4` memory-function |
| --- | --- | --- |
| target context | later process-review question | later reopening-explanation question |
| analytical purpose | orient issue selection and comparison | retain relevant historical explanation |
| load-bearing features | promise, non-realization, transitions, residue | ordered path, unresolved commitment, accumulated residue |
| validity scope | one review question | one explanatory question |
| failure condition | source no longer carries promised-review burden | historical path no longer relevant to present residue |

```text
same source object
+ different target contexts
→ potentially different compatible functions
```

This rule does not license unlimited functional elasticity. If every context can assign any function regardless of the source object’s specific structure, the source object has ceased to do analytical work.

#### Provisional source, bounded function

A target function may be locally useful while one source component remains provisional. The function must inherit that uncertainty as a limitation.

Suppose the exact boundary of `T2` remains partly uncertain while the promise, non-realization window, and later procedural residue are sufficiently documented. The function claim may state:

```text
source component T2: provisional
function dependency on T2: limited
function claim: bounded and provisional
```

The uncertainty may support:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

It may not be converted into certainty merely because the target function is useful. Function assignment is not an uncertainty-erasing operation.

### 5.3 Target Context and Transformation Context

A target function cannot be interpreted responsibly without knowing where it is proposed to operate. Chapter 5 therefore distinguishes **frame**, **target context**, and **transformation context**.

```text
frame
≠ target context
≠ transformation context
```

A **frame** bounds a reconstruction by specifying what relations are included, foregrounded, or excluded for the analytical object under examination.

A **target context** is the target-side scene, relation, configuration, or analytical use within which a target function is proposed.

A **transformation context** is the declared relational envelope governing the source–operation–target claim. It includes the operation occurrence, analytical purpose, source and target reference relation, relevant coordinates and sources, target context, temporal reach, validity scope, Claim Ceiling, exclusions, uncertainty, and revision conditions.

```text
frame
→ bounds a reconstruction

target context
→ identifies the target-side scene or relation

transformation context
→ governs the source–operation–target relation,
  purpose, source relevance, validity, and claim ceiling
```

The three terms may overlap in a simple case. They remain non-equivalent because they answer different questions.

#### Frame versus target context

A source and target may be reconstructed under the same analytical frame while the target context changes.

For example, both `K3` and `K4` may use a review-process frame. Yet `K3` is a process-review meeting asking how to orient issue selection, while `K4` is a reopening-explanation setting asking why present burden carries historical residue.

```text
stable frame
+ changed target context
→ potentially changed target function
```

Conversely, a target context may remain the same while the analytical frame changes. A meeting can be reconstructed procedurally, temporally, or responsibility-relationally without becoming a different meeting. The frame change may alter what function can be warranted, but frame change alone does not establish `PROJECT_AS`.

#### Target context versus transformation context

The target context is one component of the transformation context, not its synonym.

A declaration such as:

```text
target context: K3 process-review meeting
```

still leaves open:

- which source object is being projected;
- which operation occurrence is claimed;
- which source features are relevant;
- which source and target coordinates apply;
- what temporal reach the function possesses;
- what validity scope limits it;
- what Claim Ceiling and exclusions apply;
- which uncertainties and revision conditions remain.

The transformation context supplies that wider envelope.

#### Minimal transformation-context burden

For one `PROJECT_AS` occurrence, the transformation context must make at least the following relation visible:

```text
operation occurrence
+ analytical purpose
+ source reference and origin type
+ source coordinates and source scope
+ target context and target coordinates
+ target function and validity scope
+ relevant source trace
+ temporal reach
+ claim ceiling and exclusions
+ uncertainty and revision conditions
```

This is conceptual prose. Chapter 7 later records the complete declaration. Chapter 5 does not create a second record schema or new top-level fields.

The current Shared Transformation Record can represent the relation through existing paths, including:

```text
/source/reference_object
/source/object_typing
/source/frame
/source/granularity
/source/relative_level
/source/temporal_scope
/source/source_scope
/source/constitutive_source_trace

/operation/kind
/operation/transformation_context

/target/reference_object
/target/object_typing
/target/contextual_function
/target/frame
/target/granularity
/target/relative_level
/target/temporal_scope
/target/validity_scope
```

```text
conceptual context declaration
≠ second Shared Transformation Record
```

#### C5-CTX-01 — same frame, different transformation context

Compare two projection claims using the same source object `P_review` and the same broad review-process frame.

**Occurrence A**

```text
target context: K3 process-review meeting
purpose: orient one review question
function: bounded frame-function
validity scope: promised reconsideration and reopening burden
```

**Occurrence B**

```text
target context: K4 reopening-explanation setting
purpose: reconstruct why present burden has historical residue
function: bounded memory-function
validity scope: one explanatory question
```

The frame remains stable. The transformation contexts differ because purpose, target relation, function, validity scope, exclusions, and re-entry conditions differ.

```text
same frame
≠ same transformation context
```

The reverse also holds:

```text
same target context
≠ same transformation context
```

Two analysts may address `K3` but use different source objects, operation claims, source traces, or validity scopes. The target scene alone does not settle the transformation relation.

#### Transformation context and authority

A well-declared transformation context bounds a claim. It does not grant action authority.

```text
contextual fit
≠ normative validity
≠ decision entitlement
≠ authority inheritance
```

The frame-function may help organize a review question. It cannot decide the review outcome, authorize sanctions, rank persons, or inherit authority from the source path, the analyst, or the formal record.

#### WP1 boundary and handoff

Sections 5.1–5.3 establish:

- origin type as source-side analytical typing rather than operator type or permanent essence;
- explicit separation of source object, reference identity, origin type, target object, and target function;
- mandatory origin-type preservation under `PROJECT_AS`;
- target function as a bounded, source-carried, defeasible role;
- source-change sensitivity as an entry burden for function claims;
- same-source/different-context compatible functions as separate testable claims;
- frame, target context, and transformation context as non-equivalent;
- transformation context as the wider source–operation–target validity envelope;
- mapping to existing Shared Record paths without schema duplication;
- four controlled local cases: `C5-OT-01`, `C5-TF-01`, `C5-TF-02`, and `C5-CTX-01`.

At WP1 completion, the chapter did not yet complete:

- reference continuity criteria;
- type-integrity failure routing;
- the full functional-continuity test;
- temporal continuity;
- contextual boundedness across later or parallel contexts;
- the complete Minimal Projection Form;
- the integrated continuity matrix;
- final Chapter 5 synchronization and provisional lock.

The WP1 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality attaches to the remaining Chapter 5 continuity criteria and integrated lock, not to a known local defect in Sections 5.1–5.3.

### 5.4 Reference Identity and Reference Continuity

A transformation claim must first state what object it concerns. **Reference identity** identifies the historical or analytical object named by the claim. **Reference continuity** asks whether that object remains sufficiently identifiable across the tested source–operation–target relation.

```text
reference identity
→ what object is claimed

reference continuity
→ whether that object remains traceably identifiable
  through the transformation relation
```

The two questions are related but not interchangeable. A source object may be well identified while a later target claim silently substitutes another referent. Conversely, a derived target object may be new and still retain full traceability to the source objects from which it was formed.

```text
same name
≠ same reference object

new target object
≠ reference discontinuity automatically
```

Reference continuity therefore does not require numerical identity between every source and target. It requires the kind of reference relation appropriate to the declared operation.

#### Minimum reference-continuity burden

A positive reference-continuity finding requires at least:

1. an identified source object and bounded source reference;
2. a declared target reference object or target function;
3. the operation-specific source–target reference relation;
4. preservation or explicit revision of constitutive relations;
5. historical or analytical boundary continuity at the level required by the claim;
6. traceability through the sources that make the object identifiable;
7. disclosure of substitution, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable loss;
8. a test of whether the same label now points to a different object.

Reference continuity is not established merely because two descriptions share a title, identifier, actor, date range, document, or broad topic.

```text
nominal recurrence
+ missing constitutive bridge
→ no reference-continuity proof
```

The continuity burden is claim-relative. A narrow target function may require continuity only for the source features that carry that function. A claim that the complete historical object has been preserved requires a stronger bridge. Narrow scope does not permit silent substitution; it permits a bounded continuity claim.

#### Operation-specific reference relations

For `COMPOSE`, the target composite is a new analytical object. Reference continuity concerns the traceability of its selected source structures and the formation relation that constitutes the composite.

```text
COMPOSE:
new composite target
+ traceable source references
+ declared formation relation
```

The new object need not be identical to any source object. It must not erase or misidentify the sources from which it is formed.

For `DECOMPOSE`, the target is a finer reconstruction of the same reference object. Same-object continuity is therefore constitutive.

```text
DECOMPOSE:
same reference object
+ finer relational reconstruction
```

If the selection and formation rule produce another object, the claim is not a successful decomposition of the stated source, even when the new object is analytically useful.

For `PROJECT_AS`, the source reference remains visible while a bounded target function is added.

```text
PROJECT_AS:
source reference retained
+ origin type visible
+ target function declared separately
```

A projection that replaces the source referent with a target-side label, metaphor, or operator identity lacks the reference bridge required by the operation.

#### C5-REF-01 — reference continuity preserved

The source object remains `P_review`, the bounded review path formed from:

```text
K0
→ E1 / T1
→ K1
→ N1 / T2
→ K2
```

A later claim proposes that `P_review` perform a bounded frame-function in `K3`. The target claim continues to refer to the same historical review path. Its constitutive bridge retains:

- the original path boundary from `K0` through `K2`;
- the ordered adoption and promised reconsideration;
- the bounded non-realization `N1`;
- the transitions that alter reopening burden;
- the source documents used to reconstruct those relations;
- the distinction between the path and the later function.

The target context does not replace the referent. It supplies the scene in which the same source path is tested for one bounded function.

```text
reference object retained: P_review
origin type retained: path
target function added: bounded frame-function in K3
```

The reference-continuity finding is positive within the declared scope. It does not establish functional continuity by itself. The local candidate remains:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

provided the type and functional burdens are also carried.

#### C5-REF-02 — nominal sameness without reference continuity

A later document uses the label `P_review` for a different collection:

```text
later P_review label:
  two follow-up meetings
  no K0 boundary
  no adoption transition E1 / T1
  no promised reconsideration relation
  no bounded non-realization N1
  expanded endpoint beyond K2
```

The name remains unchanged. The referent, temporal boundary, source basis, selection rule, and constitutive formation do not.

```text
same label
+ changed referent
+ changed formation
→ reference discontinuity
```

The later collection may be a legitimate new object. It may require a new `COMPOSE` occurrence and a new identity claim. It cannot inherit the earlier path’s reference continuity by name.

If the claim is reduced to “a later collection was also called `P_review`,” the nominal statement may remain. If the analyst insists that the two objects are the same path without a constitutive bridge, the stronger transformation claim is:

```text
failed_transformation
```

Reference failure does not authorize the analyst to repair the object by adding a target function, changing frame, or selecting a wider level. Each such movement would be a new claim and would not erase the failed continuity claim.

#### Reference continuity and uncertainty

Reference continuity may remain provisional where one part of the source bridge is unresolved but the bounded referent is still sufficiently identifiable for a narrower claim.

```text
identifiable core
+ unresolved peripheral boundary
→ admissible_but_provisional candidate
```

Provisionality must name:

- the uncertain boundary or relation;
- the part of the reference claim that remains supported;
- the stronger identity claim that is withheld;
- the evidence or test required for re-entry.

Provisional continuity is not a license to treat unknown identity as established identity.

---

### 5.5 Type Integrity and Type Continuity

**Type integrity** requires source typing, target-object typing, and target function to remain visible and non-substitutable throughout a transformation. **Type continuity** asks whether the relevant typing remains warranted across the tested relation or is revised explicitly when new evidence or reconstruction requires it.

```text
type integrity
→ no hidden substitution between type and function

type continuity
→ warranted preservation or explicit revision of typing
```

The terms overlap but are not identical. Type integrity is violated whenever a function, resemblance, context, or target label silently replaces source typing. Type continuity may still permit a justified revision, provided the new claim is explicit, source-supported, and independently tested.

```text
explicit warranted type revision
≠ hidden type replacement
```

#### Type declaration across operations

For `COMPOSE`, source types and the new target-composite type must be declared separately. The composite does not retroactively retype its components.

For `DECOMPOSE`, finer reconstruction may confirm, narrow, qualify, or defeat the coarser typing. The result must identify what changed and why.

For `PROJECT_AS`, the strongest invariant is:

```text
origin type preserved: required

target function declared separately: required

origin type replaced by function: prohibited
```

A path that performs a frame-function remains origin-typed as a path. A trajectory that performs a memory-function remains origin-typed as a trajectory. A useful relation to `Frame`, `Memory`, `Attractor`, or another operator does not transform the source object into the abstract operator type.

```text
functional similarity
≠ operator identity
```

#### Minimum type-integrity burden

A type-integrity assessment must state:

1. the source object’s origin type;
2. the target object’s type, where a target object is created;
3. the target function, where a function is assigned;
4. whether the source type is preserved, narrowed, revised, or rejected;
5. the evidence or reconstruction supporting any revision;
6. whether a function or analogy is being mistaken for a type;
7. whether an abstract operator type is being treated as a decomposable occurrence;
8. whether the proposed typing exceeds the source and Claim Ceiling.

The declaration must remain intelligible even when reference continuity passes. The same referent can be typed incorrectly.

#### C5-TYPE-01 — reference preserved, type integrity failed

The analyst continues to refer to the same historical `P_review`. The path boundary, source trace, and constitutive relations remain identifiable. Reference continuity therefore passes.

The analyst then declares:

```text
P_review has become Frame as its origin type.
```

The statement collapses a bounded target function into source typing. The source path may orient a later review question in a frame-like way, but it has not become the abstract `Frame` operator type.

The dimensions separate:

```text
reference continuity: supported

type integrity: failed

functional similarity: potentially retained
```

The appropriate stronger disposition is:

```text
failed_transformation
```

If the analyst is warned and nevertheless insists that the function replaces the origin type, continuation reaches the Chapter 5 Stop boundary reserved for WP3:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The valid weaker claim is not lost. The source object may remain a path and may still be tested for a bounded frame-function.

#### Type revision without retrospective rewriting

Type continuity is not a demand that every first typing remain permanently fixed. A `DECOMPOSE` occurrence may expose that an object called a path lacked actual traversal or that a presumed event was only a record entry. A new source may establish that a previously provisional non-event did occur.

A warranted revision requires:

```text
prior typing visible
+ new evidence or finer reconstruction
+ reason for revision
+ revised scope
+ effect on downstream claims
```

The revised claim begins at the point of revision. It does not rewrite the historical record as though the earlier uncertainty or error had never existed.

```text
later warranted revision
≠ earlier claim immunity
≠ retrospective certainty
```

Every function or chain link that depended on the prior typing must be retested. Type revision is therefore a new testable claim and a possible source of downstream claim reduction.

#### Partial and provisional type findings

A composite may contain separable components with different type-integrity results. A target claim may preserve the path typing of `P_review` while leaving one internal transition provisional. The overall result need not be forced into one binary verdict.

Possible bounded findings include:

```text
supported type integrity for the source object
+ provisional typing for one component
→ admissible_but_provisional candidate
```

or:

```text
supported origin type
+ failed target-object typing
→ partially_admissible candidate
```

Such mappings remain local candidates. Chapter 6 owns the integrated Admissibility Band routing. Chapter 5 supplies the distinctions that prevent one successful dimension from compensating for another failed dimension.

---

### 5.6 Functional Continuity

**Functional continuity** is the traceable, source-sensitive relation by which a proposed target function is carried by specific features of the source object and remains bounded to a declared target context and validity scope.

```text
functional continuity
= precise target function
+ constitutive source trace
+ source-change sensitivity
+ contextual boundedness
```

Functional continuity is not continuity of wording, usefulness, resemblance, or analyst intention. A label can remain stable while the source support disappears. A source object can remain identifiable and correctly typed while failing to carry the proposed function.

```text
reference continuity
≠ functional continuity

type integrity
≠ functional continuity

contextual fit
≠ functional continuity
```

#### Minimum functional-continuity test

A positive functional-continuity finding requires:

1. a precisely stated target function;
2. a declared target context and analytical purpose;
3. a bounded validity scope and temporal reach;
4. identified load-bearing source features;
5. an explanation of how those features carry the function;
6. a counterfactual test of relevant source change;
7. visibility of rival functions, analogy, and no-projection alternatives;
8. preservation of origin type and source reference;
9. explicit loss, uncertainty, and failure conditions;
10. no authority inheritance from utility or formal declaration.

The test asks not merely whether the source object can be used in a target context, but whether its specific structure matters to the proposed function.

```text
source-independent function
→ empty label or functional discontinuity
```

#### C5-FUNC-01 — source-sensitive functional continuity

The tested claim is:

```text
source object: P_review
origin type: path
target context: K3 process-review meeting
target function: bounded frame-function
purpose: orient one question about promised reconsideration and reopening burden
```

The function is carried by a specific source pattern:

- adoption occurred through `E1 / T1`;
- reconsideration was promised in `K1`;
- the expected review did not occur within the bounded condition `N1`;
- `T2` altered later reopening conditions;
- the path carries accumulated procedural residue into `K2`.

These features matter because the frame-function distinguishes completed adoption from unfulfilled reconsideration and directs attention to why reopening now carries historical burden.

The counterfactual pressure removes `N1` and substitutes a completed timely review with a documented resolution.

```text
remove N1
+ add completed reconsideration
→ promised-reconsideration residue changes
→ reopening-burden function must weaken or change
```

The target function therefore responds to a material source change. Within `K3` and the named review question, the local candidate is:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The result does not authorize a decision, establish institutional recurrence, or make the path a universal Frame.

#### C5-FUNC-02 — metaphorical association below the function threshold

A second analyst writes:

```text
P_review is like a frame because it highlights later issues.
```

The statement identifies a resemblance but does not specify:

- the target context;
- the precise target function;
- the analytical purpose;
- the validity scope;
- the load-bearing source features;
- the source-change test;
- rival functions or failure conditions.

The resemblance may remain useful as an analogy. It does not yet support `PROJECT_AS`.

```text
metaphorical association
+ no constitutive function burden
→ analogy_only
```

This is not a failed observation. It is a bounded weaker output. Inflating it into a target-function claim would require claim reduction or produce a failed transformation.

#### C5-FUNC-03 — relevant source change defeats the function

A third variant retains the same target-function wording after materially changing the source object:

```text
variant source:
  N1 removed
  timely reconsideration completed
  no accumulated reopening residue

declared function:
  unchanged frame-function for unfulfilled reconsideration
```

The proposed function now refers to a source feature that is absent. The source object may remain a valid path, and another function may be available, but this specific function is no longer carried by the source.

```text
material source feature removed
+ function held invariant
→ functional continuity failed
```

The broad claim must be reduced or rejected:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

If no narrower source-carried function remains, the projection is:

```text
failed_transformation
```

The prior successful function in the original source configuration does not validate the changed-source claim. A material source change creates a new testable projection claim.

#### Compatible functions and non-compensation

`P_review` may still perform a separately supported memory-function in `K4`. Success of the memory-function does not compensate for failure of the frame-function in a changed `K3` source variant.

```text
one supported function
≠ all proposed functions supported
```

Likewise:

```text
reference continuity supported
+ type integrity supported
+ functional continuity failed
→ function claim still fails or reduces
```

No continuity dimension grants credit that can be transferred to another. The dimensions must be recorded separately.

#### Functional continuity under uncertainty

A target function may remain provisional where its load-bearing source trace is mostly supported but one material relation remains unresolved.

A provisional result must identify:

- which source feature is uncertain;
- why the function remains plausible;
- how the uncertainty limits the function;
- what alternative remains open;
- what evidence would confirm, narrow, or defeat the claim.

```text
bounded support
+ material unresolved relation
→ admissible_but_provisional candidate
```

Where two rival functions remain equally compatible with the available source and no discriminating evidence exists, Chapter 5 must leave room for `non_capture`. WP3 supplies the dedicated case and re-entry form.

#### WP2 boundary and handoff

Sections 5.4–5.6 establish:

- reference identity and reference continuity as separate but related claims;
- operation-specific reference relations for `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS`;
- nominal sameness as insufficient for continuity;
- type integrity as non-substitution among source typing, target-object typing, and target function;
- type continuity as warranted preservation or explicit revision rather than rigid permanence;
- reference-pass/type-fail as a valid mixed result;
- functional continuity as precise, source-carried, source-sensitive, and context-bound;
- analogy as a legitimate weaker result below the function threshold;
- relevant source change as a required pressure test;
- separate local outputs for distinct continuity dimensions;
- six controlled cases: `C5-REF-01`, `C5-REF-02`, `C5-TYPE-01`, `C5-FUNC-01`, `C5-FUNC-02`, and `C5-FUNC-03`.

At WP2 completion, the chapter did not yet complete:

- temporal continuity;
- the non-erasure rule for later functions and earlier history;
- contextual boundedness across parallel, later, wider, or unrelated contexts;
- the complete Minimal Projection Form;
- the integrated four-dimension continuity matrix;
- the dedicated Mandatory Stop and Non-Capture cases;
- final Chapter 5 synchronization and provisional lock.

The WP2 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality attaches to the remaining Chapter 5 criteria and integrated lock, not to a known local defect in Sections 5.4–5.6.
---

### 5.7 Temporal Continuity

**Temporal continuity** is the preservation of the historically relevant order, duration, carry-over, and transformation history required for a later object or function claim to remain traceable to its source.

```text
temporal continuity
= relevant historical order
+ source-to-target carry-over
+ non-erasure of prior claims and failures
+ bounded temporal reach
```

Temporal continuity does not require a complete chronology or the preservation of every source detail. It requires that the temporal relations carrying the claim remain reconstructible and that later analytical use does not convert a temporally formed object into a timeless property.

```text
temporal continuity
≠ exhaustive chronology
≠ losslessness
≠ permanent identity
≠ timeless property
```

The relevant temporal burden depends on the object and function under examination. A projected function carried by a single event may require a bounded event interval and its immediate before/after relation. A function carried by a path or trajectory may require ordered configurations, transitions, non-events, historical residue, and the later context in which that load remains active.

#### Minimum temporal-continuity test

A positive temporal-continuity finding requires:

1. an identifiable source temporal object or temporal boundary;
2. the order relations relevant to the target claim;
3. the duration, interval, or carry-over relations that materially support the function;
4. a declared bridge from source time to target time;
5. visibility of compression, omitted intervals, and uncertain edges;
6. preservation of earlier provisionality, failure, Stop, or Non-Capture findings;
7. a bounded target-time validity claim;
8. no conversion of path, trajectory, sedimentation, or non-event structure into a timeless attribute.

The source-to-target bridge may be direct or mediated. Direct temporal continuity exists where the same bounded occurrence remains under examination. Mediated temporal continuity exists where a later function depends on a source-supported historical chain whose relevant load remains traceable into the target context.

```text
later relevance
≠ temporal continuity automatically
```

A later analyst may find an earlier object useful. That usefulness becomes temporally continuous only where the later claim remains sensitive to the relevant order and historical load of the source object.

#### Compression without temporal erasure

Temporal compression is admissible where it preserves the order and heterogeneity required by the claim.

A compressed statement such as:

```text
the review procedure became harder to reopen
```

may remain admissible only if its source trace preserves the distinction among:

- adoption through `E1 / T1`;
- promised reconsideration in `K1`;
- bounded non-realization through `N1`;
- altered reopening conditions through `T2`;
- the later configuration `K2`.

Compression becomes temporally discontinuous when these heterogeneous relations are collapsed into one instantaneous or naturalized property.

```text
historically formed burden
≠ timeless burden
```

A macro-level summary may compress several intervals. It may not represent a temporally accumulated trajectory as though it had always existed in the source object.

#### C5-TEMP-01 — temporal continuity under later function

The tested projection retains `P_review` as a path and proposes a bounded frame-function in the later process-review configuration `K3`.

The source-side temporal structure is:

```text
K0
→ E1 / T1
→ K1
→ N1 / T2
→ K2
```

The target claim does not require every meeting turn or every intervening document. It does require:

- adoption before the reconsideration promise became operative;
- the bounded expectation window associated with `N1`;
- non-realization before the altered reopening conditions represented by `T2`;
- the persistence of that history into `K3`.

The later frame-function is therefore stated as:

```text
within K3,
P_review orients one bounded review question
because its ordered history distinguishes
completed adoption,
promised reconsideration,
bounded non-realization,
and later reopening burden
```

The projection preserves temporal order, retains the historical source trace, and limits validity to the declared later review context. The local temporal-continuity candidate is:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The finding does not establish a permanent institutional tendency, future recurrence, or a timeless property of the project.

#### Later function does not erase earlier history

A valid later function may coexist with an earlier provisional or failed claim.

Suppose the original reconstruction of one internal transition remained provisional while the broader path identity was supported. A later bounded function cannot rewrite the earlier record as fully certain merely because the function is useful.

```text
later valid function
≠ earlier claim upgraded automatically
```

Likewise, a later context cannot erase:

- an earlier `failed_transformation`;
- a prior `mandatory_stop`;
- a prior `non_capture` boundary;
- an earlier claim reduction;
- an explicitly uncertain temporal edge.

A new transformation is a new testable claim. It may use a surviving weaker source claim, but it may not conceal the failure history of the stronger one.

#### C5-TEMP-02 — trajectory converted into timeless property

A counterclaim states:

```text
the project is inherently resistant to reconsideration
```

The available source supports, at most, one bounded review path with accumulated reopening burden through `K2`. The counterclaim removes:

- the order of adoption, promise, and non-realization;
- the declared temporal scope;
- the possibility of later revision;
- the distinction between historically formed residue and permanent identity.

```text
bounded trajectory or path
→ timeless project property
```

This is temporal discontinuity and claim overreach. A narrower historical claim may be retained:

```text
within the reconstructed K0–K2 path,
reopening burden increased
```

The timeless property claim requires:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

If the analyst insists that the historically bounded pattern proves permanent project identity, the continuation must receive:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop protects temporal traceability and prevents a local historical formation from becoming a person-, group-, or institution-type judgment.

#### Temporal continuity and non-invertibility

Temporal continuity does not restore a pre-transformation source state. A later reconstruction, composition, or projection begins from the source object available at that occurrence and preserves the relevant transformation history.

```text
preserved temporal trace
≠ restored untouched origin
```

This relation carries Chapter 4 non-invertibility into continuity discipline without redefining irreversibility or the operations themselves.

---

### 5.8 Contextual Boundedness

**Contextual boundedness** is the requirement that a target function, continuity finding, and validity claim remain limited to the declared target context, analytical purpose, temporal reach, and claim scope for which they are supported.

```text
contextual boundedness
= declared target context
+ bounded purpose
+ validity scope
+ temporal reach
+ exclusion of automatic transfer
+ revision conditions
```

A function may be valid here and invalid, unnecessary, or differently specified elsewhere.

```text
valid here
≠ valid elsewhere
≠ valid indefinitely
≠ authorized action
```

Contextual boundedness is not isolation. The source object may be tested in several contexts. Each new context creates a new function claim with its own source trace, continuity matrix, alternatives, uncertainty, and local result.

```text
new target context
→ new testable function claim
```

No prior result is automatically inherited.

#### Context-transfer test

A proposed transfer from context `C1` to context `C2` must declare:

1. whether the source object remains the same;
2. whether its origin type remains visible;
3. which features of `C2` make the function relevant;
4. whether the load-bearing source features remain material in `C2`;
5. whether the analytical purpose and validity scope have changed;
6. whether the temporal bridge remains supported;
7. which losses or exclusions arise in the transfer;
8. whether a different function, analogy, or no projection is preferable;
9. which conditions would narrow, defeat, or revise the claim.

Similarity between contexts is not sufficient.

```text
similar context
≠ inherited target function
```

A broader relative level, later date, repeated label, or shared participant group also does not establish transfer.

#### No function or authority inheritance

Contextual transfer must not become authority inheritance.

```text
successful projection in C1
≠ validity in C2
≠ decision entitlement in C2
≠ institutional authority
```

A path functioning as an orientation device in a process-review meeting does not thereby authorize sanctions, determine policy, classify persons, or settle normative legitimacy. The target function remains analytical and bounded.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

#### C5-BOUND-01 — bounded function and failed automatic transfer

In `K3`, `P_review` performs a bounded frame-function for one question about promised reconsideration and reopening burden. A later configuration `K4` concerns archival reconstruction and institutional memory.

The `K3` result cannot simply be copied:

```text
K3 frame-function
→ K4 frame-function automatically
```

Instead, `K4` requires a new test. The same source path may support a distinct bounded memory-function because its ordered source traces preserve how the procedure developed. That possibility is compatible with the earlier frame-function, but it is not inherited from it.

```text
same source object
+ different target context
→ separate function claim
```

The bounded findings may therefore be:

```text
K3 frame-function:
  admissible_with_bounded_claim

K4 memory-function:
  admissible_but_provisional
```

The K4 result remains provisional where the archival source bridge is incomplete. The K3 result neither upgrades nor defeats it.

If the analyst instead declares that `P_review` performs the same frame-function in every future meeting, department, and institution, the claim exceeds the declared Target Context and Source Scope.

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

Insistence on universal transfer requires:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

#### Contextual revision and expiry

A target function must state when it requires revision or expires. Relevant changes may include:

- completion of the promised reconsideration;
- replacement of the governing procedure;
- loss of source trace;
- a changed analytical purpose;
- a target context in which the historical feature is no longer load-bearing;
- a contradictory later record;
- a narrower or wider validity scope.

```text
context change
+ material relation change
→ function retest required
```

A bounded function can remain useful after revision, but it must not be preserved through label inertia.

#### C5-STOP-01 — insisted cross-context authority transfer

A proposed record states:

```text
source object: P_review
origin type: path
K3 function: bounded orientation
K5 claim: binding decision rule for all future reviews
justification: the K3 projection was admissible
```

The K5 claim changes purpose, validity scope, authority relation, and target context. It supplies no new source trace and treats prior admissibility as transferable authority.

The required response is:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The analysis may re-enter only after:

- the K5 target context is declared;
- the proposed function is separated from decision authority;
- load-bearing source features are identified;
- contextual and temporal continuity are retested;
- governance exclusions are restored.

A weaker analytical orientation claim may then be tested as a new occurrence. The prior K3 projection remains neither erased nor inherited.

#### C5-NC-01 — rival functions under unresolved context relation

A later configuration `K6` is incompletely documented. Available records support two rival interpretations of how `P_review` may function:

```text
candidate A:
  bounded frame-function
  for identifying reopening burden

candidate B:
  bounded memory-function
  for preserving procedural sequence
```

The source path is identifiable, and both functions are plausible. The record does not establish which analytical purpose governed `K6`, which participants treated the historical path as operative, or whether the relevant source features carried one function more strongly.

The result is:

```text
non_capture
```

The Non-Capture record must state:

- captured structure: `P_review`, origin type `path`, and the documented K6 relation;
- uncaptured structure: which bounded target function, if any, was operative;
- limiting condition: incomplete target-context and purpose evidence;
- rival claims: frame-function versus memory-function;
- re-entry condition: source-supported target purpose or discriminating functional trace.

Non-Capture does not validate both functions and does not protect a universal-function claim. It preserves the unresolved boundary without forced classification.

---

### 5.9 Minimal Projection Form

The **Minimal Projection Form** is the smallest prose-bound declaration that makes a `PROJECT_AS` candidate inspectable for origin-type preservation, bounded target function, continuity, context, loss, and local result.

```text
Minimal Projection Form
= conceptual declaration
≠ second record schema
≠ projection proof
≠ admissibility proof
```

It complements the Minimal Level Declaration and Minimal Operation Declaration established in Chapters 2 and 4. It does not add a parallel machine vocabulary and does not replace the Shared Transformation Record owned by Chapter 7.

#### Conceptual form

```yaml
source:
  object: X
  origin_type: T_o
  reference_identity: R
  frame: F_s
  granularity: G_s
  relative_level: L_s
  temporal_scope: T_s
  source_scope: S_s
  constitutive_source_trace: [S1, S2, ...]

projection:
  occurrence_id: O
  operation: PROJECT_AS
  transformation_context: C_x

target:
  reference_object: X
  target_context: C_t
  target_function: F_t
  frame: F_tgt
  granularity: G_tgt
  relative_level: L_tgt
  temporal_scope: T_tgt
  validity_scope: V

continuity:
  reference_continuity: declared finding
  type_integrity: declared finding
  functional_continuity: declared finding
  temporal_continuity: declared finding

constraints:
  origin_type_preserved: true
  target_function_bounded: true
  source_trace_required: true
  authority_inheritance: prohibited

loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:

alternatives:
  competing_function:
  analogy_only:
  no_projection:

result:
  local_output_class:
  claim_boundary:
  stop_or_reentry_condition:
```

The indentation above is illustrative prose, not a new serializable schema. The conceptual names `origin_type`, `target_context`, and the four continuity findings are mapped to existing record paths and rule assessments rather than introduced as new top-level fields.

#### Mapping to the existing Shared Transformation Record

```text
source.object
→ /source/reference_object

source.origin_type
→ /source/object_typing

source.reference_identity
→ /source/reference_object
  + /source/constitutive_source_trace

source coordinates and scopes
→ /source/frame
  /source/granularity
  /source/relative_level
  /source/temporal_scope
  /source/source_scope

projection occurrence and context
→ /operation/occurrence_id
  /operation/kind
  /operation/transformation_context

target reference and typing
→ /target/reference_object
  /target/object_typing

target function and validity
→ /target/contextual_function
  /target/validity_scope

target coordinates
→ /target/frame
  /target/granularity
  /target/relative_level
  /target/temporal_scope

continuity findings
→ /admissibility/rule_assessments/*
  + source / target / operation declarations

loss
→ /loss/preserved
  /loss/compressed
  /loss/excluded
  /loss/uncertain
  /loss/irrecoverable

alternatives, stop, governance, and result
→ existing alternatives, stop, governance,
  and result families
```

No new fields such as the following are introduced:

```text
origin_type
target_context
source_level
target_level
continuity_result
projection_declaration
```

The conceptual form uses readable labels while the Shared Record retains its authoritative field structure.

#### Continuity matrix

A positive projection must not hide mixed continuity findings behind one summary label.

```text
reference continuity: supported / bounded / failed / unresolved

type integrity: supported / revised / failed / unresolved

functional continuity: supported / bounded / failed / unresolved

temporal continuity: supported / bounded / failed / unresolved
```

These are prose findings mapped to canonical Output Classes only at the local or integrated result layer. They are not new Output Classes or closed machine enums.

A possible mixed result is:

```text
reference continuity: supported
type integrity: supported
functional continuity: bounded
temporal continuity: provisional
→ admissible_but_provisional candidate
```

Another is:

```text
reference continuity: supported
type integrity: failed
functional similarity: retained
→ failed_transformation
  with analogy_only retained separately
```

No supported dimension compensates for a failed load-bearing dimension.

#### Complete running-case projection declaration

```text
source object:
  P_review

origin type:
  path

reference identity:
  bounded review path from K0 through K2

source coordinates:
  frame: review-process reconstruction
  granularity: configurations, events, non-events, transitions
  relative level: wider than one turn, narrower than the institution
  temporal scope: K0 through K2
  source scope: agenda, transcript, minutes, bounded follow-up record

operation occurrence:
  PROJECT_AS / C5-TF-01

transformation context:
  later process-review inquiry in K3,
  limited to promised reconsideration and reopening burden

target context:
  K3 process-review meeting

target function:
  bounded frame-function for one review question

validity scope:
  K3 and materially equivalent bounded review inquiries only;
  no universal transfer

load-bearing source features:
  E1 / T1 adoption,
  reconsideration promise,
  N1 bounded non-realization,
  T2 altered reopening conditions

continuity findings:
  reference continuity: supported
  type integrity: supported
  functional continuity: supported with bounded claim
  temporal continuity: supported with bounded claim

loss:
  preserved: ordered adoption–promise–non-realization–reopening relation
  compressed: individual meeting-turn detail
  excluded: motive, person typing, institution-wide recurrence
  uncertain: one peripheral follow-up relation
  irrecoverable: undocumented informal discussion

alternatives:
  analogy_only: path is like a frame
  competing_function: bounded memory-function in K4
  no_projection: retain P_review only as historical path

local result:
  admissible_with_bounded_claim

re-entry condition:
  retest after material source, purpose, context, or temporal change
```

This declaration does not prove the projection. It makes the tested claim and its limits auditable.

```text
machine-readable declaration
≠ semantic continuity proof
≠ empirical truth
≠ authority
```

#### Integrated WP3 continuity boundary

Sections 5.7–5.9 complete the four foundational continuity dimensions:

```text
reference continuity
type integrity / type continuity
functional continuity
temporal continuity
```

They also establish contextual boundedness and the prose-bound Minimal Projection Form. The dimensions remain non-compensatory, and each target context creates a new testable claim.

WP3 does not perform:

- the complete Admissibility Band procedure of Chapter 6;
- Shared Transformation Record serialization of Chapter 7;
- detailed `PROJECT_AS` procedure of Chapters 29–30;
- competing-projection adjudication of Chapter 36;
- full continuity-failure routing of Chapter 47;
- integrated STRATA audit of Chapter 53.

The WP3 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 5.7–5.9. Provisionality attaches to final Chapter 5 synchronization, case-duty assignment, formal-model handoff, integrated audit, and provisional lock in WP4.


#### Chapter 5 integrated completion and provisional lock

Sections 5.1–5.9 now form one integrated continuity architecture. The completed chapter fixes source-side origin typing, bounded target function, target and transformation context, four separately tested continuity dimensions, contextual boundedness, source-change sensitivity, and the prose-bound Minimal Projection Form.

```text
identified source object
+ declared origin type
+ operation occurrence and transformation context
+ bounded target context and function
+ reference / type / functional / temporal continuity findings
+ loss, alternatives, uncertainty, and local result
→ auditable continuity claim
```

The integrated completion test confirms:

- origin type remains distinct from abstract operator type, target-object typing, target function, and permanent essence;
- reference continuity, Type Integrity, functional continuity, and temporal continuity remain separately testable and non-compensatory;
- the same source may support different compatible functions only through separately declared contexts, traces, scopes, and results;
- material change to a load-bearing source feature must be capable of weakening, changing, or defeating the target function;
- a later function does not erase earlier uncertainty, reduction, failure, Stop, Non-Capture, or historical load;
- every new target context creates a new testable function claim and inherits neither validity nor authority;
- analogy may remain below the functional-continuity threshold without being inflated into `PROJECT_AS`;
- the Minimal Projection Form maps to existing Shared Transformation Record paths and creates no second schema, new field family, new Output Class, or semantic proof engine;
- complete declaration, formal consistency, or continuity notation does not establish empirical truth, semantic adequacy, admissibility, authority, or complete capture.

The integrated Chapter 5 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 5 is provisionally locked. The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves legitimate later revision pressure from Chapter 6 Admissibility Band integration, Chapter 7 record serialization, Chapter 8 non-equivalence audit, RETYPE and detailed `PROJECT_AS` procedure, Chapter 47 continuity-failure routing, substantive projection cases, LIMITS, appendices, corpus audit, and model finalization. Reopening requires a documented contradiction, failed case burden, ownership conflict, schema pressure, or later source-supported boundary finding.

<a id="chapter-6-the-strata-admissibility-band"></a>

## 6. The STRATA Admissibility Band

The availability of a STRATA operation does not establish that the operation should be carried through, that its result adds a warranted distinction, or that the resulting claim remains sufficiently tied to its sources. `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` make controlled transformations possible. Chapter 6 defines the operating range within which a particular transformation occurrence can count as admissible.

```text
available transformation
≠ admissible transformation
```

The governing question is:

> When does additional resolution, composition, or functional projection change a warranted reconstruction of praxis-relevant structure, and when does it become detail without purchase or abstraction without traceable load?

The chapter answers through a relational **Admissibility Band**. Its lower boundary is the **Praxeological Relevance Floor**. Its upper boundary is the **Praxeological Traceability Ceiling**. The boundaries address different failure modes and are not symmetric.

```text
below the band
→ distinction without praxeological purchase

within the band
→ warranted difference
  with reconstructible source load,
  Type Integrity,
  and Contextual Boundedness

above the band
→ abstraction, fragmentation, or projection
  without traceable load
```

Sections 6.1–6.4 establish the lower-bound architecture. Sections 6.5–6.8 establish the upper-bound and source-dependency tests. Sections 6.9–6.13 now integrate both boundaries with inherited integrity conditions, the unified non-compensatory test, output routing, and the No Universal STRATA Scale rule.

The Admissibility Band does not grade objects, people, domains, operations, or analytical levels. It tests a delimited transformation claim in a declared context and source basis.

```text
Admissibility(T, X, C, Q, S)
```

where:

- `T` is the declared operation occurrence;
- `X` is the identified source object or source set;
- `C` is the transformation context;
- `Q` is the delimited claim being tested;
- `S` is the declared source basis.

No source object, operation type, granularity, relative level, or function is globally admissible independently of those relations.

### 6.1 Why STRATA Requires an Operating Range

STRATA permits analytical movement that ordinary prose often performs without control. A sequence may be composed into a path, a compressed occurrence may be decomposed into finer relations, and a source object may be projected as performing a bounded function in another context. Because each operation can be repeated, elaborated, or reapplied, mere operational availability creates two opposed risks.

The first risk is **indefinite resolution**. A source can almost always be described more finely. Meeting turns can be divided into sentences, phrases, pauses, lexical shifts, or timing differences. A composite can be redescribed through ever more components. Yet additional detail does not automatically alter the warranted reconstruction.

```text
more detail
≠ more discrimination
≠ more warrant
```

The second risk is **unbounded abstraction or projection**. A source set can be gathered under an increasingly powerful macro-label. A local path can be projected as a universal memory, frame, attractor, or institutional logic. Yet rhetorical scope does not establish structural dependence on the source.

```text
broader label
≠ greater traceable load
```

An operating range is therefore required because neither analytical direction contains its own stopping rule. `DECOMPOSE` does not stop merely because a finer distinction is possible. `COMPOSE` does not become warranted merely because several sources can be placed in one set. `PROJECT_AS` does not pass merely because the proposed function is apt, useful, or intelligible.

The band imposes a double burden:

```text
added distinction must matter
and
result must remain source-carried
```

WP1 defines the first burden. Sections 6.5–6.8 define the upper source-load burden. Sections 6.9–6.13 now complete the integrated non-compensatory gate and relational band architecture.

#### The operating range is claim-specific

The same distinction may have purchase for one claim and remain neutral for another. A ten-second delay inside a meeting exchange may be irrelevant to a bounded reconstruction of review-path formation but relevant to a separately supported claim about procedural timing. Conversely, a broad distinction between adoption and non-adoption may be sufficient for one question but too coarse for a claim about promised reconsideration.

```text
relevance for Q1
≠ relevance for Q2 automatically
```

The correct question is not whether a detail is important in general. It is whether the distinction changes a warranted reconstruction of the delimited claim under review.

#### The operating range is not an efficiency rule

The Admissibility Band does not command minimalism for its own sake. A detailed reconstruction may be necessary when several relations are load-bearing. A compact reconstruction may be sufficient when finer distinctions are neutral. The issue is not how much structure is present but what analytical work each distinction performs.

```text
less structure
≠ better analysis

more structure
≠ better analysis
```

The band therefore prevents both premature compression and unnecessary proliferation. It does not prescribe one preferred granularity.

#### C6-RANGE-01 — bounded operating-range candidate

The shared source family remains:

```text
K0
→ E1 / T1
→ K1
→ N1 / T2
→ K2
→ P_review
```

A bounded `DECOMPOSE` occurrence reconstructs `P_review` more finely by distinguishing:

- adoption through `E1 / T1`;
- the explicit promise of later reconsideration;
- the bounded non-realization `N1`;
- the later transition `T2` into altered reopening conditions.

The distinction changes the warranted reconstruction of why reopening in `K2` carries a different procedural burden from initial adoption in `K0–K1`. It preserves the same path referent, the temporal order, the origin typing, and the bounded review frame.

At the WP1 stage, this is an **operating-range candidate** because the lower-bound question receives a positive answer:

```text
changed warranted reconstruction: yes
PraxisPurchase: supported
```

The full admissibility class is not decided by the floor finding alone. Sections 6.5–6.8 test TraceableLoad and Counterfactual Sensitivity; Sections 6.9–6.13 now integrate the non-compensatory gate and final output-routing boundary.

```text
positive floor finding
≠ complete admissibility finding
```

### 6.2 Praxeological Relevance Floor

The **Praxeological Relevance Floor** is the lower relational boundary below which an added distinction, refinement, composition, or functional claim creates no changed warranted reconstruction of praxis-relevant structure for the tested claim.

Canonical formulation:

> **Below the Praxeological Relevance Floor, additional resolution is not an additional praxis finding. It is distinction without praxeological purchase.**

The positive condition tested at this boundary is **PraxisPurchase**.

```text
PraxisPurchase
= claim-specific change
  in warranted reconstruction
  of praxis-relevant structure
```

PraxisPurchase is present when the additional distinction requires a warranted claim to be formulated differently. The change may strengthen, narrow, revise, separate, defeat, or stop a claim. It need not make the claim larger or more dramatic.

#### Changed-reconstruction test

The primary lower-bound question is:

> Which warranted reconstruction must be stated differently because of this additional distinction or transformation?

A responsible answer identifies:

1. the proposed additional distinction;
