2. the delimited claim it is supposed to affect;
3. the praxis-relevant dimension alleged to change;
4. the prior warranted reconstruction;
5. the revised warranted reconstruction;
6. the source support for the difference;
7. the consequence for claim scope, comparison, loss, or Stop.

A compact form is:

```text
proposed distinction D
+ source support S
+ changed praxis dimension P
→ warranted reconstruction Q becomes Q'
```

Where no warranted `Q → Q'` relation can be shown, the distinction has not established additional PraxisPurchase for that claim.

```text
additional information
+ no changed warranted reconstruction
= no additional PraxisPurchase
```

#### PraxisPurchase is not actionability

PraxisPurchase does not require an available intervention, recommendation, or controllable outcome. A distinction may matter because it reveals that an option is closed, a commitment has sedimented, a cost has already been incurred, or an alternative is no longer responsibly reconstructible.

```text
praxis relevance
≠ present actionability
≠ recommendation
≠ authority
```

The Band concerns the reconstruction of praxis structure, not whether an analyst can now change that structure.

#### PraxisPurchase is not salience or usefulness

A vivid distinction may be salient without altering the tested reconstruction. A useful descriptive convention may improve readability without adding a praxis finding. Conversely, an unremarkable distinction may change the distribution of cost, exposure, role, or temporal burden and therefore possess purchase.

```text
salience
≠ PraxisPurchase

usefulness
≠ PraxisPurchase automatically
```

#### Local lower-bound findings and canonical outputs

The lower-bound assessment may record local findings such as:

```text
gain
neutral
below_floor
underdetermined
```

These are not four new canonical Output Classes.

```text
local relevance-floor finding
≠ canonical Output Class automatically
```

A final canonical output requires the complete claim, applicable rule assessments, other boundary findings, and output-selection rationale.

Most importantly:

```text
neutral local finding
≠ resolution_neutral automatically

below_floor local finding
≠ failed_transformation automatically
```

A valid `resolution_neutral` result requires a supported and correctly typed refinement that responsibly demonstrates that the added distinction is not load-bearing for the tested claim. Unsupported detail, object substitution, type confusion, or evasive proliferation does not qualify merely because no gain was shown.

### 6.3 Praxis-Relevant Dimensions

An additional distinction can possess PraxisPurchase when it changes at least one warranted praxis-relevant dimension. The list below is a controlled orientation, not a closed ontology or scoring system.

#### Action corridors and alternatives

A distinction may show that an option was available, conditionally available, narrowed, deferred, or closed.

```text
same observed outcome
+ different available alternatives
→ potentially different praxis reconstruction
```

The relevant difference lies in the reconstructed corridor, not merely the terminal state.

#### Costs, exposure, and risk distribution

A distinction may redistribute who bears cost, delay, uncertainty, vulnerability, or exposure. The change need not be quantifiable to be analytically material, but it must be source-supported and claim-specific.

#### Roles, asymmetries, and dependencies

A finer reconstruction may reveal that apparently similar participants occupy different roles, possess different dependencies, or face unequal capacity to reopen, refuse, delay, or absorb loss.

Role multiplication without changed relational burden has no automatic purchase.

```text
more role labels
≠ changed asymmetry
```

#### Bindings, commitments, and expectations

Promises, adopted procedures, reliance structures, deadlines, review commitments, and expectation frames can alter the meaning of later events and non-events. A non-event is relevant only where an expectation frame and source-supported interval are present.

#### Temporal order, delay, and irreversibility

The order in which events, non-events, and transitions occur may change what later moves remain available or costly. Delay may accumulate burden. Irreversibility may close a corridor within a declared frame.

```text
non-actionable closed corridor
→ may remain praxeologically relevant
```

#### Paths, trajectories, and path-dependence claims

A distinction may alter whether a sequence qualifies as a path, whether a path supports a trajectory, or whether a stronger path-dependence claim remains warranted. Chapter 6 does not redefine those temporal objects; it tests whether the stronger or finer distinction changes the warranted reconstruction.

#### Target functions and transformation context

A distinction may change whether a source object can perform a bounded target function, the scope of that function, or the target context in which it remains valid. A source feature alleged to carry a function must be capable of affecting the function claim.

#### Claim scope, comparison, and failure conditions

PraxisPurchase can consist in narrowing a claim, exposing a countercase, separating mixed results, identifying loss, or establishing that continuation must stop.

```text
claim becomes narrower
or
countercase becomes visible
or
Stop becomes necessary
→ possible PraxisPurchase
```

The floor is therefore not biased toward positive enlargement. A justified reduction or failure boundary can be an analytically valuable difference.

#### C6-FLOOR-01 — genuine resolution gain

A coarse reconstruction states only:

```text
an arrangement was adopted
and later reconsidered
```

The finer source-supported reconstruction distinguishes:

```text
E1 / T1: adoption
promise: later reconsideration expected
N1: promised reconsideration not realized within the bounded interval
T2: transition into altered reopening conditions
```

This changes the warranted claim from a generic two-stage history to a path carrying unresolved promised-reconsideration residue. It changes:

- the expectation structure;
- the interpretation of `N1` as a bounded non-event;
- the temporal relation between adoption and reopening;
- the reconstructed burden attached to later review;
- the scope of any frame- or memory-function claim.

The local floor finding is:

```text
PraxisPurchase: supported
resolution effect: gain
```

No complete Chapter-6 output follows yet. The case must still pass the upper boundary and integrated gates.

#### C6-DIM-01 — non-actionable but praxis-relevant

Suppose the source basis establishes that the promised reconsideration window has closed and that the original adoption cannot be reopened under the declared review procedure. No present action can restore the earlier corridor within that frame.

The distinction between:

```text
reopening still procedurally available
```

and:

```text
reopening corridor closed within the declared frame
```

nevertheless changes the warranted reconstruction of:

- available alternatives;
- accumulated commitment;
- exposure to the retained arrangement;
- the meaning of later review activity;
- the ceiling of any claim that assumes reversibility.

The structure therefore possesses PraxisPurchase even though it supplies no action recommendation and no authority to intervene.

```text
closed corridor
+ changed warranted reconstruction
→ relevance without actionability
```

### 6.4 Distinction without Praxeological Purchase

A distinction lacks additional PraxisPurchase when it adds resolution, description, labels, or components without changing any warranted reconstruction relevant to the tested claim.

Typical forms include:

- temporal over-resolution;
- lexical or stylistic subdivision without relational consequence;
- atomistic component lists;
- role proliferation without changed asymmetry;
- event inflation;
- repeated reformulation of the same claim;
- decomposition pursued only because further decomposition is possible;
- technical precision that changes no typing, path, function, scope, loss, countercase, or Stop condition.

```text
additional detail
+ no changed praxis dimension
+ no changed warranted claim
→ distinction without praxeological purchase
```

This finding does not imply that the detail is false. It means that the detail does not constitute an additional praxis finding for the declared claim.

#### Valid resolution neutrality

A supported refinement may responsibly show that a suspected distinction is not load-bearing. Such a result can have analytical value because it confirms that the coarser reconstruction remains sufficient, closes an unnecessary branch, or prevents false inflation.

```text
supported finer comparison
+ same reference object
+ no changed warranted reconstruction
+ no hidden defect
→ resolution_neutral candidate
```

`resolution_neutral` is therefore not failed analysis. It is a bounded terminal result for a valid comparison that produces no additional praxis finding.

```text
resolution_neutral
≠ failed_transformation
≠ unsupported detail
≠ permission for indefinite continuation
```

#### C6-FLOOR-02 — valid but resolution-neutral refinement

A finer reconstruction distinguishes several source-supported wording changes within the meeting exchange that produced `E1`. The wording differences are real. They do not change:

- the occurrence typing of `E1`;
- the adoption transition `T1`;
- participant roles or asymmetries;
- the expectation of reconsideration;
- the path formation;
- the later target function;
- the claim scope;
- the declared loss;
- or any Stop condition.

The comparison establishes that the wording variants are not load-bearing for the current review-path claim.

```text
source-supported detail: yes
changed warranted reconstruction: no
coarser reconstruction remains sufficient: yes
```

Provided no other admissibility defect is present, the canonical candidate is:

```text
resolution_neutral
```

The result does not add support to the stronger path claim. It establishes only the neutrality of the tested refinement.

#### Below-floor proliferation and Stop

Not every no-gain result deserves `resolution_neutral`. A distinction may remain analytically inert because the analyst has not supplied a delimited comparison, because the same result is repeatedly redescribed, or because the operation is used to postpone an objection.

The lower-bound Stop pressure arises when:

- no warranted reconstruction changes after repeated refinement;
- only terminological subdivision remains;
- added complexity produces no discrimination;
- the source basis cannot support the proposed distinction;
- the operation repeats an already neutral finding;
- or continuation is used to evade a failed claim.

```text
no additional purchase
+ no new delimited test
+ continued proliferation
→ Stop pressure
```

A responsible voluntary end to analysis does not create an eleventh Output Class. Where continuation is explicitly required to cease under the governing rules and the analyst refuses the boundary, the canonical route may become `mandatory_stop`.

#### C6-FLOOR-03 — indefinite detail without purchase

After `C6-FLOOR-02` establishes that wording variation does not affect the path claim, the analyst continues splitting the same exchange into punctuation, pause length, repeated synonyms, and successively smaller turn fragments. No new comparison is stated, no praxis dimension changes, and no countercase or loss becomes visible.

The local assessment is:

```text
additional resolution: present
additional PraxisPurchase: absent
new delimited claim: absent
continuation necessity: absent
```

The claim that each additional split strengthens the reconstruction fails. The coarser result remains available. Continued use of `DECOMPOSE` to imply ever-increasing support must stop.

```text
more resolution
≠ more support
```

If the analyst accepts the boundary, the prior neutral result remains. If the analyst insists that unlimited refinement itself proves greater warrant or uses continuation to defer the objection, the appropriate canonical route is:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

#### WP1 boundary and handoff

Sections 6.1–6.4 establish:

- why operational availability requires a bounded range;
- the Praxeological Relevance Floor;
- `PraxisPurchase` as changed warranted reconstruction;
- praxis-relevant dimensions without a universal checklist score;
- relevance without actionability;
- genuine resolution gain;
- valid `resolution_neutral` refinement;
- distinction without purchase;
- lower-bound Stop pressure;
- five controlled cases: `C6-RANGE-01`, `C6-FLOOR-01`, `C6-FLOOR-02`, `C6-FLOOR-03`, and `C6-DIM-01`.

At the WP1 checkpoint, the following were not yet complete:

- the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling;
- `TraceableLoad`;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity classifications;
- the integrated Type Integrity and Reference Continuity handoff;
- the unified non-compensatory formula;
- complete below/within/above-band routing;
- No Universal STRATA Scale;
- final Chapter 6 synchronization and provisional lock.

The WP1 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 6.1–6.4. Their historical provisionality attached to the then-uncompleted upper-bound and integrated admissibility architecture, not to a claimed full-band passage.

### 6.5 Praxeological Traceability Ceiling

The **Praxeological Traceability Ceiling** is the upper relational boundary above which a transformation result no longer carries reconstructible structural load from its declared source objects. The ceiling applies to all three STRATA operations. A composition may exceed it by replacing heterogeneous source relations with a source-indifferent macro-label. A decomposition may exceed it by fragmenting an occurrence until the reference object, coarser function, or constitutive relation can no longer be reconstructed. A projection may exceed it by assigning a target function whose apparent fit no longer depends on the origin-typed source object.

Canonical formulation:

> **Above the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling, abstraction no longer carries reconstructible praxis load. It becomes a label without traceable source structure.**

The upper boundary is not a prohibition on abstraction, compression, finer analysis, or contextual function. Those are ordinary and often necessary analytical acts. The question is whether the result remains inspectably dependent on the sources it identifies as constitutive.

```text
abstraction
≠ abstraction without traceable load

compression
≠ source erasure

finer reconstruction
≠ source-independent fragmentation
```

#### The ceiling is claim-specific and occurrence-specific

The same analytical object may carry sufficient load for one bounded claim and insufficient load for another. `P_review` may remain traceable as a path whose later reopening burden depends on adoption, promised reconsideration, bounded non-realization, and altered reopening conditions. The same object may fail to support a universal “institutional attractor” claim across unrelated projects and periods.

```text
traceable for Q1
≠ traceable for Q2 automatically
```

The ceiling therefore tests a declared source–operation–target occurrence, not the general quality of a concept or label. A term such as “frame,” “memory,” “path,” or “attractor” is not above or below the ceiling in itself. The tested question is whether the specific target claim remains reconstructible from the declared source load under its transformation context and validity scope.

#### Source mention is not source carriage

A result may contain many citations, quotations, file references, or source identifiers and still fail the ceiling. Citation shows that a source was mentioned. It does not show which features are constitutive, how they are structurally mapped, what was compressed or excluded, or whether the result would change if the declared source load changed.

```text
source citation
≠ structural mapping
≠ source-result dependency
```

The inverse also holds. A compact transformation can remain traceable without reproducing every source detail, provided the load-bearing elements, relations, temporal order, selection, loss, and claim limits remain reconstructible.

#### Operation-specific ceiling questions

For `COMPOSE`, the upper-bound question is:

> Can the composite target be reconstructed from the selected source objects, their declared ordering or constitutive relation, and the disclosed loss profile?

For `DECOMPOSE`, the question is:

> Can the finer result still be traced to the same reference object and to the relations or coarser function that the decomposition claims to open?

For `PROJECT_AS`, the question is:

> Does the bounded target function remain dependent on identifiable source features while origin type, target context, and validity scope remain visible?

No operation receives a privileged direction. “Higher” abstraction and “finer” fragmentation can both exceed the ceiling.

#### C6-CEIL-01 — traceable path reconstruction and bounded projection

Return to the bounded review source family:

```text
K0
→ E1 / T1
→ K1
→ promise of reconsideration
→ N1 / T2
→ K2
→ P_review
```

A `DECOMPOSE` occurrence reconstructs `P_review` more finely by preserving:

- `E1 / T1` as the adoption event and transition;
- the explicit expectation of later reconsideration;
- `N1` as bounded non-realization within that expectation frame;
- `T2` as the transition into altered reopening conditions;
- the temporal order and the same path referent;
- the loss profile covering compressed wording detail and uncertain minor intervening acts.

The target claim is that the finer reconstruction explains why later reopening bears a procedurally different load from initial adoption. The result remains reconstructible from the declared source family and does not require the path to become a new primitive or operator type.

A later bounded `PROJECT_AS` occurrence uses the same path as an orientation function in one process-review meeting. Its function depends on the adoption–promise–non-realization–reopening relation. Removing or materially revising that relation must weaken or alter the function.

The local upper-bound finding is:

```text
constitutive source mapping: visible
relevant temporality: reconstructible
selection and loss: disclosed
source-result dependency: supported
TraceableLoad: supported
```

This is a positive ceiling finding. It does not yet complete the unified Chapter-6 result because Type Integrity, Contextual Boundedness, non-compensation, and final routing remain integrated in WP3.

### 6.6 Traceable Load

`TraceableLoad` is the preserved and inspectable dependency of a transformation result on identifiable source objects, load-bearing features, relevant relations and temporality, declared selection and loss, and bounded claim reach.

```text
TraceableLoad
= reconstructible constitutive source burden
  carried by the tested result
```

A transformation result possesses TraceableLoad only where the analyst can show **why this result depends on these sources in this way**. It is not enough to list inputs. The result must carry a source-to-result relation that another reviewer can reconstruct and challenge.

#### Minimum load-bearing declaration

A responsible TraceableLoad claim identifies:

1. the source objects or source set;
2. the features treated as constitutive rather than merely accompanying;
3. the relations among those features;
4. relevant temporal order, interval, duration, or non-event structure;
5. the selection, ordering, formation, reconstruction, or projection rule;
6. what is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable;
7. the target object, finer finding, or contextual function;
8. the claim segment carried by each source feature;
9. the Source and Claim Ceiling that bound the result;
10. the expected response to a material source change.

A compact dependency statement is:

```text
source features S1...Sn
+ constitutive relations R
+ declared operation occurrence T
+ disclosed loss L
→ bounded result Q
```

If the target could be stated unchanged after removing every alleged source carrier, the declaration has not established TraceableLoad.

#### Structural mapping and dependency

Structural mapping states how source elements correspond to target elements or functions. Dependency adds the stronger claim that the target would be different if a declared load-bearing source relation were materially different.

```text
mapping without dependency
→ may remain descriptive

mapping plus source-sensitive dependency
→ TraceableLoad candidate
```

A diagram, table, or schema path can display mapping. It cannot by itself establish semantic dependency. The justification remains prose- and case-governed.

#### Traceability does not require exhaustiveness

TraceableLoad is compatible with compression. It does not require a lossless duplicate of every source record, all wording, or every possible relation. Exhaustive reproduction can itself obscure which features are load-bearing.

```text
traceability
≠ exhaustive reproduction
≠ zero compression
≠ zero uncertainty
```

What matters is that omitted or compressed material is disclosed and that the remaining structure is sufficient for the bounded claim. A source gap may produce a provisional traceability finding where one coherent claim remains supportable. It does not automatically produce `non_capture`, and it cannot be hidden by an enlarged label.

#### Source and Claim Ceiling handoff

TraceableLoad operates below the Source and Claim Ceilings already controlled elsewhere. A target cannot carry more warrant than the source basis supplies, and it cannot expand beyond the claim that the declared mapping supports.

```text
traceable mapping
+ claim beyond source support
→ Claim Ceiling still exceeded
```

Conversely, a conservative claim does not repair a broken source mapping. Scope restraint and TraceableLoad are distinct duties.

### 6.7 Abstraction without Traceable Load

The Traceability Ceiling is exceeded when a target result remains rhetorically stable while the structures allegedly carrying it can be changed, removed, reordered, or replaced without consequence. The resulting label may still be intelligible, memorable, or heuristically useful. It is not thereby a warranted STRATA transformation result.

Typical failure forms include:

- a macro-label independent of source selection or formation;
- fragmentation that no longer reconstructs the reference object;
- complete compression of heterogeneity, conflict, cost, asymmetry, or non-event structure;
- loss of origin type or historical reference;
- metaphorical source association without structural mapping;
- a target function that remains invariant under material source change;
- citation density without a constitutive source-to-result relation;
- unbounded transfer across contexts, projects, teams, periods, or domains.

```text
powerful label
+ source-indifferent result
→ abstraction without traceable load
```

#### C6-CEIL-02 — universal macro-label without load

An analyst takes `P_review` and projects it as a universal “institutional attractor” governing all future decisions across multiple projects, teams, and periods. The claim does not identify a bounded target context, relevant source features, a validity scope, or a loss profile.

The stress test then changes the alleged source load:

```text
remove the reconsideration promise
replace N1 with completed review
reverse the relevant temporal order
change the target project and participant structure
```

The target label remains “institutional attractor” without revision. The analyst treats the label as self-confirming and cites the original review records only as illustrations.

The local findings are:

```text
source citation: present
structural mapping: absent or generic
source-result dependency: absent
material source-change response: absent
TraceableLoad: failed
```

The universal projection therefore crosses the Traceability Ceiling. A narrower resemblance may remain available as `analogy_only`. A bounded local function may require `claim_reduction_required` and a new test. If no source-dependent target survives, the declared transformation may route to `failed_transformation`. Insistence that rhetorical fit compensates for missing load creates Stop pressure. Final class selection remains assigned to the integrated WP3 gate.

#### Partial load and separable claims

A result may carry traceable load for one segment and fail for another. For example, the local review path may be reconstructible while a universal cross-project function is not. The analyst must separate the claims rather than average them.

```text
traceable local path claim
+ untraceable universal function claim
→ mixed local findings
≠ one compensated result
```

Later output routing may preserve the bounded segment, require reduction of the stronger segment, or record partial admissibility. The upper-bound finding itself does not stack Output Classes.

### 6.8 Counterfactual Sensitivity

**Counterfactual Sensitivity** is a bounded source-dependency stress test. It asks whether a relevant change in a declared load-bearing source structure would alter the composed object, the decomposition finding, or the projected function.

Core question:

> **Would a relevant change in a declared load-bearing source structure alter the transformation result?**

The test does not ask what would actually have happened in an unrealized world. It asks whether the analytical result is genuinely dependent on the source features it names as constitutive.

```text
Counterfactual Sensitivity
≠ causal proof
≠ necessity proof
≠ prediction
```

#### Controlled findings

The local findings are:

```text
sensitive
partially sensitive
insensitive
underdetermined
not testable with available sources
```

- **sensitive** — a relevant source change requires a material change in the tested result;
- **partially sensitive** — one claim segment changes while another remains source-supported;
- **insensitive** — the result remains unchanged despite removal or alteration of an alleged load-bearing feature;
- **underdetermined** — available sources support more than one responsible response and do not warrant selection;
- **not testable with available sources** — the required source relation or interval is unavailable, so the dependency test cannot presently be executed.

These findings are not new Output Classes.

```text
sensitive
≠ admissible automatically

insensitive
≠ failed_transformation automatically

underdetermined
≠ non_capture automatically
```

#### Constructing a responsible source-change test

The analyst must specify:

1. the tested result and claim segment;
2. the feature declared load-bearing;
3. the bounded source change;
4. why that change is relevant rather than arbitrary;
5. the expected target change if the dependency claim is correct;
6. what remains invariant;
7. the evidential and interpretive limits of the exercise.

The change should be local enough to test the declared dependency without silently replacing the entire object or transformation context.

#### C6-CF-01 — source-sensitive result

The bounded orientation function of `P_review` in the later process-review meeting depends on the unresolved promised-reconsideration relation.

The stress test changes the source family:

```text
remove N1
+ insert documented completed reconsideration
+ preserve adoption E1 / T1 and the later meeting context
```

Under this change, the function can no longer orient the meeting around an unresolved reconsideration residue in the same way. It must weaken, change its focus, or fail.

```text
material source change
→ material target-function change
```

The local finding is:

```text
Counterfactual Sensitivity: sensitive
```

This supports source-result dependency. It does not prove that `N1` caused every later outcome, that completed reconsideration would have produced a particular decision, or that the bounded function is globally valid.

#### C6-CF-02 — counterfactual test under source limitation

Assume the available records establish adoption and later reopening pressure but the source interval in which reconsideration may or may not have occurred is incomplete. The analyst cannot determine whether the missing interval contains:

- a completed but undocumented reconsideration;
- an attempted but inconclusive review;
- no review activity;
- or a differently bounded expectation frame.

A source-change test focused on `N1` therefore cannot responsibly decide how the target function would change. The local finding is:

```text
Counterfactual Sensitivity: underdetermined
or
not testable with available sources
```

The analyst must name the missing interval, the rival source possibilities, and the re-entry condition. Where a narrower claim remains coherent—for example, that later reopening pressure is documented without specifying its precise reconsideration mechanism—the result may remain provisional or require reduction. Missing material alone does not authorize a causal inference and does not automatically produce `non_capture`.

#### WP2 boundary and handoff

Sections 6.5–6.8 establish:

- the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling as the upper relational boundary;
- `TraceableLoad` as reconstructible source-result dependency;
- source citation, structural mapping, and functional dependency as non-equivalent;
- operation-specific upper-bound questions for `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS`;
- traceability without exhaustive reproduction;
- source and claim ceiling handoffs;
- abstraction, fragmentation, and projection without traceable load;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity as a dependency stress test rather than causal proof;
- the five controlled sensitivity findings;
- source-sensitive, partially sensitive, insensitive, underdetermined, and not-testable distinctions;
- four controlled cases: `C6-CEIL-01`, `C6-CEIL-02`, `C6-CF-01`, and `C6-CF-02`.

At the WP2 checkpoint, the following remained incomplete:

- the integrated Type Integrity and Reference Continuity handoff;
- the unified non-compensatory formula as a full gate;
- the complete below/within/above-band representation;
- final Output Class routing;
- No Universal STRATA Scale;
- the Formal Model handoff and Chapter 6 provisional lock.

The WP2 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 6.5–6.8. Their historical provisionality attached to the then-uncompleted integrity, non-compensation, routing, scale, model-handoff, and lock architecture.
### 6.9 Type Integrity

The Admissibility Band does not replace the type discipline established in Chapters 1, 4, and 5. A transformation may possess clear praxeological purchase and substantial source trace while still failing because it silently changes what kind of object is being discussed. **Type Integrity** therefore remains a load-bearing condition of admissibility.

```text
PraxisPurchase
+ TraceableLoad
+ failed Type Integrity
= not admissible as declared
```

Chapter 6 does not redefine origin type, operator type, occurrence, composite, target object, or target function. It imports those distinctions as constraints on the band test. The task here is narrower: to show that no gain, elegance, utility, or source density can compensate for an undisclosed type substitution.

#### Required type commitments

A transformation occurrence must keep visible:

1. the origin type of each source object;
2. whether the source is an occurrence, composite, derived analytical object, or abstract operator type;
3. the class of the target object, where an object is formed or reconstructed;
4. the target function, where a contextual function is assigned;
5. any warranted revision of typing;
6. any change in frame, granularity, relative level, or scope;
7. the boundary between a derived object or function and the unchanged PMS primitive inventory.

The operation-specific burdens remain:

```text
COMPOSE
→ may form a new composite object
→ may not rewrite the origin types of its components

DECOMPOSE
→ may refine, revise, or reject an occurrence typing
→ may not decompose an abstract Δ–Ψ operator type

PROJECT_AS
→ preserves origin type
→ declares target function separately
→ does not create a new primitive
```

A legitimate type revision is possible, especially after a finer reconstruction. But it must be supported and declared as a revision. It cannot be introduced retrospectively because a later label appears semantically attractive.

```text
explicit warranted revision
≠ hidden retyping
```

#### Semantic attraction is not type passage

A path may resemble a frame, a trajectory may perform a memory-function, and a repeated composite may orient attention in a way that resembles an attractor. None of these similarities establishes operator identity.

```text
functional fit
≠ operator identity

semantic attraction
≠ Type Integrity
```

A function can be analytically useful while the retyping claim fails. The correct result is then segmented: retain the supported bounded function or analogy, reject the type substitution, and prevent the successful segment from immunizing the failed one.

#### C6-TYPE-01 — semantic attraction without Type Integrity

The source object is the bounded review path `P_review`. Its origin type is `path`. In a later process-review context, the path helps delimit which unresolved commitments must be considered. The analyst therefore proposes:

```text
P_review is now a Frame operator
because it frames later review.
```

The claim preserves the historical referent and cites the relevant source family. It also possesses some praxeological utility. Yet it replaces the origin type `path` with the abstract PMS operator type `Frame` solely because the path performs a frame-like function.

The local findings are:

```text
PraxisPurchase: supported
TraceableLoad: supported for the bounded function
Reference Continuity: supported
Type Integrity: failed for operator retyping
```

The retyping claim is not admissible. A weaker statement may survive:

```text
origin type: path
target function: bounded frame-function in K3
```

If the weaker claim is restated and retested, `admissible_with_bounded_claim` may become available. If only resemblance remains, `analogy_only` may be appropriate. If the analyst insists that semantic attraction authorizes operator replacement, continuation crosses a type boundary and requires `mandatory_stop`.

### 6.10 Reference Continuity

The Admissibility Band also inherits **Reference Continuity**. The transformation must continue to refer to the declared historical or structural source object, except where `COMPOSE` explicitly creates a new composite from declared sources. A source name, citation, identifier, or familiar label is not sufficient if the constitutive referent has changed.

```text
same name
≠ same reference object

source citation
≠ Reference Continuity
```

Reference Continuity asks whether the transformation preserves the conditions that make its own source claim intelligible. These conditions may include:

- the identified source objects;
- their constitutive relations;
- the relevant temporal order and bounded intervals;
- the selection or formation rule;
- the relation between a finer result and its coarser object;
- the origin-typed object that carries a projected function;
- the disclosed loss and exclusions.

The requirement is not that every operation preserve an identical target object. `COMPOSE` forms a new composite. Its reference duty is to preserve traceable reference to its declared components and formation relation. `DECOMPOSE` carries the stronger same-reference burden. `PROJECT_AS` must preserve the source referent while assigning a separate bounded function.

```text
new composite target
≠ reference failure automatically

nominally unchanged target
≠ reference continuity automatically
```

#### Transformation cannot erase its own possibility conditions

A transformation exceeds the band where its result can no longer reconstruct the object, relations, or source history that made the operation possible. A decomposition that replaces the source path with an unrelated collection is not a finer reconstruction of that path. A projection that retains only a metaphorical label while exchanging the source object is not a source-carried function. A composition that cites components but no longer preserves their constitutive formation relation is not traceable merely because the names remain.

#### C6-REF-01 — citation without Reference Continuity

An analyst presents a later collection of review meetings under the existing label `P_review`. The presentation cites the original adoption record, the reconsideration promise, `N1`, and the reopening record. However, the target collection:

- begins after `K2` rather than at `K0`;
- excludes the adoption transition `E1 / T1`;
- includes unrelated meetings from a second project;
- uses thematic similarity rather than the original formation rule;
- treats the new collection as the same path rather than a new composite.

The source list is rich, but the historical referent and constitutive formation have changed.

```text
source citation: present
same label: present
same referent: not supported
formation continuity: failed
Reference Continuity: failed
```

The later collection may support a new `COMPOSE` claim under a new identity and source declaration. It does not inherit `P_review` merely through naming. TraceableLoad for the original path claim fails because the transformation no longer carries the original referent.

A conservative thematic resemblance may remain `analogy_only`. If a new composite is responsibly declared, it receives a new testable claim. The original same-reference claim routes toward `failed_transformation`; insistence that citation volume compensates for reference exchange creates Stop pressure.

### 6.11 Transformation Admissibility Test

The core Chapter-6 gate integrates the lower and upper boundaries with inherited type and context constraints.

```text
Admissible(T, X, C)
iff
PraxisPurchase(T, X, C)
and
TraceableLoad(T, X, C)
and
TypeIntegrity(T, X, C)
and
ContextualBoundedness(T, X, C)
```

This formula states required conjunction, not a numerical score, proof calculus, empirical law, or automatic classifier. `Reference Continuity`, Functional Continuity, Temporal Continuity, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, Alternatives, Source and Claim Ceilings, Stop, and Non-Capture remain applicable checks around the core gate.

A fuller operational reading is:

```text
CoreBandSatisfied
and SourceSupportAdequate
and ReferenceContinuityPreserved
and FunctionalContinuityWarranted
and CounterfactualSensitivityAssessed
and LossDeclared
and AlternativesConsidered
and ClaimCeilingDeclared
and StopConditionDefined
and AuthorityInheritanceProhibited
```

The extended list does not create additional core operations or Output Classes. It identifies the burdens that prevent a formally complete declaration from being mistaken for substantive passage.

```yaml
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

Admissibility does not transmit decision entitlement, legitimacy, or application authority.

#### Non-compensation

The gate is explicitly non-compensatory. Its conditions are not dimensions of one weighted score.

```text
high detail
cannot compensate for
no PraxisPurchase
```

```text
strong rhetoric or utility
cannot compensate for
no TraceableLoad
```

```text
source trace
cannot compensate for
invalid type jump
```

```text
formal completeness
cannot compensate for
unbounded context or claim
```

```text
earlier admissible chain link
cannot compensate for
later failed link
```

A failed load-bearing condition requires segmentation, reduction, stop, failure, or Non-Capture as appropriate. The analyst may not average the conditions into an overall positive impression.

#### C6-NCOMP-01 — purchase cannot compensate for missing load

The universal “institutional attractor” label from `C6-CEIL-02` is useful: it makes later reviewers notice recurring reopening pressure. The label therefore produces some PraxisPurchase for a bounded orientation question. Yet its universal cross-project claim remains unchanged when the reconsideration promise, `N1`, temporal order, participant structure, and target project are materially altered.

```text
PraxisPurchase: supported for bounded orientation
TraceableLoad: failed for universal attractor claim
Type Integrity: at risk if attractor becomes operator identity
Contextual Boundedness: failed for cross-project universality
```

The transformation is not admissible as declared. Utility and purchase cannot repair missing load or unbounded scope. A narrower local orientation function may be reformulated and retested. The universal claim must be reduced, failed, or stopped.

#### Claim segmentation before routing

Mixed findings must be segmented before a final Output Class is chosen. For example:

```text
claim segment A:
P_review is a traceable bounded path
→ supported

claim segment B:
P_review performs one local orientation function
→ potentially supported with bounded claim

claim segment C:
P_review is a universal attractor across projects
→ unsupported
```

One record or integrated audit may preserve all three findings, but each delimited routed claim receives one canonical class. The positive local path does not rescue the universal projection, and the failed universal projection does not erase the path.

#### C6-REDUCE-01 — overclaim with testable narrower form

The analyst claims that `P_review` governs all later review decisions. The source basis supports only a narrower proposition: in `K3`, the path orients attention toward unresolved reconsideration and reopening burden.

The strong claim exceeds Contextual Boundedness and TraceableLoad. A testable narrower claim remains.

```text
current claim:
universal governance function

required reduction:
bounded orientation function in K3
```

The correct immediate route is:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

Reduction is not passage. The narrower claim must be explicitly restated, receive its own validity scope, and be retested. Only then may `admissible_with_bounded_claim` or another class be selected.

### 6.12 The Admissibility Band

The complete Band contains three relational zones. They are outcomes of a delimited transformation assessment, not fixed locations occupied by an object, operation, granularity, or analytical level.

```text
Below the band
→ distinction without praxeological purchase

Within the band
→ discriminating and reconstructively anchored transformation

Above the band
→ abstraction, fragmentation, or projection
  without traceable load
```

The zones are non-symmetric. The lower failure concerns added distinction without changed warranted reconstruction. The upper failure concerns a target that no longer carries reconstructible source load. A transformation may also fail inside the attempted passage because Type Integrity, Reference Continuity, Contextual Boundedness, Source Ceiling, Claim Ceiling, or another load-bearing rule fails.

#### Below, within, and above

A below-band refinement may be source-supported and correctly typed yet add no further praxis finding. Where the refinement itself remains valid and no hidden defect exists, `resolution_neutral` may be the proper class. Indefinite continuation after that finding can require Stop.

A within-band transformation must show at least:

```text
changed warranted reconstruction
+ reconstructible source load
+ preserved type commitments
+ bounded transformation context
```

The within-band example is the bounded reconstruction and later orientation use of `P_review`: adoption, promised reconsideration, `N1`, and `T2` change the reopening-burden reconstruction; the same path referent and temporal formation remain visible; origin type remains `path`; the target function is bounded to one declared context.

An above-band result retains rhetorical or formal shape while losing source dependency. The universal macro-label from `C6-CEIL-02` is above the ceiling because materially different source structures leave it unchanged.

```text
below
≠ failed automatically

within
≠ universally valid

above
≠ merely abstract
```

#### Canonical Output Class routing

Chapter 6 preserves exactly ten Output Classes:

```text
admissible
admissible_with_bounded_claim
admissible_but_provisional
resolution_neutral
analogy_only
partially_admissible
claim_reduction_required
mandatory_stop
failed_transformation
non_capture
```

Their boundary roles are:

- `admissible` — all applicable load-bearing requirements pass for the declared claim and scope;
- `admissible_with_bounded_claim` — the transformation passes only under a material and explicit restriction of context, time, function, relation, or generality;
- `admissible_but_provisional` — one coherent bounded claim is supportable, but named source, calibration, or counterfactual limits remain material;
- `resolution_neutral` — a valid finer reconstruction adds no additional praxis finding and conceals no other defect;
- `analogy_only` — a bounded structural resemblance remains, but source-traceable functional projection is not established;
- `partially_admissible` — separable stages or subclaims receive different local outcomes and remain explicitly segmented;
- `claim_reduction_required` — the current claim must be weakened, restated, and retested before any positive passage;
- `mandatory_stop` — continuation under the current conditions would cross a binding relevance, traceability, type, source, claim, or anti-immunization boundary;
- `failed_transformation` — the declared operation did not establish its required target object, finer reconstruction, or contextual function;
- `non_capture` — relevant structure cannot presently be responsibly selected or integrated within the declared operation, source basis, or STRATA grammar.

No local rule word automatically selects a class:

```text
below_floor
≠ resolution_neutral automatically

insensitive
≠ failed_transformation automatically

underdetermined
≠ non_capture automatically

stop trigger
≠ mandatory_stop automatically
```

Final routing requires the whole claim pattern, alternatives, losses, boundary collisions, and a substantive selection rationale.

#### C6-STOP-01 — continuation across a failed load-bearing gate

The analyst receives the `C6-TYPE-01` finding that `P_review` cannot be retyped as a `Frame` operator. Rather than preserving the bounded function and stopping the type claim, the analyst continues to derive operator-level properties, cross-context authority, and additional projections from the failed retyping.

```text
failed Type Integrity
+ continued derivation from failed type claim
→ prohibited continuation
```

The result is:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The earlier valid path reconstruction and any independently supported bounded function remain visible. Stop blocks the impermissible continuation; it does not delete earlier findings.

#### C6-NC-01 — rival reconstructions beyond responsible capture

The available source set supports two rival mappings of later reopening pressure:

```text
candidate A:
P_review carries a bounded memory-function
because prior unresolved commitments remain retrievable

candidate B:
P_review carries a bounded frame-function
because the same commitments delimit present review relevance
```

The target context record is incomplete. It does not establish whether the later use was primarily retrospective retrieval, present delimitation, or both. Both candidates preserve the path referent, origin type, and some source load. Available sources do not responsibly discriminate between them, and a combined function would merely conceal the unresolved difference.

The result is:

```text
non_capture
```

The record must preserve both candidates, the missing target-context evidence, the limiting condition, and a re-entry condition. Non-Capture does not validate either strong function and does not imply that the object is inherently uncapturable.

### 6.13 No Universal STRATA Scale

The Admissibility Band is relational. It does not define a universal metric of analytical size, height, fineness, abstraction, complexity, importance, or validity.

There is no universal:

- smallest praxeologically relevant element;
- finest admissible granularity;
- largest legitimate composite;
- maximum number of relative levels;
- fixed repetition threshold for attractor-function;
- fixed duration threshold for trajectory;
- fixed similarity threshold for projection;
- weighted admissibility score whose dimensions compensate for one another.

```text
relational boundary
≠ universal scale value
```

Admissibility depends on the declared frame, source object, source basis, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, transformation context, claim scope, and tested claim. A distinction neutral for one question may be decisive for another. A composite traceable in one bounded source family may become untraceable when generalized. A projection valid in one context may fail in another.

#### Local comparison remains possible

No Universal STRATA Scale does not prohibit bounded ordinal or comparative judgments. An analyst may state that one reconstruction is finer than another, one claim is broader, one composite includes more source objects, or one projection has a narrower validity scope. Such comparisons remain local to declared coordinates and do not become universal admissibility thresholds.

```text
local comparison
≠ universal metric
```

The Planck analogy may serve as a heuristic reminder that analysis has practical lower limits. It does not establish a literal minimum unit of praxis, a fixed physical analogy, or an ontological constituent.

#### C6-SCALE-01 — universal numeric scale proposal

A proposed system assigns every transformation four scores from zero to one:

```text
PraxisPurchase: 0.9
TraceableLoad: 0.3
TypeIntegrity: 0.8
ContextualBoundedness: 0.7
```

It then calculates a weighted mean and declares any result above `0.65` admissible. Under this method, high purchase and type scores compensate for failed source load.

The proposal violates the core conjunction and invents a universal threshold unsupported by the corpus.

```text
weighted score
+ compensatory threshold
≠ STRATA Admissibility Band
```

The scale claim receives:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

Local descriptive indicators may still be recorded where explicitly bounded, but they cannot replace rule-level findings, substantive adjudication, or canonical Output Class routing.

#### WP3 boundary and handoff

Sections 6.9–6.13 establish:

- Type Integrity as a non-compensable inherited condition;
- Reference Continuity as preservation of the actual referent rather than nominal or citation continuity;
- the unified conjunctive admissibility test;
- the extended operational burden without proof-calculus inflation;
- claim segmentation and non-compensation;
- below-, within-, and above-band representation;
- the complete ten-class routing boundary;
- Claim Reduction as reformulation plus retest rather than passage;
- Mandatory Stop and Non-Capture as distinct outcomes;
- No Universal STRATA Scale and the permissibility of bounded local comparison;
- seven controlled cases: `C6-TYPE-01`, `C6-REF-01`, `C6-NCOMP-01`, `C6-REDUCE-01`, `C6-STOP-01`, `C6-NC-01`, and `C6-SCALE-01`.

At the WP3 checkpoint, the following remained incomplete:

- the integrated Chapter-6 redundancy and completion audit;
- final Reference Kernel synchronization across all current routes;
- the formal prose-to-model handoff;
- registration of the sixteen later case obligations;
- schema, package, fingerprint, and release-roundtrip verification;
- the Chapter 6 provisional-lock decision.

The WP3 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 6.9–6.13. Their historical provisionality attached to the then-uncompleted integrated audit, model handoff, case registration, package verification, and lock decision.

#### Chapter 6 integrated completion and provisional lock

Sections 6.1–6.13 now form one integrated Admissibility Band architecture. The completed chapter fixes the relational lower and upper boundaries, `PraxisPurchase`, `TraceableLoad`, Counterfactual Sensitivity, inherited Type Integrity and Reference Continuity burdens, the conjunctive non-compensatory test, claim segmentation, ten-class routing boundaries, Stop and Non-Capture separation, and No Universal STRATA Scale.

```text
identified transformation occurrence
+ delimited tested claim
+ declared context and source basis
+ PraxisPurchase
+ TraceableLoad
+ Type Integrity
+ Contextual Boundedness
+ applicable continuity, loss, alternatives,
  counterfactual, source, claim, Stop,
  Non-Capture, and authority findings
→ auditable admissibility judgment
```

The integrated completion test confirms:

- operational availability does not establish admissibility;
- the Relevance Floor tests changed warranted reconstruction rather than detail volume, salience, usefulness, present actionability, or authority;
- valid `resolution_neutral` refinement remains distinct from unsupported detail, object substitution, and indefinite proliferation;
- the Traceability Ceiling tests reconstructible source-result dependency rather than citation presence, label fit, exhaustive reproduction, or rhetorical scope;
- source citation, structural mapping, and source-result dependency remain non-equivalent;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity tests declared source dependence without proving empirical causality, necessity, or prediction;
- `PraxisPurchase`, `TraceableLoad`, Type Integrity, and Contextual Boundedness are conjunctive and non-compensatory;
- Reference, Functional, and Temporal Continuity remain independently applicable and cannot be replaced by the four-term compact gate;
- mixed claims are segmented before routing, and Claim Reduction requires reformulation and retest rather than automatic passage;
- local findings such as `below_floor`, `sensitive`, `insensitive`, `underdetermined`, or `stop trigger` are not Output Classes;
- the ten canonical Output Classes remain closed and require substantive, claim-relative selection;
- Mandatory Stop blocks impermissible continuation without deleting prior valid findings;
- Non-Capture preserves unresolved rival claims, limiting conditions, and re-entry requirements without validating a stronger claim;
- no universal score, weighted average, fixed threshold, granularity minimum, composite maximum, level count, recurrence threshold, duration threshold, or projection-similarity threshold replaces relational judgment;
- formal completeness, schema validity, source volume, analytical elegance, or local utility cannot compensate for a failed load-bearing condition;
- admissibility does not establish empirical truth, causal truth, semantic or normative validity, person judgment, legitimacy, recommendation, intervention warrant, complete capture, or authority.

The integrated Chapter 6 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 6 is provisionally locked. The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves legitimate later revision pressure from Chapter 7 Shared Transformation Record integration, Chapter 8 Foundational Non-Equivalences, the detailed operation and Part procedures, substantive cases and countercases, Chapters 44–53 LIMITS, appendices, corpus audit, model finalization, and later source-supported boundary findings. Reopening requires a documented contradiction, failed case burden, ownership conflict, schema pressure, audit failure, or later source-supported boundary finding.

<a id="chapter-7-shared-transformation-record"></a>

## 7. Shared Transformation Record

A STRATA transformation is an analytical occurrence. A **Shared Transformation Record** is the inspectable declaration through which that occurrence, its tested claim, its source basis, its target, its limits, and its result can be reviewed. The record is therefore neither the transformation itself nor a substitute for the substantive reconstruction that supports it.

```text
transformation
≠ transformation record
```

```text
record completeness
≠ transformation admissibility
```

The record has one primary purpose: to make heterogeneous transformation claims comparable without flattening their semantics. `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` require different source–target relations, but every occurrence must still disclose what was transformed, which operation was claimed, what target was produced or function was assigned, what was preserved or lost, which alternatives remained, which limits governed the claim, and how the local result was routed.

The common conceptual envelope is:

```yaml
source:
operation:
target:
admissibility:
loss:
alternatives:
governance:
status_and_result:
```

The first three families are defined in Sections 7.2–7.4. Sections 7.5–7.10 later define the remaining duties, separated status axes, chains, extensions, and integrated use. Record identity, metadata, claim identity, relations, chain declarations, extensions, result payloads, and formal diagnostics may accompany this envelope as serialization structures. They do not create additional STRATA operations, primitives, object classes, or sources of authority.

```text
shared envelope
≠ flattened operation semantics
```

```text
serialization companion
≠ new canonical primitive
```

### 7.1 Purpose of the Record

The Shared Transformation Record serves five connected purposes.

First, it makes the **tested claim** explicit. A record does not merely state that a transformation happened. It identifies the bounded proposition being assessed. The same operation occurrence may support one narrow claim while failing a broader one, so claim identity and claim scope must remain visible.

Second, it preserves **source–operation–target traceability**. The record must permit a reviewer to reconstruct how the declared source object or source set is related to the operation occurrence and how that occurrence is related to its target object or target function.

Third, it preserves **loss, alternatives, and limits**. An inspectable record does not present one transformation as though no rival reconstruction, no excluded material, no uncertainty, and no claim ceiling existed.

Fourth, it supports **local and integrated audit**. A later operation-specific chapter, case, chain review, or Integrated STRATA Audit can read the same common duties without replacing the local occurrence result.

Fifth, it supports **formal conformance without formal authority**. A schema may verify that required families are present, values have allowed types, an occurrence has one operation kind, or a routed record has the required result carrier. It cannot determine whether a source trace is adequate, whether the transformation is true, whether causality has been established, or whether the interpretation is semantically or normatively justified.

```text
formal validation
≠ substantive validation
≠ authority
```

#### Record boundary

The record is an artifact about a transformation claim. It is not a second occurrence and does not alter the historical object it describes. Revision of a record is likewise not retroactive revision of the source event, path, composite, or earlier claim history.

```text
revised record
≠ revised past
```

A new source basis, target context, claim scope, operation occurrence, or successor claim normally requires a new record or an explicit revision relation. It may not silently overwrite a failed, reduced, stopped, superseded, or non-captured predecessor.

```text
new record or revision
= new inspectable claim state
≠ erasure of prior result
```

#### Declaration completeness and epistemic completeness

A record is **declaration-complete** when every applicable common duty is explicitly addressed, including known gaps, empty categories, and routing state. It is **epistemically complete** only to the extent that the available sources support all distinctions required by the tested claim.

```text
declaration completeness
≠ epistemic completeness
```

A declaration-complete record may therefore state that a source relation is underdetermined, that a target context is insufficiently specified, or that rival transformations cannot yet be resolved. Honest incompleteness is inspectable. Invented precision is not.

```text
declaration-complete
+ epistemically incomplete
→ possible and reviewable
```

Analyst omission remains different. A known gap that is hidden, an applicable duty that is silently absent, or an extension field used to avoid the common envelope is not justified epistemic incompleteness.

#### Compact canonical envelope and existing schema

The Structure blueprint uses compact labels such as `source.object`, `source.operator_typing`, `target.object`, and `target.contextual_function`. The current formal schema uses more explicit paths such as `/source/reference_object`, `/source/object_typing`, and `/target/reference_object`. Chapter 7 controls the semantic duty; the schema supplies one technical carrier.

```text
canonical prose duty
↔ controlled schema path
```

The mapping is not one-way authority inheritance:

```text
existing machine field
↛ automatic canonical concept
```

Nor may compact prose ignore a genuinely necessary trace duty merely because it is not visible in the abbreviated blueprint. The appropriate relation is a controlled two-way comparison: prose specifies why a duty exists; the schema demonstrates how it is serialized; discrepancies create explicit clarification or revision pressure.

#### C7-REC-01 — compact shared record candidate

The following compact record declares a bounded `PROJECT_AS` occurrence for the already composed review path `P_review`:

```yaml
record_id: C7-REC-01
record_scope: operation_occurrence
routing_state: routed
claim:
  statement: >
    In K3, P_review performs a bounded orientation function
    for reviewing unresolved reconsideration burden.
  claim_scope: local to K3 and the declared review purpose
source:
  reference_object: P_review
  object_typing: path
  frame: review process
  granularity: bounded path composite
  relative_level: source-relative path level
  temporal_scope: K0 through K2 as carried into K3
  source_scope: selected review materials supporting P_review
  source_basis:
    - K0, E1/T1, K1, N1/T2, K2
  constitutive_source_trace:
    - adoption and promised reconsideration
    - bounded non-realization N1
    - altered reopening conditions through T2
  known_gaps: []
  source_ceiling: no claim beyond the declared source family
operation:
  occurrence_id: C7-REC-01-PROJ
  kind: PROJECT_AS
  justification: the path orders source features relevant to the K3 review
  expected_praxeological_difference: changes how reopening burden is reconstructed
  selection_rule: retain only source features constitutive of that bounded function
  transformation_context: K3 process-review orientation
  details: origin type remains path
target:
  reference_object: P_review
  object_typing: path
  contextual_function: bounded orientation function
  frame: K3 process review
  granularity: path used as one bounded contextual source
  relative_level: target-context function level
  temporal_scope: K3 review occurrence
  validity_scope: K3 and the stated analytical purpose only
  contextual_function_origin_occurrence_ref: C7-REC-01-PROJ
admissibility:
  declared_for_later_sections: true
loss:
  declared_for_later_sections: true
alternatives:
  declared_for_later_sections: true
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
status_and_result:
  routed_output: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The compact example is illustrative rather than a replacement for the formal schema. Its strength comes from the declared source–operation–target relation and bounded claim, not from YAML syntax. Sections 7.5–7.10 later define the abbreviated family entries in full.

#### C7-REC-02 — complete syntax, insufficient trace

A second record names the same source and target but supplies only:

```yaml
source_basis:
  - several review documents
constitutive_source_trace:
  - the documents are relevant
operation:
  kind: PROJECT_AS
target:
  contextual_function: institutional memory
```

Every required key could be populated with similarly generic text. The record would remain substantively insufficient because it does not identify which source features carry the function, how the function changes under material source change, or why the target claim remains bounded.

```text
syntactic completion
+ generic source language
≠ TraceableLoad
```

The stronger claim may later require `claim_reduction_required` or `failed_transformation`. Syntax cannot rescue it.

### 7.2 Source Declaration

The Source Declaration identifies the object or source set from which the transformation claim begins and the evidential ceiling under which it is tested. It must permit a reviewer to determine not merely what materials were consulted, but what source structure is asserted to carry the transformation.

A controlled source family includes:

```yaml
source:
  reference_object:
  object_typing:
  frame:
  granularity:
  relative_level:
  temporal_scope:
  source_scope:
  source_basis:
  constitutive_source_trace:
  known_gaps:
  source_ceiling:
```

#### Reference object and object typing

`reference_object` identifies the source-side referent. `object_typing` states the analytical type under which it enters the occurrence. These duties are related but non-equivalent.

```text
source reference identity
≠ source object typing
```

For `DECOMPOSE`, the reference object must remain the same compressed occurrence or composite being reconstructed. For `COMPOSE`, the source declaration may identify several typed objects or a sequential source family. For `PROJECT_AS`, the origin-typed source object remains visible while the target function is declared separately.

The record does not create the object type. It records a typing justified under Chapters 1–5.

#### Coordinates and scopes

Frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, and source scope make the source claim comparable and bounded. They do not determine operation identity.

```text
recorded coordinate
≠ operation kind
```

The Source Declaration must not infer `DECOMPOSE` merely from finer granularity, `COMPOSE` from a wider relative level, or `PROJECT_AS` from a changed frame. The operation remains separately declared and justified.

#### Source basis and Constitutive Source Trace

`source_basis` identifies the materials or observations available to the claim. `constitutive_source_trace` identifies the load-bearing features and relations that the result depends on.

```text
source basis
≠ proof
```

```text
Constitutive Source Trace
≠ source list
```

A record that lists ten documents but cannot map any of them to the formation relation, finer reconstruction, or contextual function has not established TraceableLoad. Conversely, a bounded record may rely on a small source family if its constitutive mapping and ceiling are explicit.

#### Known gaps and Source Ceiling

Known gaps must be declared where relevant. A gap is not an invitation to infer the most convenient event, non-event, transition, intention, or source relation.

```text
known gap
≠ non-event
≠ permission to infer
```

The Source Ceiling states the strongest source-supported limit. It prevents a record from using machine-required specificity to exceed the available material.

#### C7-SRC-01 — underdetermined source relation

Suppose the available materials establish `K0`, adoption through `E1/T1`, and a later `K2`, but the interval in which reconsideration may have occurred is absent. The record may responsibly state:

```yaml
known_gaps:
  - reconsideration interval between K1 and K2 is not documented
constitutive_source_trace:
  - adoption is supported
  - later reopening is supported
  - whether promised reconsideration occurred is underdetermined
source_ceiling:
  - no non-event claim about reconsideration
  - no causal claim linking absence to later decisions
```

It may not fill the gap with `N1` merely because the later path would become more coherent.

```text
source-limited underdetermination
≠ analyst failure
```

At the declaration stage, the record may remain formal-diagnostic or preserve a narrower claim. The correct response is explicit limitation, not invented precision.

### 7.3 Operation Declaration

The Operation Declaration identifies the single transformation occurrence being tested and states why the declared source–target relation has the signature of `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS`.

A controlled operation family includes:

```yaml
operation:
  occurrence_id:
  kind:
  justification:
  expected_praxeological_difference:
  selection_rule:
  transformation_context:
  details:
```

#### One occurrence, one kind

Every occurrence has exactly one operation identity.

```text
one operation occurrence
→ exactly one operation kind
```

A chain is represented through multiple linked occurrence records or explicit occurrence segments. The operation field does not admit compound values.

```text
operation chain
≠ multi-kind occurrence
```

#### Justification and expected difference

`justification` explains why the source–target relation matches the declared operation signature. `expected_praxeological_difference` states what changed warranted reconstruction the occurrence is intended to produce or test.

```text
operation justification
≠ automatic warrant
```

```text
expected praxeological difference
≠ demonstrated PraxisPurchase
```

Both remain claims subject to later adjudication. A well-written rationale may still fail the Admissibility Band.

#### Selection rule and transformation context

The selection rule is operation-sensitive. For `COMPOSE`, it identifies which source structures are selected and how formation occurs. For `DECOMPOSE`, it identifies which compressed relations are reopened and how same-object reference is preserved. For `PROJECT_AS`, it identifies the source features selected as carrying the bounded target function.

The transformation context states the full source–operation–target purpose and validity relation. It is not merely the target context, frame, or location.

```text
selection rule
≠ transformation context
```

The `details` carrier may serialize operation-specific information. It may not replace the common operation duties or invent new operation semantics.

#### C7-OP-01 — valid occurrence identity candidate

A record declares:

```yaml
occurrence_id: C7-OP-01-CMP
kind: COMPOSE
justification: >
  Selected configurations and transitions are ordered by supported
  traversal and form the bounded review path P_review.
expected_praxeological_difference: >
  The composite changes the reconstruction from isolated entries
  to a path with accumulated reopening burden.
selection_rule: >
  Include K0, E1/T1, K1, N1/T2, and K2 only where the source
  supports their constitutive relation to the review path.
transformation_context: bounded reconstruction of review-path formation
```

The occurrence has one kind and a recognizable `COMPOSE` signature. The record makes the claim inspectable; it does not itself prove the formation relation.

#### C7-OP-02 — collapsed dual operation

A second record declares:

```yaml
occurrence_id: C7-OP-02-MIXED
kind: DECOMPOSE + PROJECT_AS
justification: >
  The path is opened into finer transitions and thereby becomes
  an institutional memory function.
```

This declaration collapses two targets and two independent burdens:

1. a finer reconstruction of the same path;
2. a bounded contextual function performed by that path.

```text
finer target reconstruction
≠ contextual target function
```

The record cannot proceed as one occurrence. The result is:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

Re-entry requires at least two separately identified occurrence records with an explicit handoff.

### 7.4 Target Declaration

The Target Declaration identifies what the operation occurrence claims to produce or assign. It must distinguish a target reference object, target typing, and contextual function rather than treating them as interchangeable labels.

A controlled target family includes:

```yaml
target:
  reference_object:
  object_typing:
  contextual_function:
  frame:
  granularity:
  relative_level:
  temporal_scope:
  validity_scope:
  contextual_function_origin_occurrence_ref:
```

#### Target object and target function

`COMPOSE` normally produces a new composite analytical object. `DECOMPOSE` produces a finer reconstruction of the same reference object. `PROJECT_AS` assigns a bounded contextual function while preserving the source reference and origin type.

```text
target object
≠ target function
```

A field may be explicitly inapplicable or empty only where the operation signature warrants that declaration. Empty declaration remains different from omission.

```text
explicitly empty or inapplicable target field
≠ silently omitted duty
```

#### Target typing

Target typing must follow the operation signature. A `COMPOSE` target may legitimately have a composite type not held by any individual source component. A `DECOMPOSE` target must not silently become a competing object if the operation claim is same-reference reconstruction. A `PROJECT_AS` target retains the source object typing and records the function separately.

```text
PROJECT_AS target function
≠ origin-type replacement
```

#### Target coordinates and Validity Scope

Frame, granularity, relative level, and temporal scope locate the target claim. `validity_scope` states where and for what purpose the target claim may be used.

```text
valid target here
≠ valid elsewhere
≠ valid indefinitely
```

Coordinates do not grant authority. A bounded function in one review meeting does not become a universal governance rule.

#### Contextual-function origin reference

Where a contextual function is declared, the record should preserve the occurrence that established it. `contextual_function_origin_occurrence_ref` prevents a later record from treating the function as an intrinsic, timeless property of the source object.

```text
function origin reference
→ traceable projection occurrence
≠ intrinsic source essence
```

#### Source–target comparison table

| Operation | Source declaration | Target declaration | Central protection |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `COMPOSE` | many or sequential typed source structures | new composite analytical object | composite ≠ source set or chronology |
| `DECOMPOSE` | compressed occurrence or composite | finer reconstruction of the same reference object | detail ≠ new object automatically |
| `PROJECT_AS` | origin-typed source object | same reference and origin typing plus bounded function | function ≠ type replacement |

#### WP1 boundary and handoff

Sections 7.1–7.4 establish:

- transformation versus transformation record;
- record completeness versus transformation admissibility;
- declaration completeness versus epistemic completeness;
- the eight-family conceptual envelope and serialization-companion boundary;
- the controlled prose-to-schema relation;
- Source, Operation, and Target family purposes;
- Source Basis versus Constitutive Source Trace;
- explicit known gaps and Source Ceiling;
- one operation kind per occurrence;
- justification and expected difference as testable rather than self-validating declarations;
- target object, target typing, and contextual function separation;
- five controlled cases: `C7-REC-01`, `C7-REC-02`, `C7-SRC-01`, `C7-OP-01`, and `C7-OP-02`.

At the WP1 checkpoint (retained as a historical handoff), the following remained incomplete:

- Admissibility Declaration;
- the five-part Loss Declaration;
- Alternative Transformations;
- Governance Declaration;
- separated status and result axes;
- routed versus formal-diagnostic use;
- operation-chain representation;
- local extensions and integrated use;
- integrated Chapter-7 audit, case registration, model handoff, schema comparison, package verification, and provisional lock.

The WP1 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 7.1–7.4. Their provisionality attaches to the still-open record families, status architecture, chain and extension rules, formal-model comparison, integrated audit, and lock decision.

### 7.5 Admissibility Declaration

The Admissibility Declaration records how the tested claim was examined under the rules already defined in Chapters 5 and 6. It does not redefine those rules, infer their findings from completed fields, or turn a record into an adjudicating tribunal.

```text
recorded rule finding
≠ automated adjudication
```

```text
complete admissibility fields
≠ admissible transformation
```

The declaration must expose enough of the audit path that a reviewer can determine which rule was applied to which delimited claim, what source relation carried the finding, which limitations remained, and how the finding contributed to later routing. A compact conceptual burden includes:

```yaml
admissibility:
  affected_praxis_dimensions:
  relevance_floor_result:
  constitutive_source_trace:
  traceable_load_result:
  counterfactual_sensitivity:
  traceability_ceiling_result:
  reference_continuity:
  type_integrity:
  origin_reference_preserved:
  origin_type_preserved:
  functional_continuity:
  temporal_continuity:
  target_function_bounded:
  contextual_boundedness:
  non_compensation_confirmation:
  stop_assessment:
  non_capture_assessment:
  audit_to_routing_basis:
```

This list states conceptual duties, not a second schema. The current formal carrier distributes them across `/admissibility/audit_stages`, `/admissibility/rule_assessments`, `/admissibility/non_compensation_confirmation`, `/admissibility/audit_to_routing_basis`, `/admissibility/anti_immunization_assessment`, `/admissibility/stop_assessment`, and `/admissibility/non_capture_assessment`. Source Trace remains grounded in the Source Declaration; origin type and target function remain visible in Source and Target; Claim Ceiling remains owned by the Claim Declaration.

```text
conceptual duty
↔ existing controlled carrier
≠ duplicate field family
```

#### Applicability and local findings

Every potentially governing rule must be either assessed or marked not applicable with a reason tied to the operation signature and tested claim. Silence is not a negative finding and not an acceptable not-applicable declaration.

```text
not applicable with reason
≠ omitted assessment
```

Local findings such as `below_floor`, `sensitive`, `partially sensitive`, `underdetermined`, failed Type Integrity, or a Stop trigger remain findings within the audit path. They are not automatically canonical Output Classes.

```text
local rule finding
≠ canonical Output Class
```

The compact Structure blueprint includes `admissibility.result`. In the shared record this is a handoff duty: the audit must explain the basis on which the delimited claim is later routed. It must not collapse the separated status and result architecture reserved for Section 7.9.

```text
audit-to-routing basis
≠ routing shortcut
```

#### Non-compensation in the record

The record must affirm that no strong dimension was used to compensate for a failed load-bearing requirement. A useful target function cannot rescue absent TraceableLoad; source detail cannot rescue no PraxisPurchase; formal completeness cannot rescue Type Integrity or Contextual Boundedness.

```text
recorded strengths
```
