```text
cannot compensate for
failed governing duty
```

For `C7-REC-01`, the bounded `PROJECT_AS` candidate can record positive local findings only because its source object, origin type, target function, target context, source trace, loss, and claim ceiling remain visible. The YAML form does not produce those findings. Review of the declared source–operation–target relation does.

#### Uncertainty and re-entry

An Admissibility Declaration may remain provisional, underdetermined, or formal-diagnostic where available sources cannot support a stronger finding. It must preserve the limiting condition and any permitted re-entry requirement rather than manufacturing a completed route.

```text
recorded uncertainty
≠ analytical immunity
≠ automatic non_capture
```

The exact separation of support status, capture statement, routing state, and canonical Output Class remains assigned to Section 7.9.

### 7.6 Loss Declaration

Every transformation occurrence must declare loss through the same closed five-category structure:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

The categories are mandatory because every STRATA operation redistributes visibility or information, even when no item is currently asserted in one category. Operation-specific templates may elaborate entries but may not remove, rename, merge, or replace the five categories.

```text
operation-specific elaboration
≠ loss-family override
```

#### Preserved

`preserved` records source identities, relations, temporal order, type information, contextual limits, or claim burdens that remain available in the target declaration. Preservation is always claim- and scope-relative.

```text
preserved within declared scope
≠ preserved universally
≠ lossless transformation
```

#### Compressed

`compressed` records distinctions that remain represented but at reduced resolution, specificity, or visibility. Compression does not mean disappearance. The record must state what representational burden remains and what finer distinctions are no longer separately available in the current target.

```text
compressed
≠ excluded
```

#### Excluded

`excluded` records known material deliberately left outside the transformation or tested claim. Exclusion requires a reason, especially where the excluded material could alter selection, formation, continuity, function, or scope.

```text
excluded by declared rule
≠ absent from sources
```

#### Uncertain

`uncertain` records loss whose extent, location, or effect cannot be established from the available source basis. Uncertainty must remain visible and revisable.

```text
uncertain loss
≠ irrecoverable loss
```

#### Irrecoverable

`irrecoverable` is reserved for loss that cannot be reconstructed within the declared source basis and available record lineage. It is a bounded analytical finding, not a metaphysical claim that recovery is impossible under every future source condition.

```text
irrecoverable in declared record context
≠ impossible in principle
```

#### Empty categories and negative declarations

An empty category is an explicit statement that no item is currently asserted there within the tested scope. It is not an omission and does not prove universal absence.

```text
empty declared category
≠ omitted category
≠ proof of no loss
```

Each entry should identify the affected object or relation, the reason for its classification, its relevance to the tested claim, and where possible the source or operation occurrence that supports the classification.

#### C7-LOSS-01 — complete five-part disclosure

A bounded `COMPOSE` record for `P_review` can declare:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
    - selected configurations K0, K1, and K2
    - order of E1/T1 and N1/T2
    - path reference and bounded review purpose
  compressed:
    - wording differences among review documents
    - fine-grained timing within each review interval
  excluded:
    - unrelated staffing and budget discussions
  uncertain:
    - undocumented informal exchanges during the reconsideration interval
  irrecoverable: []
```

This declaration does not claim losslessness. The explicit empty `irrecoverable` category states only that no irrecoverable item is currently asserted under the declared source basis. The uncertain interval may not be recoded as an irrecoverable loss or as a Non-Event without additional support.

The local result is that the loss architecture is declaration-complete and reviewable. The substantive transformation still depends on the remaining Admissibility, Alternatives, Governance, and status/result duties.

### 7.7 Alternative Transformations

The Alternatives Declaration preserves serious rival analytical routes and the possibility that the tested source material should not be transformed under the proposed claim.

```yaml
alternatives:
  rival_compositions:
  rival_decompositions:
  rival_projections:
  no_transformation:
  non_translation:
  unresolved:
```

The `no_transformation` carrier makes explicit a duty already required by the Chapter 7 contract and current schema. Its presence does not create a fourth operation. It records the possibility that none of the three operations is warranted for the tested claim.

```text
no transformation warranted
≠ fourth operation
```

#### Rival transformations

A rival transformation proposes a different operation occurrence, selection rule, reference relation, target, or function over the same or related source family. A rival must be stated strongly enough to be tested, not merely named as a vague possibility.

Two rivals may require separate sibling records. They must not be forced into one record when doing so would collapse operation identity, reference object, target claim, or loss profile.

```text
rival records
≠ forced integrated record
```

#### No transformation

`no_transformation` records the possibility that the existing source object should remain untransformed for the tested analytical purpose. It may be preferable where the proposed movement adds no PraxisPurchase, exceeds Source Ceiling, or would obscure a better direct description.

#### Non-translation

`non_translation` records that the proposed analytical movement cannot responsibly be represented as `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS` under the tested claim. It is not simply another rival operation.

```text
rival transformation
≠ non-translation
```

Non-translation may arise where the proposed target lacks a traceable source–target relation, where a label substitutes for a function, or where the claim requires a semantic, normative, causal, or domain-specific judgment outside STRATA's structural authority.

#### Unresolved alternatives

`unresolved` preserves alternatives that are identifiable but cannot be discriminated with available sources. The declaration must state what evidence or distinction would permit re-entry.

```text
unresolved rival
≠ weak rival silently rejected
```

If no additional alternative is asserted, the record must provide a reasoned negative finding. Empty arrays alone do not establish exhaustive search.

```text
no alternative identified with rationale
≠ no alternative exists
```

#### C7-ALT-01 — sibling records for rival transformations

The additional responsibility relations around `P_review` support two plausible candidates:

```text
candidate A
DECOMPOSE P_review
→ finer responsibility reconstruction of the same path
```

```text
candidate B
COMPOSE responsibility-residue structures
→ new responsibility-residue path
```

Candidate A retains the original reference object; Candidate B declares a new selection and formation thesis. They require sibling occurrence records with separate sources, targets, losses, and results. A convenience record labelled `DECOMPOSE/COMPOSE` would violate occurrence identity and conceal the exact issue under dispute.

Where the available sources do not decide same-object continuity versus new formation, both sibling records may remain unresolved. Their separation is itself a valid record duty; it is not a decision in favor of either candidate.

#### C7-ALT-02 — rival transformation versus non-translation

A reviewer proposes that `P_review` should instead be recorded as a universal “institutional memory” projection. If the proposal supplies a bounded target context, source-carried function, and material source dependency, it is a rival `PROJECT_AS` claim and receives its own record.

If the phrase supplies only a suggestive label and no responsible STRATA source–target relation, it is not a rival projection. It belongs under `non_translation` or, where limited similarity remains useful, may later support an `analogy_only` route for a separately delimited claim.

```text
named alternative
≠ rival operation automatically
```

The record must therefore separate the candidate operation from the possibility that no STRATA translation is warranted.

### 7.8 Governance Declaration

Governance records the limits under which a transformation claim may be reviewed, routed, and reused. It does not grant empirical, causal, semantic, normative, organizational, legal, political, diagnostic, or application authority.

```text
governance declaration
≠ authority grant
```

The compact conceptual family is:

```yaml
governance:
  claim_ceiling:
  authority_ceiling:
  stop_condition:
  non_capture_status:
  prohibited_inferences:
  external_warrant:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

The duties are distributed across the existing record carrier. Claim Ceiling remains at `/claim/claim_ceiling`; Stop and Non-Capture assessments remain under `/admissibility/*`; authority ceiling, prohibited-inference acknowledgements, external-warrant pointers, and authority-inheritance controls remain under `/governance/*`.

```text
distributed carrier
≠ fragmented responsibility
```

#### Claim Ceiling and Authority Ceiling

Claim Ceiling limits what the transformation record itself may assert from its sources and operation. Authority Ceiling limits what may be done with the record in an application or decision context.

```text
claim ceiling
≠ authority ceiling
```

A record may support a bounded structural reconstruction while lacking authority to prescribe an action, classify a person, determine legitimacy, impose a sanction, or settle a legal or political question.

#### Stop and Non-Capture carriers

The Governance family must point visibly to any Stop Condition or Non-Capture status affecting continuation or reuse. It may not convert Stop into failure erasure or Non-Capture into a protective shield for a weak claim.

```text
Stop recorded
≠ prior valid result erased
```

```text
Non-Capture recorded
≠ stronger claim protected
```

The exact status and Output Class mapping remains assigned to Section 7.9.

#### External warrant

An external warrant may identify a separate authorization, policy, legal rule, empirical finding, or domain-specific procedure. The record may point to that warrant but may not inherit or manufacture it.

```text
external warrant pointer
≠ inherited authority
```

```text
record routing permission
≠ application authority
```

#### Prohibited inferences

A governance-complete record acknowledges the inferences the transformation cannot support. These may include causal attribution, normative evaluation, person classification, universalization, origin-type replacement, or authority transfer. The exact list is claim-relative and cannot be replaced by generic boilerplate where a specific risk is visible.

#### C7-GOV-01 — schema validity used as decision authority

Assume a `PROJECT_AS` record is schema-valid, includes all required fields, and routes a bounded local orientation claim. A reviewer then states:

```text
The record is formally valid;
therefore the projected function authorizes
all later review decisions.
```

The inference violates the Claim Ceiling, Authority Ceiling, Contextual Boundedness, and the explicit governance rule:

```yaml
authority_inheritance: prohibited
```

The authority claim receives:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop attaches to the prohibited continuation and authority inference. It does not retroactively erase a separately supported bounded analytical function.

#### WP2 boundary and handoff

Sections 7.5–7.8 establish:

- Admissibility as recorded audit disclosure rather than automated adjudication;
- applicability, local findings, non-compensation, uncertainty, and audit-to-routing basis;
- the closed five-part Loss Declaration and explicit empty-category rule;
- distinctions among rival transformations, no transformation, non-translation, and unresolved alternatives;
- sibling-record treatment for materially different candidates;
- Governance as claim and authority limitation rather than authority creation;
- distributed mapping to existing Claim, Admissibility, and Governance carriers;
- four controlled cases: `C7-LOSS-01`, `C7-ALT-01`, `C7-ALT-02`, and `C7-GOV-01`.

At the WP2 checkpoint (retained as a historical handoff), the following remained incomplete:

- separated support, resolution, disposition, capture, routing, and canonical-result axes;
- routed versus formal-diagnostic records;
- chain-level representation and preservation of local results;
- declaration completeness, epistemic incompleteness, and claim-relative Non-Capture in integrated use;
- the local extension rule;
- completion of the positive and negative compact record pair;
- integrated Chapter-7 audit, case registration, model handoff, schema comparison, package verification, and provisional lock.

The WP2 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 7.5–7.8. Their provisionality attaches to the still-open status architecture, chain and extension rules, integrated record use, formal-model comparison, audit, and lock decision.

### 7.9 Separated Record Status and Result Axes

The Shared Transformation Record does not use one mixed `record_status`. A single status label would collapse evidential support, resolution behavior, claim history, capture limits, routing readiness, and the canonical Output Class into one opaque judgment. Those questions are related, but they are not equivalent and may legitimately receive different findings.

```text
support status
≠ resolution-test result
≠ claim disposition
≠ capture statement
≠ routing state
≠ canonical Output Class
```

The conceptual status-and-result burden contains at least six axes:

```yaml
status_and_result:
  support_status:
  resolution_test_result:
  claim_disposition:
  capture_statement:
  routing_state:
  canonical_output_class:
```

The current formal carrier distributes these duties across `/routing_state`, `/result/status_declaration`, `/result/routing`, `/result/class_payload`, and `/formal_diagnostic`. This distribution is a serialization choice. It does not alter the canonical requirement that the axes remain separately inspectable.

#### Support status

`support_status` records the present relation between the tested claim and its declared source basis:

```text
supported
provisional
contested
underdetermined
unsupported
```

Support status is claim-relative and time-indexed. `provisional` means that the current support remains bounded by identified uncertainty, incomplete source coverage, or a declared reopening condition. It does not itself select `admissible_but_provisional`.

```text
provisional support
≠ admissible_but_provisional
```

A claim may have provisional support yet route to `admissible_with_bounded_claim` where the uncertainty does not defeat the constitutive local claim and the boundedness is the governing result. Conversely, a claim may be strongly supported at the source level but still fail because of Type Integrity, Contextual Boundedness, authority transfer, or another non-compensable duty.

#### Resolution-test result

`resolution_test_result` records what the tested change in analytical resolution did for the delimited claim:

```text
resolution_gain
resolution_neutral
resolution_drift
resolution_escape
null when the resolution test is not applicable
```

This axis is not a generic quality score. `resolution_neutral` on the test axis does not automatically select the canonical Output Class `resolution_neutral`; the latter still requires a valid same-object comparison, adequate support, no hidden admissibility defect, and substantive routing. `resolution_drift` and `resolution_escape` remain diagnostic findings that may contribute to reduction, failure, Stop, or another route depending on the claim.

```text
resolution-test label
≠ automatic Output Class
```

#### Claim disposition

`claim_disposition` records what happened to the currently tested claim as a claim:

```text
maintained
withdrawn
failed
superseded_without_erasure
```

Disposition preserves history. A claim that is replaced by a narrower or revised successor is not erased from the record lineage.

```text
superseded
≠ erased
```

A failed claim disposition does not automatically mean that the transformation occurrence itself receives `failed_transformation`. The failed claim may be one segment of a split claim, a prior formulation superseded by reduction, or a prohibited authority extension attached to an otherwise bounded analytical result.

```text
failed claim disposition
≠ failed_transformation automatically
```

#### Capture statement

The capture statement describes what the record captures and where its declared capture limit lies. It must be claim-relative:

```yaml
capture_statement:
  statement:
  claim_relative: true
  capture_limit_present:
```

A capture limit may arise from source gaps, rival reference objects, unresolved operation identity, underdetermined target function, or limits on the representable claim. Its presence does not automatically select `non_capture`.

```text
capture limit present
≠ non_capture automatically
```

`non_capture` is selected only where the limiting condition is constitutive for the tested claim, available alternatives cannot be responsibly adjudicated or reduced, the uncaptured structure is identified, and a stronger route would manufacture support or precision.

#### Routing state

The record has two routing states:

```text
routed
formal_diagnostic
```

A `routed` record contains enough claim segmentation, substantive assessment, and boundary adjudication to select exactly one canonical Output Class for each delimited tested claim.

```text
one delimited routed claim
→ exactly one canonical Output Class
```

The ten classes remain the closed inventory defined in Chapter 6. Non-selected findings, earlier results, losses, Stop conditions, and capture limits remain preserved; they are not silently overwritten by the selected route.

A `formal_diagnostic` record is used when the packet cannot yet be substantively routed because a formal prerequisite remains unresolved—for example an incomplete packet, unresolved claim segmentation, no supported candidate, unresolved class collision, or unresolved selection. It preserves available material and states the next permitted handoff.

The diagnostic state must also remain specific enough to audit. A generic statement that “more analysis is needed” does not identify the missing prerequisite, preserve the available material, or establish a lawful re-entry path. Diagnostic precision concerns the process defect; it must not simulate precision about the unresolved substantive claim.

```text
formal diagnostic
→ no canonical Output Class
```

```text
formal diagnostic
≠ failed_transformation
≠ mandatory_stop
≠ non_capture
```

Formal diagnostics are neither an eleventh Output Class nor a way to protect weak claims. Once the missing formal requirement is resolved, the resulting delimited claim must be assessed and routed or explicitly stopped under the ordinary rules.

```text
No fourth operation is introduced.
No eleventh Output Class is introduced.
```

#### Evidence availability as a serialization companion

The current schema also records an evidence-availability summary. This is useful, but it is not a seventh canonical status axis and does not replace support status. `available`, `partially_available`, `missing`, `unavailable`, or `inaccessible` describe access conditions for the source basis. They do not state whether the resulting claim is supported.

```text
evidence availability
≠ evidential support
```

A source packet may be fully available yet support an `unsupported` claim. A partially available packet may support a tightly bounded claim while leaving another claim underdetermined. The record must preserve both statements without converting access difficulty into support or failure.

#### Candidate assessment and routing collision

Before a routed record selects one class, materially plausible candidates must be compared against the same delimited claim. This is especially important where `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, `claim_reduction_required`, `mandatory_stop`, `failed_transformation`, or `non_capture` appear simultaneously plausible. The record must state which constitutive conditions each candidate meets, which condition defeats it, and whether the apparent collision disappears after claim segmentation.

```text
multiple candidate classes considered
≠ multiple classes selected
```

A same-claim collision cannot be resolved by counting passed fields, choosing the rhetorically strongest class, or preserving all classes as co-equal. The record must either adjudicate the boundary, split genuinely distinct claims, or remain formal-diagnostic where the selection prerequisite is unresolved.

The selected route therefore contains a substantive rationale and preserves non-selected findings. It does not erase them. A Stop trigger can remain recorded even when the delimited surviving claim routes elsewhere; an analogy can remain visible when a stronger projection fails; a prior bounded result can remain preserved after a later chain link stops.

#### Claim lineage and historical preservation

Status separation requires a visible lineage among prior, revised, reduced, split, sibling, and successor claims. A record relation may state that a claim was revised from, reduced from, split from, or superseded by another claim. The relation does not make the later claim true and does not overwrite the earlier disposition.

```text
claim lineage
≠ authority inheritance
≠ result inheritance
```

This is the record-level implementation of the rule that a new transformation is a new testable claim. A reduced claim must be tested under its own scope. A successor occurrence must identify its source handoff. A sibling rival must retain its own operation and loss profile. Historical preservation is therefore not archival decoration; it is necessary to prevent failed or stopped claims from being silently rehabilitated through renaming.

#### Diagnostic re-entry and anti-immunization

Every formal diagnostic must identify a next permitted process handoff. Re-entry is conditional: the missing packet element, unresolved segmentation, collision, candidate set, or selection rule must actually be repaired. Repeatedly reissuing the same diagnostic without changing the limiting condition is not progress.

```text
formal diagnostic retained
≠ claim immunized from routing
```

Where the unresolved condition becomes constitutive and cannot be repaired without invented precision, the record must move to the appropriate substantive boundary—possibly `non_capture`, `failed_transformation`, or `mandatory_stop`—rather than remaining indefinitely diagnostic. Conversely, an incomplete packet must not be prematurely routed merely to avoid a diagnostic state.

#### C7-STAT-01 — provisional support without provisional Output Class

The bounded `PROJECT_AS` claim that `P_review` performs a local orientation function in `K3` uses a source basis with one documented gap concerning informal exchanges. The gap is declared, but the function depends on the documented adoption, reconsideration promise, bounded non-realization, and changed reopening conditions rather than on the missing exchanges.

A coherent status declaration is:

```yaml
support_status: provisional
resolution_test_result: null
claim_disposition: maintained
capture_limit_present: true
routing_state: routed
canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The provisional support records the source limitation. The selected class records that the surviving claim is constitutively bounded in context and scope. The two findings are compatible because they answer different questions.

#### C7-STAT-02 — failed claim segment without total transformation failure

A composite claim states both:

1. `P_review` supports a bounded orientation function in `K3`; and
2. the same record authorizes all later review decisions.

Claim segmentation separates the two statements. The first may remain maintained and route to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. The authority segment fails and receives `mandatory_stop` for the prohibited continuation. If the original unsplit statement is retained in lineage, it may be marked `superseded_without_erasure` after the split.

```text
failed authority segment
≠ bounded function erased
≠ entire occurrence forced to failed_transformation
```

The record must preserve both local results and the relation between the original and split claims.

#### C7-DIAG-01 — formal diagnostic without semantic substitution

A packet names two candidate claims over the same source family but does not separate whether it is testing a finer reconstruction of `P_review` or a new responsibility-residue composite. The fields are syntactically populated, yet claim segmentation and operation identity remain unresolved.

The correct routing state is:

```yaml
routing_state: formal_diagnostic
formal_diagnostic:
  diagnostic_id: unresolved_claim_segmentation
  missing_or_unresolved_requirements:
    - distinct claim statements
    - one operation kind per occurrence
    - separate target and loss declarations
  preserved_available_material:
    - source references
    - rival formation hypotheses
    - declared uncertainty
```

No canonical Output Class is selected. This is not `non_capture`, because the packet has not yet reached a properly delimited substantive claim. It is not `failed_transformation`, because no single transformation claim has yet been responsibly tested.

### 7.10 Local and Integrated Use

Every STRATA Part and every operation-specific chapter uses the same shared record duties. Local fields may add resolution where the operation or Part requires it, but they may not replace, rename, merge, or bypass the common envelope.

```text
shared record core
+ bounded local extension
→ operation- or Part-specific record
```

```text
local specialization
≠ local override
```

#### Local extension rule

A local extension is admissible only when it:

- has a stable extension identifier;
- names its owner or control source;
- points to the governing chapter, contract, template, or schema location;
- states a bounded purpose;
- keeps its payload separate from common fields;
- affirms that it does not replace a required field;
- introduces no operation, object primitive, Output Class, authority state, or semantic shortcut;
- remains removable without destroying the common record's interpretability.

A compact carrier may appear as:

```yaml
extensions:
  - extension_id:
    owner_or_source:
    control_source_pointer:
    purpose:
    payload:
    does_not_replace_required_field: true
```

An extension may elaborate `COMPOSE` selection and formation relations, `DECOMPOSE` reconstruction links, `PROJECT_AS` target-function dependencies, or Part-specific PATH, SUB, RETYPE, and LIMITS duties. It may not relocate Loss, Alternatives, Governance, status, or routing into an opaque payload.

#### C7-EXT-01 — admissible bounded extension

A PATH extension adds:

```yaml
extension_id: path_traversal_trace
owner_or_source: PATH
control_source_pointer: Chapter 11 path identification duties
purpose: record actual traversal and transition support
payload:
  traversed_transition_ids:
    - T1
    - T2
  path_selection_rationale: documented review progression
  continuity_breaks: []
does_not_replace_required_field: true
```

The common Source, Operation, Target, Loss, Alternatives, Governance, and result families remain populated. The extension adds inspectable path-specific detail and does not redefine `COMPOSE`, Path, or any shared field.

Local result:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The result applies to the extension design, not automatically to every transformation record that uses it.

#### C7-EXT-02 — extension used as bypass

A proposed local payload contains `final_truth_status`, `loss_override`, and `authority_granted`, while the common Loss and Governance fields are empty or contradicted.

```text
extension payload
used to replace common duties
→ prohibited bypass
```

The record cannot continue as a valid shared record. The bypass receives:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

Re-entry requires removal of the overriding fields, restoration of all shared duties, and a fresh substantive assessment. The extension cannot be saved by schema flexibility or local convention.

#### Operation chains

The default chain representation remains:

```text
one operation occurrence
→ one occurrence record

multiple operation occurrences
→ linked occurrence records
+ separate chain declaration
```

The chain declaration records order and handoffs without becoming a fourth operation or replacing occurrence-level records:

```yaml
chain:
  chain_id:
  ordered_occurrence_record_ids:
  declared_operation_sequence:
  chain_claim_id:
  continuity_handoffs:
  preserved_occurrence_results:
  preserved_occurrence_losses:
  preserved_stops_and_non_capture:
  sequence_alignment_rationale:
```

The chain-level claim must be distinguished from each occurrence claim. It may state that a sequence of transformations was attempted or that one result was used as the declared source of the next occurrence. It may not inherit admissibility across the handoff.

```text
valid predecessor target
≠ valid successor transformation automatically
```

```text
chain summary
≠ replacement of local results
```

#### C7-CHAIN-01 — admissible first link, failed second link

Consider:

```text
R1: COMPOSE source structures into P_review
R2: PROJECT_AS P_review as a universal decision authority
```

`R1` may route to:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

`R2` fails its contextual and authority boundaries and routes to:

```text
failed_transformation
```

or, where the continuation itself insists on prohibited authority transfer:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The chain declaration preserves:

- `R1` and its path result;
- the source handoff from `R1` to `R2`;
- the distinct loss profiles;
- the failure or Stop at `R2`;
- the fact that `R2` neither invalidates `R1` nor receives support from it automatically.

No single chain status replaces these local findings.

#### Integrated use and completeness

Integrated use means that the same claim lineage can be followed from Source through Operation, Target, Admissibility, Loss, Alternatives, Governance, status axes, routing, relations, and any chain or extension declarations. It does not mean that every record must be epistemically complete or routed.

The integrated review proceeds at three distinct scales:

1. **Occurrence scale:** Does one record declare one operation kind, a coherent source–target relation, all common duties, and one local routing state?
2. **Cross-record scale:** Do sibling, prior, successor, reduced, split, and rival records preserve reference, claim, and disposition relations without erasure?
3. **Package scale:** Do chain order, occurrence identifiers, source handoffs, selected classes, preserved losses, extension controls, and Root/schema constraints remain mutually consistent?

```text
package consistency
≠ substantive truth
```

A record can pass occurrence-level syntax and still fail cross-record reference integrity. A chain can contain individually valid records but misstate their order or source handoff. A package can be byte-consistent and schema-valid while its populated claims remain semantically unsupported. The three scales must therefore be audited separately.

#### Compact integrated positive record

The positive `C7-REC-01` candidate can be read as one routed occurrence whose common families remain mutually connected:

```yaml
record_id: C7-REC-01
routing_state: routed
claim:
  statement: P_review performs a bounded orientation function in K3.
source:
  reference_object: P_review
  object_typing: path
  constitutive_source_trace:
    - adoption through E1 and T1
    - promised reconsideration
    - bounded non-realization N1
    - changed reopening conditions through T2
operation:
  kind: PROJECT_AS
target:
  contextual_function: bounded orientation for the K3 process review
  validity_scope: K3 only
admissibility:
  audit_to_routing_basis: source-carried, type-preserving, context-bounded function
loss:
  preserved: [path identity, origin type, relevant temporal order]
  compressed: [fine wording and timing differences]
  excluded: [unrelated project discussions]
  uncertain: [undocumented informal exchanges]
  irrecoverable: []
alternatives:
  rival_projections: [bounded memory-function]
  non_translation: [unbounded institutional-memory label]
governance:
  authority_inheritance: prohibited
status_and_result:
  support_status: provisional
  routing_state: routed
  canonical_output_class: admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

This is a conceptual compact form, not a replacement JSON Schema instance. Its purpose is to show that the selected class depends on relations among the field families, not on their mere presence.

#### Compact integrated negative record

The negative `C7-REC-02` may contain the same headings while replacing Constitutive Source Trace with generic language such as “multiple documents support the conclusion.” If the tested claim is delimited and the deficiency can be substantively assessed, formal completeness does not prevent:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

or:

```text
failed_transformation
```

If operation identity or claim segmentation is still unresolved, the packet instead remains `formal_diagnostic`. The distinction depends on whether a substantive claim has actually been formed and tested.

A declaration-complete record may remain epistemically incomplete when it explicitly records missing sources, unresolved rivals, or a capture limit. Conversely, a syntactically complete record may be substantively weak where generic fields conceal absent Source Trace or operation confusion.

The positive compact record `C7-REC-01` therefore remains a routed candidate only because all common duties are substantively connected. The negative compact record `C7-REC-02` remains vulnerable to `claim_reduction_required` or `failed_transformation` where its generic source trace is sufficient to expose the defect. Where the packet cannot yet delimit the claim or operation, it remains `formal_diagnostic` instead of receiving a fabricated Output Class.

```text
semantic defect that can be adjudicated
→ canonical route

formal prerequisite unresolved
→ formal_diagnostic
```

#### C7-NC-01 — claim-relative Non-Capture

A fully declared packet identifies `P_review`, preserves origin type `path`, and records two source-plausible functions in the same later meeting:

```text
candidate A:
bounded frame-function

candidate B:
bounded memory-function
```

The available target-context evidence does not distinguish whether the path is delimiting current review attention or supplying retrospective retrieval. Both candidates have source support, neither can be reduced without changing the tested claim, and selecting either would manufacture greater target precision.

The capture statement records:

- what the record captures about the path and source features;
- the uncaptured target-function distinction;
- the limiting target-context condition;
- both rival functions;
- the evidence required for re-entry.

The routed Output Class is:

```text
non_capture
```

This case demonstrates the difference between a mere capture limit and canonical Non-Capture. Here the limit is constitutive, claim-relative, resistant to responsible reduction, and preserved with an explicit re-entry condition.

#### Integrated audit boundary

The Integrated STRATA Audit may test cross-record consistency, chain order, reference continuity, preserved losses, status separation, routing exclusivity, extension boundaries, and schema conformance. It may not infer empirical truth, causal adequacy, correct interpretation, normative validity, person status, or application authority from record completeness.

```text
integrated traceability
≠ integrated authority
```

#### WP3 boundary and handoff

Sections 7.9–7.10 establish:

- six separated status and result axes;
- the distinction between routed and formal-diagnostic records;
- exactly one canonical Output Class per delimited routed claim and none for a formal diagnostic;
- claim-relative capture statements and the non-automatic relation between capture limits and `non_capture`;
- historical preservation through claim disposition and record relations;
- bounded local extensions that cannot replace shared duties;
- separate occurrence records and a non-operative chain declaration;
- preservation of local results, losses, Stop, and Non-Capture across chains;
- integrated use that distinguishes declaration completeness, epistemic incompleteness, semantic defect, and formal routing incompleteness;
- seven controlled cases: `C7-STAT-01`, `C7-STAT-02`, `C7-DIAG-01`, `C7-CHAIN-01`, `C7-EXT-01`, `C7-EXT-02`, and `C7-NC-01`.

At the WP3 checkpoint, Chapter 7 was theoretically complete through Section 7.10. The following then remained assigned to WP4:

- integrated redundancy and ownership audit across Sections 7.1–7.10;
- registration of the sixteen Chapter-7 case obligations without false evidence claims;
- prose-to-schema comparison and bounded model handoff;
- decision on any schema synchronization pressure;
- Root, Reference, package, link, schema, fingerprint, and roundtrip verification;
- provisional-lock decision.

The WP3 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

No known local defect remains in Sections 7.9–7.10. Their historical provisionality attached only to the then-uncompleted integrated audit, formal-model comparison, case registration, package verification, and lock decision.

#### Chapter 7 integrated completion and provisional lock

Sections 7.1–7.10 now form one integrated Shared Transformation Record architecture. The completed chapter defines the record boundary, the eight-family common envelope, Source–Operation–Target declaration duties, Admissibility, Loss, Alternatives, Governance, separated status and result axes, Formal Diagnostics, occurrence and chain representation, local extensions, claim lineage, and integrated use.

```text
identified record and claim state
+ declared Source, Operation, and Target
+ Admissibility, Loss, Alternatives, and Governance
+ separated support, resolution, disposition,
  capture, routing, and Output-Class axes
+ occurrence, relation, chain, and extension controls
→ inspectable transformation-record state
```

The integrated completion test confirms:

- a transformation and its record remain non-equivalent;
- record completeness and schema validity do not establish admissibility, truth, causality, semantic adequacy, legitimacy, diagnosis, or authority;
- declaration completeness remains distinct from epistemic completeness;
- the common envelope preserves operation-specific semantics rather than flattening them;
- Source reference, typing, coordinates, scopes, basis, Constitutive Source Trace, known gaps, and Source Ceiling remain separately inspectable;
- one operation occurrence has exactly one operation kind, while chains use linked occurrence records plus a separate chain declaration;
- Target Object and Target Function remain distinct, and `PROJECT_AS` preserves origin type;
- Admissibility fields record substantive findings but do not decide them automatically;
- all five Loss categories remain declared and non-interchangeable;
- rival transformations, no transformation, non-translation, and unresolved alternatives remain distinct;
- Governance records Claim Ceiling, Authority Ceiling, Stop, Non-Capture, prohibited inferences, external warrant, and the prohibition on authority inheritance;
- support status, resolution-test result, Claim Disposition, capture statement, routing state, and canonical Output Class remain separate axes;
- each delimited routed claim receives exactly one canonical Output Class, while a Formal Diagnostic receives none;
- claim lineage preserves failed, reduced, split, withdrawn, and superseded states without erasure or inherited result;
- chain summaries do not overwrite local results, losses, Stop, or Non-Capture;
- local extensions may add bounded detail but may not replace common duties, operations, classes, primitives, or authority limits;
- occurrence-, cross-record-, and package-level consistency checks remain distinct from substantive judgment;
- no schema, root manifest, or machine-valid record becomes a theory source or application authority.

The completed prose-to-schema comparison finds that the existing `Transformation_Record.schema.json` already supplies structural carriers for the canonical duties. No structural schema revision, new field family, new routing state, new operation, new Output Class, or smoke-record migration is required. A prose-owned Chapter-7 handoff annotation records the comparison result without transferring semantic authority to the schema.

The integrated Chapter 7 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

Chapter 7 is provisionally locked. The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves legitimate later revision pressure from Chapter 8 Foundational Non-Equivalences, the operation- and Part-specific record procedures, substantive cases and countercases, Appendix C and record templates, the Integrated STRATA Audit, package-level validators, corpus audit, model finalization, and later source-supported record-boundary findings. Reopening requires a documented contradiction, failed case burden, ownership conflict, schema mismatch, audit failure, or later source-supported boundary finding.

---

## 8. Foundational Non-Equivalences

Chapter 8 converts the distinctions established in Chapters 0–7 into a compact audit catalogue. It does not create a second definition layer. Each pair returns to definitions already owned by PMS Base or the preceding Foundations chapters and asks whether a later claim preserves those distinctions.

The governing audit rule is:

```text
A non-equivalence does not prohibit comparison,
relation, analogy, or transformation.

It prohibits unmarked identity,
category collapse,
and authority transfer.
```

The symbol `≠` therefore performs a bounded audit function. It denies that one term may silently inherit the type, scope, warrant, success conditions, truth status, or authority of the other. It does not assert that the terms are unrelated, that one term is false, or that no transformation may connect them.

```text
non-equivalence
≠ non-relation
≠ empirical counterclaim
≠ automatic Output Class
```

A valid analysis may compare finer and coarser reconstructions, locate objects at different relative levels, form composites, or reconstruct an occurrence more finely. The audit burden is to declare the relation rather than substitute identity.

Every foundational pair in this chapter uses the same compact form:

```text
pair statement
→ identity collapse blocked
→ invalid identity claim
→ admissible relation or transformation
→ primary definition return
```

A detected collapse remains a local finding. Its eventual route depends on the delimited claim, the relevant operation, source support, continuity, loss, admissibility, alternatives, Stop, and Non-Capture.

```text
non-equivalence breach
≠ one automatic Output Class
```

A collapse may require claim reduction, preserve only analogy, defeat a transformation, contribute to partial admissibility, require Mandatory Stop, or remain unresolved under Non-Capture. Chapter 8 supplies the category-error test; Chapters 6 and 7 retain routing control.

A relation across a non-equivalence must name its own type and burden. A comparison identifies an axis and does not by itself transform either object. An analogy states bounded resemblance and does not inherit semantic preservation. An operation occurrence declares a source–target transformation under one of the three closed operation kinds. A property test asks whether an object bears a separately defined property. A contextual-function claim preserves origin type while assigning a bounded role. A local-superiority claim compares performance for one declared question without creating a universal ranking.

```text
declared relation type
+ relation-specific warrant
≠ identity by implication
```

Although `≠` is symmetric as a denial of identity, the characteristic audit pressure can be directional. Finer granularity is often promoted into higher truth; relative level into ontology; composition into totality; decomposition into essence. The reverse direction can also fail—for example, denying a valid finer distinction because no universal truth hierarchy is allowed. Chapter 8 therefore blocks automatic transfer in either direction while identifying the historically common direction of inflation.

```text
blocking promotion
≠ forbidding bounded comparative judgment

blocking reduction
≠ requiring identity
```

The audit must preserve any weaker or differently typed claim that remains source-supported after the collapse is removed. This anti-erasure rule prevents Non-Equivalence from becoming a blunt rejection mechanism.

### 8.1 Finer Granularity Is Not Higher Truth

```text
finer granularity
≠ higher truth
```

Granularity identifies how finely an object or relation is reconstructed within a declared frame, source basis, and claim scope. A finer reconstruction may expose distinctions that a coarser reconstruction compresses. It may also amplify noise, exceed the Source Ceiling, obscure relations that matter at another resolution, or add no PraxisPurchase.

The non-equivalence blocks the following transfer:

```text
more detail
→ automatically more truth
```

That transfer is invalid because truth, warrant, and admissibility do not arise from resolution alone. A finer reconstruction still depends on source support, object identity, Type Integrity, Contextual Boundedness, TraceableLoad, loss disclosure, and the claim being tested.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
R_fine contains more distinctions than R_coarse.
Therefore R_fine is the truer reconstruction as such.
```

The conclusion exceeds the premise. Additional distinctions establish neither their own source support nor their relevance to the tested claim. They also do not show that the coarser reconstruction is false, that every excluded distinction matters, or that the finer reconstruction preserves the relations the coarser account makes visible.

#### Admissible relation

A bounded comparative claim may remain valid:

```text
For claim Q under frame F and source basis S,
R_fine discriminates relation D
that R_coarse compresses.
```

This is a local performance claim, not a truth hierarchy. It must identify the tested claim, the relevant distinction, the sources carrying it, the change in warranted reconstruction, and the costs of the finer resolution.

```text
bounded superiority for Q
≠ higher truth in general
```

A coarser reconstruction may remain preferable for another claim, frame, or comparison. Multiple granularities can be jointly necessary without being ranked as ontological or epistemic strata.

#### C8-GRAN-01 — bounded superiority without truth inflation

The coarse account of the review process records:

```text
adoption
→ later review difficulty
```

A finer reconstruction distinguishes:

```text
E1 / T1
→ adoption and transition

promised reconsideration
→ explicit expectation

N1 / T2
→ bounded non-realization
  and changed reopening conditions
```

For the delimited claim concerning reopening burden, the finer reconstruction performs better. It shows that the later burden is not merely subsequent to adoption but is carried by the combination of an explicit reconsideration expectation, bounded non-realization, and altered continuation conditions.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The bounded claim is that the finer reconstruction better supports this specified reopening-burden analysis. It is not that fine-grained analysis is universally truer, that every detail matters, or that the coarse account has no legitimate use.

#### C8-GRAN-02 — detail used as truth rank

A second analysis subdivides the same meeting into pauses, sentence fragments, synonyms, and minor timing differences. It then claims superior truth solely because it contains more coded units.

The added distinctions do not change the warranted reconstruction of adoption, reconsideration, `N1`, `T2`, roles, costs, alternatives, or target function. Some distinctions may be accurately observed, but they create no claim-specific Resolution Gain.

The inflated claim must be reduced:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

A weaker descriptive statement about additional detail may remain. If the analyst insists that detail count itself confers truth or authority and continues deriving claims from that promotion, the analysis reaches:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop concerns the truth-rank transfer, not the existence of the finer observations.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 0 for claim and authority boundaries;
- Chapter 2 for granularity and comparison discipline;
- Chapter 6 for PraxisPurchase, the Relevance Floor, TraceableLoad, and non-compensation.

Chapter 8 does not redefine granularity or truth. It blocks their unmarked identification.

### 8.2 Relative Level Is Not Ontological Layer

```text
relative level
≠ ontological layer
```

A relative level locates an analytical object in relation to other objects under a declared frame, granularity, reference set, and comparison axis. It is relational and revisable. The same object may occupy a different relative position under another legitimate analytical declaration.

An ontological layer, by contrast, would claim that reality itself is divided into intrinsic strata carrying fixed depth, height, necessity, or authority. STRATA does not make that claim.

The non-equivalence blocks:

```text
analytically higher
→ more real, more fundamental,
  more explanatory, or more authoritative
```

It also blocks the reverse collapse:

```text
analytically finer or lower
→ deeper essence
```

Relative position does not determine being, truth, causal priority, moral rank, or application authority.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review is located at a higher relative level
than E1 and N1.
Therefore P_review belongs to a higher layer of reality
and explains the lower elements by virtue of that status.
```

The first statement may be a legitimate coordinate declaration. The second adds ontology and explanatory privilege without warrant. `P_review` is a composite analytical object formed from selected structures and relations. Its relative position does not make it more real than its sources, and its existence does not automatically explain them.

#### Admissible relation

```text
Within frame F and reference set R,
P_review is relatively wider than E1 and N1
because it contains and relates their selected transitions.
```

This relation is admissible when the comparison axis, reference set, frame, and purpose are declared. The resulting coordinate can support navigation, comparison, operation selection, or scope control without becoming ontology.

```text
relative location
→ analytical coordination
≠ ontological ranking
```

A coordinate change may be required without changing operation identity. An operation may also occur while relative level remains stable. Chapter 4 retains operation identity; Chapter 2 retains coordinate identity.

#### C8-LEVEL-01 — analytical height converted into ontology

An analyst composes `P_review` and calls it a “higher-order reality” that governs the “lower-level events.” The analysis then treats any disagreement with the composite as a failure to understand the deeper layer.

The composite may be useful and its relative level may be correctly declared. The ontological and authority transfer is not supported. It immunizes the composite from source criticism and reverses the authority order by making a derived object superior to the structures from which it was formed.

The correct repair is:

```text
higher relative level
→ bounded analytical position

not

higher reality
→ inherited explanatory authority
```

The ontological segment receives:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

If the derived-layer claim is used to override Source Ceiling, countercases, or earlier failed claims, continuation requires:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 0 for No Ontology of Layers and more structure ≠ more authority;
- Chapter 1 for derived object versus primitive;
- Chapter 2 for relative level, reference set, frame, and comparison axis;
- Chapter 4 for operation identity versus coordinate direction.

Chapter 8 adds no level ontology. It preserves analytical location without metaphysical promotion.

### 8.3 Composition Is Not Lossless Addition

```text
composition
≠ lossless addition
```

`COMPOSE` forms a new composite analytical object from multiple or sequential source structures. The operation requires selection, ordering where relevant, a constitutive formation relation, preservation duties, and explicit loss.

A composite is not merely all source material placed together. Its formation establishes a new analytical object and therefore changes what is foregrounded, related, compressed, and excluded.

```text
new composite object
≠ arithmetic sum of sources
≠ total reproduction of sources
```

The non-equivalence blocks two opposite errors:

1. treating composition as lossless accumulation;
2. denying the reality of the new composite merely because formation is selective and lossy.

Selective formation can be admissible. Loss must be declared rather than denied.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review contains K0, E1/T1, K1, N1/T2, and K2.
Therefore P_review preserves everything in those sources
and equals their complete addition.
```

This claim hides the selection rule, compresses heterogeneity, ignores excluded alternatives, and treats the new path object as if it were only a container. Even a carefully constructed composite cannot preserve every source feature at every granularity and for every claim.

#### Admissible transformation

```text
COMPOSE selected review structures
under declared ordering and formation rules
→ P_review as a bounded path composite
with explicit preservation and loss
```

The admissible claim identifies:

- which source objects are included;
- why they are selected;
- how order and relations constitute the composite;
- what the composite permits the analyst to reconstruct;
- what is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable within the source basis;
- which rival compositions remain possible.

#### C8-COMP-01 — real composite with disclosed loss

The formation of `P_review` preserves:

```text
adoption through E1/T1
promised reconsideration
bounded non-realization N1
changed reopening conditions through T2
relevant order from K0 to K2
```

It compresses fine wording and timing differences, excludes unrelated discussion, and leaves undocumented informal exchanges uncertain. No irrecoverable loss is asserted beyond the available source basis.

The composite supports a bounded path claim and later source-sensitive functions. Its local result remains:

```text
admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The result does not mean that the composite is exhaustive, that every source relation survives unchanged, or that alternative compositions have been defeated.

```text
loss disclosure
≠ composite invalidation
```

Loss is part of responsible composition, not proof that composition failed. The relevant question is whether the disclosed loss remains compatible with the tested claim and the Admissibility Band.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 1 for composite structure and derived object boundaries;
- Chapter 4 for the `COMPOSE` signature and operation occurrence;
- Chapter 6 for PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, non-compensation, and claim routing;
- Chapter 7 for the five-part Loss Declaration and alternatives.

Chapter 8 does not define `COMPOSE` again. It blocks the fiction that formation is total or additive without loss.

### 8.4 Decomposition Is Not Discovery of Final Constituents

```text
decomposition
≠ discovery of final constituents
```

`DECOMPOSE` reconstructs a provisionally compressed occurrence or composite at a finer relational resolution while retaining the same reference object for testing. The result remains frame-, source-, granularity-, and claim-bound.

A successful decomposition may reveal previously compressed relations. It does not establish that the resulting elements are ontologically final, universally privileged, context-free, or immune from later revision.

```text
finer reconstruction
≠ ultimate constituent inventory
≠ deeper essence
```

The non-equivalence protects both the operation and its Stop boundary. Because another finer reconstruction may be possible, the current one is not final. Because further decomposition is possible, it is not automatically required.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
DECOMPOSE P_review
→ E1, T1, N1, and T2

Therefore E1, T1, N1, and T2
are the final real constituents of P_review.
```

The result overstates the operation. The selected elements are finer components under a declared frame and source basis. Another claim may require different distinctions; missing sources may leave relations uncertain; a still finer analysis may be possible; and further detail may fall below the Relevance Floor.

The operation also does not authorize decomposition of an abstract PMS operator type. Occurrences and composites may be opened; Δ–Ψ types remain non-decomposable.

#### Admissible transformation

```text
DECOMPOSE P_review
under frame F, source basis S,
and claim Q
→ finer reconstruction of the same path reference
```

The admissible claim declares:

- the same reference object;
- the provisional compression being opened;
- the finer relations reconstructed;
- the source traces supporting them;
- the relation of the finer result to the coarser function;
- the selected granularity and Stop condition;
- uncertainty, loss, and rival decompositions.

The finer reconstruction may confirm, narrow, revise, or defeat the coarser claim. It has no automatic truth priority merely because it is finer.

#### C8-DEC-01 — decomposition inflated into ontological atomization

An analyst opens `P_review` into meeting utterances, pauses, individual actions, and coded micro-events. The analysis then asserts that these micro-units are the final constituents from which all path meaning must be rebuilt.

Some micro-units may be source-supported. The final-constituent claim is not. It ignores frame dependence, selection, relation, uncertainty, the possibility of different admissible decompositions, and the Relevance Floor.

The repair separates the claims:

```text
source-supported finer reconstruction
→ may remain admissible or resolution_neutral

final-constituent ontology
→ failed_transformation
```

If analysis continues to derive universal or operator-level conclusions from the known atomization error, the route becomes:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

A Stop does not deny that the finer observations exist. It denies the prohibited promotion from selected reconstruction to final ontology.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 1 for occurrence, composite, and operator-type boundaries;
- Chapter 2 for frame, granularity, scope, and comparison discipline;
- Chapter 4 for the `DECOMPOSE` signature, reference continuity, and non-invertibility;
- Chapter 6 for the Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, and Stop;
- Chapter 7 for Source, Loss, Alternatives, and status routing.

Chapter 8 adds no constituent ontology. It preserves finer reconstruction as a bounded, revisable analytical operation.

#### WP1 comparison matrix

| Non-equivalence | Invalid identity claim | Admissible relation or transformation | Primary definition return |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| finer granularity ≠ higher truth | more coded detail is truer as such | finer reconstruction performs better for claim `Q` under declared sources and scope | Chapters 0, 2, 6 |
| relative level ≠ ontological layer | relatively higher composite is a higher reality | object is relatively wider under declared frame, reference set, and axis | Chapters 0, 1, 2, 4 |
| composition ≠ lossless addition | composite equals complete sum of sources | `COMPOSE` forms a new bounded object with disclosed selection and loss | Chapters 1, 4, 6, 7 |
| decomposition ≠ discovery of final constituents | finer elements are ultimate real parts | `DECOMPOSE` reconstructs the same reference object at a declared, revisable resolution | Chapters 1, 2, 4, 6, 7 |

The matrix establishes a reusable pattern: deny unmarked identity while preserving a typed, bounded relation. The remaining nine rows are assigned to WP2 and WP3.

#### C8-AUD-01 — non-equivalence with admissible relation

A finer reconstruction `R_fine` and a coarser reconstruction `R_coarse` are explicitly declared as non-identical in granularity and performance. The analysis compares them for one reopening-burden claim and concludes only that `R_fine` reveals a source-supported distinction compressed by `R_coarse`.

```text
R_fine ≠ R_coarse
and
R_fine may outperform R_coarse for Q
```

The relation is admissible because it declares comparison rather than identity, preserves both objects, names the claim and source basis, and does not transfer universal truth or authority.

#### C8-AUD-02 — unmarked identity collapse

A report states that “higher-level analysis is deeper truth” and then uses the phrase interchangeably for wider composition, finer decomposition, and stronger authority. The wording collapses granularity, relative level, operation identity, truth rank, and authority.

The immediate requirement is claim segmentation:

```text
coordinate claim
operation claim
comparative-performance claim
authority claim
```

Until those claims are separated, the packet remains:

```text
formal_diagnostic
```

If the analyst refuses separation and continues deriving conclusions from the collapse, the continuation reaches:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The diagnostic state is not an eleventh Output Class. It records that the claim has not yet been delimited sufficiently for canonical routing.

#### WP1 boundary and handoff

Sections 8.1–8.4 establish:

- non-equivalence as a prohibition of unmarked identity, category collapse, and authority transfer;
- non-equivalence versus non-relation, empirical counterclaim, and automatic Output-Class routing;
- the five-part audit form and the first four matrix rows;
- finer granularity versus higher truth;
- relative level versus ontological layer;
- composition versus lossless addition;
- decomposition versus discovery of final constituents;
- seven controlled pressure cases: `C8-AUD-01`, `C8-AUD-02`, `C8-GRAN-01`, `C8-GRAN-02`, `C8-LEVEL-01`, `C8-COMP-01`, and `C8-DEC-01`.

The WP1 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves the still-pending temporal, type, function, operator, analogy, recursion, authority, integrated-catalogue, model-handoff, and lock duties of WP2–WP4.

### 8.5 Path Is Not Sequence

```text
path
≠ sequence
```

A sequence and a path can contain some of the same configurations, events, non-events, or transitions. They can preserve the same supported temporal order. They are nevertheless not the same analytical object.

A sequence declares selected units and their supported order. A path adds a stronger burden: the selected configurations and transitions must form an actually traversed, constitutively connected chain for the tested claim. The path must disclose how the units were selected, what traversal is source-supported, which branches or continuations matter, and what omission or compression remains.

```text
supported temporal order
≠ actually traversed constitutive chain
```

The non-equivalence blocks two opposite errors:

1. promoting any ordered series into a path merely because the dates line up;
2. treating a valid sequence as analytically worthless because the stronger path burden has not been met.

A sequence can be a necessary input to a path reconstruction without being identical to the resulting path. The relation is developmental and burden-increasing, not ontological promotion.

```text
sequence may support path formation
≠ sequence becomes path automatically
```

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
K0 precedes E1/T1,
which precedes K1,
which precedes N1/T2,
which precedes K2.

Therefore the ordered series is P_review as a path.
```

The inference is incomplete. Temporal order alone does not establish that the selected units constitute the actually traversed chain relevant to the claim. It does not identify the selection rule, the source trace for traversal, the role of non-realization, the status of possible branches, or the loss created by the reconstruction.

The same chronology could support several sequence candidates. Only some may support the path claim. A path cannot be obtained by replacing the missing formation burden with narrative fluency.

#### Admissible relation

```text
sequence S_review
+ supported traversal
+ declared selection and formation rule
+ constitutive transition relations
+ branch, omission, and loss disclosure
→ path candidate P_review
```

The sequence remains visible as an input object. `COMPOSE` may form the path from selected temporal structures, but the operation does not erase the distinction between the ordered source series and the new path composite.

The admissible relation therefore states:

- which sequence units are used;
- which are not used and why;
- how actual traversal is supported;
- which configurations and transitions carry the path claim;
- how events and non-events contribute to those transitions;
- which branches are realized, blocked, deferred, aborted, unresolved, or unsupported;
- which compression and uncertainty remain;
- which claim and temporal scope the path serves.

#### C8-PATH-01 — chronology substituted for realized formation

The project-review archive supports the following order:

```text
K0
→ E1/T1
→ K1
→ N1/T2
→ K2
```

An analyst calls this order `P_review` without identifying why these units constitute one traversed review path rather than one useful sequence among several. The archive contains an agenda record, adoption minutes, a reconsideration promise, an absent review within a bounded window, and later minutes. The order is supported. The actual-traversal and formation claim remains under-declared.

The appropriate repair is not to discard the chronology. It is to reduce and then retest the stronger claim:

```text
supported sequence
→ retained

path identity
→ claim_reduction_required
```

Re-entry requires a declared path selection rule, constitutive transition relations, traversal trace, branch treatment, and loss disclosure. If those burdens are supplied, a new `COMPOSE` claim may be tested. If the analyst continues to use the sequence as though path formation had already been established, the stronger path claim may become `failed_transformation`.

```text
failure of path claim
≠ erasure of valid sequence
```

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 1 for temporal objects and composites;
- Chapter 2 for frame, temporal scope, source scope, and claim scope;
- Chapter 3 for the primary definitions of sequence and path;
- Chapter 4 for `COMPOSE`, formation, selection, and operation identity;
- Chapter 6 for PraxisPurchase, TraceableLoad, Claim Reduction, and Stop;
- Chapter 7 for Source, Operation, Target, Loss, Alternatives, and routing declarations.

Chapter 8 does not redefine sequence or path. It blocks chronology from silently inheriting actual traversal and constitutive formation.

### 8.6 Path Is Not Trajectory

```text
path
≠ trajectory
```

A path reconstructs an actually traversed chain. A trajectory carries an additional historical burden: selected path structures must have sedimented through repetition, persistence, accumulated residue, changed costs, altered expectations, or another source-supported mechanism that changes later continuation possibilities.

```text
actually traversed path
≠ historically sedimented development automatically
```

Not every path has strong trajectory form. A path may be brief, weakly persistent, easily reversible, or limited to one bounded transition chain. A trajectory claim requires more than duration or retrospective importance. It requires evidence that the path-form has become historically load-bearing for later praxis.

The non-equivalence also protects valid path analysis from inflationary rejection. A path does not fail merely because trajectory formation cannot yet be established.

```text
path without sufficient sedimentation
→ may remain a valid path
≠ failed temporal reconstruction automatically
```

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review is an actually traversed path from K0 to K2.
Therefore P_review is the governance trajectory.
```

The inference omits the trajectory burden. It does not show persistence across relevant contexts, accumulated residue, altered continuation possibilities, irreversibility, or another supported form of sedimentation. Naming the path historically important does not supply those relations.

A long path is not automatically a trajectory. A frequently narrated path is not automatically sedimented. A path that influences one later meeting may carry a bounded contextual function without possessing the broader historical form required for trajectory typing.

#### Admissible relation

```text
path P_review
+ source-supported sedimentation
+ accumulated historical load
+ altered later continuation possibilities
+ bounded temporal and claim scope
→ trajectory candidate Tr_review
```

The relation may be established through later evidence showing, for example, that the unfulfilled reconsideration promise repeatedly changes reopening costs, agenda access, expectations, or available continuations across subsequent configurations.

The trajectory candidate must state:

- which path structures persist or recur;
- what has sedimented rather than merely occurred;
- which later continuations become easier, harder, unavailable, or newly salient;
- how the historical load remains traceable to the source path;
- which rival trajectory formations remain possible;
- where the trajectory begins and where the current evidence stops.

A trajectory is therefore not a more authoritative version of a path. It is a differently burdened historical object.

#### C8-TRAJ-01 — path promoted without sedimentation

`P_review` is source-supported as a bounded path from adoption through the unfulfilled reconsideration window to `K2`. An analyst labels it a trajectory because it spans several weeks and appears relevant to a later meeting.

The record contains no evidence that the path repeatedly constrained later continuations, accumulated durable residue beyond the bounded endpoint, or altered available action corridors across a wider temporal scope.

The claim must be segmented:

```text
P_review as path
→ retained

P_review as trajectory
→ claim_reduction_required
```

If the trajectory label is merely stylistic, it should be removed. If a trajectory is intended, re-entry requires evidence of sedimentation and changed continuation possibilities. If the analyst insists that every realized path is already a trajectory, the trajectory claim becomes:

```text
failed_transformation
```

The failure does not retroactively invalidate `P_review` as a path.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 3 for path, trajectory, sedimentation, historical load, and continuation-change;
- Chapter 4 for operation and composite boundaries;
- Chapter 5 for temporal and functional continuity;
- Chapter 6 for Source/Claim Ceiling, non-compensation, and claim routing;
- Chapter 7 for temporal scope, alternatives, loss, and status/result separation.

Chapter 8 adds no second trajectory definition. It blocks path existence, duration, or importance from substituting for sedimentation.

### 8.7 Trajectory Is Not Path Dependence

```text
trajectory
≠ path dependence
```

Trajectory and path dependence stand in an object–property relation. A trajectory is a historically developed analytical object. Path dependence is a separately tested property concerning the degree and manner in which earlier path positions condition, constrain, enable, or make costly later continuations.

```text
historical object
≠ property of historical determination
```

A trajectory may display weak, moderate, strong, local, contested, or currently underdetermined path dependence. The existence of sedimentation does not by itself prove that earlier selections determined later outcomes or that alternative continuations became inaccessible.

The non-equivalence blocks automatic historical determinism. It also prevents the opposite error of denying trajectory status whenever strong path dependence cannot be proven.

```text
trajectory with weak or underdetermined path dependence
→ possible
```

Path-dependence testing must identify the relevant earlier position, the later continuation set, the mechanism of constraint or enablement, counterfactual sensitivity, available alternatives, and the claim ceiling. Repetition, persistence, irreversibility, sunk cost, expectation formation, or institutional residue may be relevant, but none is a universal shortcut.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
Tr_review is a trajectory.
Therefore its later outcome was path-dependent
and could not reasonably have developed otherwise.
```

The statement collapses object identity into a strong property claim and then inflates that property into inevitability. It does not specify which earlier selection constrained which later continuation, how alternatives changed, or what source evidence supports the mechanism.

```text
path dependence
≠ inevitability
≠ complete historical determination
```

Even a positive path-dependence finding remains bounded. It may concern one continuation, one cost structure, one temporal interval, or one decision corridor rather than the total later development.

#### Admissible property test

```text
trajectory Tr_review
+ declared earlier path position
+ declared later continuation set
+ traceable conditioning mechanism
+ counterfactual and alternative testing
+ bounded claim scope
→ path-dependence finding
```

The result may be positive, negative, partial, provisional, or underdetermined. Those are local findings, not automatic Output Classes.

A responsible test asks:

- Which earlier path element is claimed to matter?
- Which later possibilities are claimed to be conditioned?
- What source-supported mechanism carries the historical effect?
- Would a relevant change in the earlier structure alter the later continuation set or burden?
- Which alternatives remained available, became costly, or disappeared?
- What uncertainty prevents stronger attribution?

#### C8-PD-01 — trajectory converted into determinism

Suppose later records support `Tr_review`: the unfulfilled reconsideration promise sediments across several review cycles and repeatedly changes the burden of reopening. An analyst concludes that the later governance arrangement is therefore path-dependent in a strong and irreversible sense.

The trajectory evidence supports historical load. It does not yet establish the strength, mechanism, scope, or counterfactual sensitivity of path dependence.

The local routing is:

```text
trajectory claim
→ retained

strong path-dependence claim
→ formal_diagnostic until separately tested
```

If the property test shows only increased reopening cost while alternatives remain accessible, the stronger claim may require:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

If no traceable conditioning relation can be established, the path-dependence claim may become `failed_transformation` while the trajectory remains intact. Where two plausible mechanisms remain unresolved and the available sources cannot responsibly discriminate, `non_capture` may later be appropriate for the delimited property claim—but not automatically for the trajectory object.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 3 for trajectory and path dependence as distinct burdens;
- Chapter 5 for temporal continuity and source-sensitive functional relations;
- Chapter 6 for Counterfactual Sensitivity, TraceableLoad, and Non-Capture;
- Chapter 7 for claim segmentation, Formal Diagnostics, capture statements, and routing.

Chapter 8 preserves trajectory as an object and path dependence as a separately tested property. It authorizes no automatic historical determinism.

### 8.8 Origin Type Is Not Target Function

```text
origin type
≠ target function
```

`PROJECT_AS` permits an origin-typed source object to perform a bounded function in a declared target context. The operation does not rewrite the source object's origin type.

```text
origin-typed source object
→ bounded contextual target function
```

The relation is functional, contextual, source-sensitive, and scope-limited. It is not a type conversion, primitive creation, or retrospective redescription of what the source object always was.

A path may perform a frame-function. A trajectory may perform a memory-function. A composite may perform an orienting or limiting function. In each case, the source remains a path, trajectory, or composite unless a separate, independently warranted type revision is made under the relevant object rules.

```text
functional similarity
≠ type identity
```

The non-equivalence is direction-sensitive. It blocks both:

1. replacing the origin type with the target function;
2. treating origin-type preservation as a ban on contextual function.

The valid relation retains both sides.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review performs a frame-function in K3.
Therefore P_review is a Frame operator as its origin type.
```

The statement converts contextual performance into source identity. It also confuses a derived function with the abstract PMS `Frame` operator type. The source may orient what becomes salient in the later review context, but it remains a path.

#### Admissible projection

```text
source object: P_review
origin type: path
target context: K3 process-review meeting
target function: bounded frame-function
validity scope: one reopening-burden question
```

The admissible claim states which source features carry the function, how a relevant source change would alter it, what rival functions remain possible, and where the function ceases to apply.

```text
path preserved
+ frame-function declared separately
→ possible PROJECT_AS candidate
```

#### C8-TYPE-01 — frame-function treated as origin-type replacement
