The source path contains adoption, a promised reconsideration, bounded non-realization, and altered reopening conditions. In `K3`, this history orients one question about why reopening now carries procedural burden.

The contextual function may be source-sensitive and bounded. The following identity claim is not:

```text
P_review is now a Frame operator.
```

The dimensions must be separated:

```text
reference continuity
→ supported

origin type: path
→ preserved

target function: frame-function
→ separately testable

operator-type replacement
→ failed
```

The stronger type-replacement claim receives:

```text
failed_transformation
```

If the analyst continues to derive operator-level consequences from the known replacement error, the route becomes:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop does not erase the weaker bounded frame-function candidate.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 1 for object, occurrence, composite, derived object, and primitive boundaries;
- Chapter 4 for `PROJECT_AS` identity and operation occurrence;
- Chapter 5 for origin type, target function, Transformation Context, Type Integrity, and Functional Continuity;
- Chapter 6 for the non-compensatory Admissibility Band;
- Chapter 7 for separate Source and Target declarations, function-origin reference, governance, and routing.

Chapter 8 does not redefine projection. It blocks contextual function from erasing source identity.

### 8.9 Projection Is Not Operator Identity

```text
projection
≠ operator identity
```

A valid `PROJECT_AS` result is a derived contextual function. It is not a new PMS operator and does not make the source object identical to an existing Δ–Ψ operator type.

```text
operator-like contextual performance
≠ operator type
≠ new primitive
```

The phrase “operator-like” may describe a limited functional resemblance: a projected object can orient salience, preserve a boundary, constrain continuation, or make a relation visible in a target context. Such language remains subordinate to origin-type preservation, target-function precision, source trace, and contextual boundedness.

Projection cannot:

- rename a Δ–Ψ operator;
- insert a derived function into the PMS Base inventory;
- change canonical operator order or dependencies;
- decompose an operator type;
- create a new primitive by repeated use;
- inherit authority from semantic resemblance.

The non-equivalence therefore protects both projection and PMS Base. Projection remains possible precisely because it does not need operator identity.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review performs an operator-like orienting function in K3.
Therefore it is a new operator between Frame and the next PMS operator.
```

The claim converts a bounded derived function into a grammar revision. Frequency, usefulness, or apparent stability does not supply the authority required to revise PMS Base. Repeated projections remain repeated derived-function occurrences, not evidence of primitive status.

```text
repeated contextual function
≠ operator admission procedure
```

#### Admissible relation

```text
PROJECT_AS(P_review, K3, bounded frame-function)
→ derived contextual function
with origin type preserved
and no operator-identity claim
```

The projection may be admissible, provisional, reduced, analogical, or failed depending on its source trace and target scope. None of those results changes the PMS operator inventory.

A structural analogy to an operator may assist orientation. That analogy must remain marked and cannot substitute for the stronger projection burden. WP3 will test that separate pair.

#### C8-PROJ-01 — operator-like function inflated into a primitive

An analyst observes that the projected path repeatedly organizes salience in later review meetings. The analyst names the result `Λ_review` and inserts it into the Δ–Ψ sequence as a new operator.

The record may retain the following weaker material:

```text
recurrent bounded frame-function
→ potentially source-supported
```

It may not retain:

```text
new operator identity
or
new dependency position
```

The primitive-inflation claim is a `failed_transformation`. Because the proposal would alter the closed PMS Base grammar and use later STRATA results as upstream authority, continuation with the inserted operator requires:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

A new versioned PMS Base proposal would be a separate project under separate authority. It cannot be smuggled into STRATA through a projection record.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- PMS Base for the Δ–Ψ operator grammar, order, and dependencies;
- Chapter 0 for No Meta-PMS and more structure ≠ more authority;
- Chapter 1 for derived object/function versus primitive;
- Chapter 4 for `PROJECT_AS` and the prohibition of a fourth operation;
- Chapter 5 for origin-type preservation and target-function boundedness;
- Chapter 7 for governance and authority-inheritance prohibition.

Chapter 8 creates no operator-admission rule. It preserves projection as derived contextual function and PMS Base as the sole owner of operator identity.

### 8.10 Operator Weighting Is Not Operator Replacement

```text
operator weighting
≠ operator replacement
```

A configuration or composite may display source-supported differences in the relative load, accessibility, visibility, persistence, threshold, or temporal effect of existing PMS operator occurrences. Such weighting can materially affect how praxis develops and which continuations become salient or costly.

Weighting does not revise the operator grammar.

```text
relative prominence of existing operator occurrences
≠ changed operator inventory
≠ changed dependency order
```

The distinction is necessary because descriptions such as “dominant,” “weak,” “suppressed,” or “amplified” can be analytically useful while inviting grammar inflation. In canonical terms, dominance is not exclusivity. A highly salient occurrence does not remove the dependencies that make its function possible. A weakly visible occurrence does not cease to exist as an operator type. A recurrent profile does not become a stable person essence or new primitive.

Weighting is also dimension-specific. One occurrence may be highly visible in recorded language while another carries greater temporal persistence, cost, threshold effect, or dependency load. An analysis that compresses these dimensions into one undifferentiated number can produce the same substitution error as direct replacement. The weighting record must therefore identify the comparison dimension rather than merely attach a strength label.

Weighting may vary by role, interval, source access, or analytical granularity. A configuration-wide claim cannot be inferred from one participant, one document, or one short temporal window without a new warrant. Conversely, local variation does not automatically defeat every bounded profile. The responsible question is whether the declared weighting changes the tested reconstruction while the full operator relation remains visible.

```text
visibility weighting
≠ temporal weighting
≠ dependency relation
≠ total operator importance
```

A profile can therefore be descriptively compact without being exhaustive. It can remain provisional where rival dimensions or role-specific weightings are unresolved. No profile score may compensate for missing sources, absent PraxisPurchase, failed Type Integrity, or grammar replacement.

The Δ–Ψ grammar remains unchanged in:

- operator names;
- canonical order;
- dependencies;
- abstract type identity;
- non-decomposability;
- absence of newly inserted operators.

```text
weighting may alter observed configuration form
≠ weighting alters PMS Base
```

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
Ω is dominant in configuration K3.
Therefore Ω replaces the operators on which it depends,
and K3 follows an Ω-only grammar.
```

The claim turns relative analytical prominence into dependency deletion and operator replacement. It also risks converting a configuration-bound observation into a global type or person label.

A named profile is not automatically warranted. It requires stable criteria, source traces, bounded scope, alternative profiles, and a discriminable praxis difference. Even then, the profile remains a derived description of existing operator relations.

#### Admissible weighting claim

```text
within configuration K3
and claim scope Q,
Ω-related occurrences carry greater visible load
relative to specified comparison occurrences,
while Δ–Ψ identity, order, and dependencies remain unchanged
```

The claim must state:

- which occurrences are compared;
- which weighting dimension is used;
- which sources support the comparison;
- which frame, granularity, and temporal scope apply;
- whether the weighting changes over time or by role;
- what praxis-relevant difference follows;
- which rival weighting profiles remain plausible;
- why the result does not imply operator replacement or person typing.

#### C8-WEIGHT-01 — local dominance treated as grammar replacement

A bounded review configuration shows strong Ω-related binding and persistence in the available records. The analyst concludes that Ω has replaced the preceding operator relations and that the project participants instantiate a new Ω-type.

The valid and invalid claims separate:

```text
configuration-bound Ω weighting
→ may remain a testable derived profile

Δ–Ψ dependency deletion
→ failed

new Ω-type person classification
→ prohibited
```

Because the grammar-replacement claim directly revises PMS Base and extends the result into person typing, the continuation requires:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop preserves any narrower source-supported observation about operator weighting. It blocks grammar revision, stable person typing, and authority inheritance.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- PMS Base for operator identity, order, and dependencies;
- Chapter 0 for claim and authority boundaries;
- Chapter 1 for occurrence/type and derived-profile boundaries;
- Chapter 2 for comparison axes and scope;
- Chapter 5 for Type Integrity and Contextual Boundedness;
- Chapter 6 for non-compensation and Mandatory Stop;
- Chapter 7 for Source, claim, governance, and extension records;
- Chapter 35 for the later primary RETYPE procedure governing weighting, modulation, and profiles.

Chapter 8 does not define the full operator-weighting procedure in advance. It establishes the foundational prohibition against replacement, reordering, dependency deletion, primitive inflation, and person typing.

#### WP2 comparison-matrix extension

| Non-equivalence | Invalid identity claim | Admissible relation or transformation | Primary definition return |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| path ≠ sequence | supported order is already an actually traversed path | a declared sequence may supply selected inputs to a separately warranted path formation | Chapters 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 |
| path ≠ trajectory | every traversed path is a sedimented trajectory | a path may support trajectory formation where historical load changes later continuation possibilities | Chapters 3, 5, 6, 7 |
| trajectory ≠ path dependence | trajectory existence proves historical determination or inevitability | a trajectory may be separately tested for bounded path-dependence properties | Chapters 3, 5, 6, 7 |
| origin type ≠ target function | a path performing a frame-function becomes a Frame operator | `PROJECT_AS` preserves path typing while declaring a bounded frame-function separately | Chapters 1, 4, 5, 6, 7 |
| projection ≠ operator identity | recurrent operator-like function creates a new PMS operator | a source-traceable contextual function remains derived and non-primitive | PMS Base; Chapters 0, 1, 4, 5, 7 |
| operator weighting ≠ operator replacement | local dominance deletes dependencies or creates a new type | source-supported relative weighting may describe a configuration while Δ–Ψ remains unchanged | PMS Base; Chapters 0, 1, 2, 5, 6, 7; later Chapter 35 |

Together with the four WP1 rows, the running matrix now contains ten of the thirteen foundational pairs. The remaining analogy, recursion, and authority rows belong to WP3.

#### WP2 cross-pair pressure

The six pairs form two linked control sequences:

```text
sequence
→ path
→ trajectory
→ path-dependence test
```

and:

```text
origin-typed object
→ PROJECT_AS target function
→ no operator identity
→ no grammar replacement
```

The arrows indicate burden-increasing relations, not automatic promotion. Each step requires a new delimited claim, an applicable operation or property test, and its own Source, Loss, Alternatives, Governance, and routing record.

```text
new relation
= new testable claim
```

A failure at a stronger step does not erase a weaker result. A valid weaker result does not compensate for a failed stronger claim.

#### WP2 boundary and handoff

Sections 8.5–8.10 establish:

- path versus sequence;
- path versus trajectory;
- trajectory versus path dependence;
- origin type versus target function;
- projection versus operator identity;
- operator weighting versus operator replacement;
- six additional comparison-matrix rows;
- six controlled pressure cases: `C8-PATH-01`, `C8-TRAJ-01`, `C8-PD-01`, `C8-TYPE-01`, `C8-PROJ-01`, and `C8-WEIGHT-01`;
- explicit protection of Δ–Ψ identity, order, dependencies, non-decomposability, and closed operator inventory;
- preservation of weaker temporal, functional, and weighting claims when stronger identity claims fail.

The WP2 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves the still-pending analogy, recursion, authority, integrated-catalogue, Formal-Model-handoff, Foundations-completion, and provisional-lock duties of WP3–WP4.

### 8.11 Structural Analogy Is Not Valid Projection

```text
structural analogy
≠ valid projection
```

A structural analogy identifies a bounded resemblance between objects, relations, temporal forms, or functions. It can help formulate a question, compare configurations, reveal a candidate pattern, or make an otherwise opaque distinction legible. Its value does not depend on the compared objects sharing an origin type or on one object being admissibly usable as a function in the other's context.

`PROJECT_AS` carries a stronger burden. It begins with an origin-typed source object, declares a bounded target context and target function, preserves source reference and origin type, identifies the constitutive source features on which the function depends, and passes continuity and admissibility tests. Analogy alone supplies none of these conditions automatically.

```text
similar form
≠ preserved semantics
≠ source-result dependency
≠ bounded target function
```

The distinction is especially important in cross-domain work. A reviewer may observe that an unfulfilled reconsideration promise resembles institutional memory, technical debt, deferred maintenance, or a standing reservation. The comparison may be insightful. It does not yet show that the review path performs one of those functions in a declared target context, that the selected source structures carry the function, or that the terms retain their meaning across domains.

The non-equivalence blocks two opposite errors:

1. treating resemblance as automatic passage into `PROJECT_AS`;
2. treating every analogy that fails projection as useless or false.

```text
failed projection
≠ failed analogy automatically
```

An analogy may remain bounded and explicit under:

```text
analogy_only
```

That Output Class does not conceal an incomplete projection. It records that the warranted claim is resemblance rather than typed contextual function.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review resembles institutional memory.
Therefore P_review validly functions as institutional memory
in every later governance context.
```

The conclusion silently imports:

- target-context validity;
- semantic continuity;
- source-result dependency;
- temporal persistence;
- cross-context generality;
- authority over later interpretation.

None follows from resemblance alone. A useful analogy can be rhetorically strong while remaining structurally under-declared as a projection.

The cross-domain burden cannot be discharged by label repetition. Reusing the same word in source and target domains does not establish common reference, preserved type, equivalent causal role, or functional continuity. Conversely, different vocabulary does not by itself defeat a relation. The test concerns the declared structure and dependency, not lexical identity.

```text
same label
≠ same function

different label
≠ no relation
```

#### Admissible analogy

A bounded analogy claim may state:

```text
Within comparison frame F,
P_review resembles an institutional-memory structure
with respect to retained reopening burden and later retrieval,
without claiming type identity or a valid target function.
```

The analogy should declare:

- the comparison frame;
- the specific dimensions of resemblance;
- the dimensions not claimed to transfer;
- the source basis for each compared side;
- the temporal and contextual scope;
- the reason the comparison changes the tested reconstruction;
- the point at which analogy would require a separate `PROJECT_AS` record;
- the possibility that no projection is warranted.

An analogy is stronger when its similarities and disanalogies are both visible. Unmarked disanalogy does not merely reduce elegance; it can conceal the exact point at which functional continuity fails.

```text
explicit disanalogy
→ bounded comparison
≠ defect automatically
```

#### Admissible projection relation

A projection candidate requires a distinct claim:

```text
source object: P_review
origin type: path
target context: K3
target function: bounded institutional-memory function
load-bearing source features:
  retained reconsideration expectation
  documented N1
  changed reopening burden
  later retrieval dependence
validity scope:
  specified review context and interval
```

The candidate must then pass the Chapter-5 continuity duties and Chapter-6 Admissibility Band. The analogy may motivate the candidate, but it cannot substitute for those tests.

```text
analogy
→ may generate projection hypothesis
≠ projection passage
```

#### C8-ANALOGY-01 — useful resemblance below projection

The project-review path contains a documented reconsideration promise, bounded non-realization, and later retrieval of the unresolved issue. An analyst compares this structure to institutional memory because the earlier unresolved relation remains available and changes later reopening work.

The analogy is source-supported on three dimensions:

- retained historical reference;
- later retrieval;
- changed reopening burden.

The available sources do not establish that the path performs a stable memory function across governance contexts, that later actors depend on it in the same way, or that the comparison preserves the target-domain semantics.

The appropriate result is:

```text
analogy_only
```

The result preserves the useful comparison and blocks the stronger universal projection. A later `PROJECT_AS` claim may be entered only with a declared target context, target function, source dependency, continuity record, loss disclosure, and bounded claim.

#### C8-NC-01 — unresolved analogy/projection boundary

A second record contains evidence that the unresolved review path is later cited both to retrieve prior commitments and to delimit what may be reopened. Two rival relations remain plausible:

```text
candidate A:
structural analogy to institutional memory

candidate B:
bounded PROJECT_AS claim
with a local retrieval function
```

The available source material does not establish whether later retrieval depends constitutively on the earlier path or merely uses the same archive as an illustrative comparison. The record cannot responsibly collapse the rivals, and neither candidate defeats the other.

The claim-relative result is:

```text
non_capture
```

The record must preserve:

- both candidate relations;
- the unresolved constitutive dependency;
- the target context and interval;
- the evidence needed for re-entry;
- the prohibition on treating Non-Capture as projection passage.

```text
non_capture
≠ hidden projection
≠ protected analogy claim
```

If later evidence establishes dependency, a new projection claim may be tested. If it establishes only bounded resemblance, `analogy_only` remains appropriate. The new source state does not retroactively make the earlier stronger claim valid.

- Chapter 0 for claim, scope, and authority boundaries;
- Chapter 1 for source-object and derived-function identity;
- Chapter 2 for comparison frame, target context, and scope;
- Chapter 4 for `PROJECT_AS` operation identity;
- Chapter 5 for origin-type preservation, Reference Continuity, Functional Continuity, Temporal Continuity, and Contextual Boundedness;
- Chapter 6 for TraceableLoad, Counterfactual Sensitivity, `analogy_only`, Claim Reduction, and Non-Capture;
- Chapter 7 for separate analogy, projection, alternative, loss, governance, and routing records;
- later RETYPE chapters for the full projection and analogy-stress procedures.

Chapter 8 does not define analogy or projection anew. It blocks resemblance from inheriting typed functional passage while preserving analogy as a legitimate bounded result.

### 8.12 Recursion Is Not Completeness

```text
recursion
≠ completeness
```

STRATA permits previously formed objects, occurrences, composites, and transformation results to become sources for later transformation claims. A composite may be decomposed, a finer reconstruction may be recomposed, and an origin-typed result may be projected into a bounded function. This recursive availability increases inspectability and analytical reach.

It does not guarantee complete capture.

```text
further transformation is possible
≠ every relevant structure is recoverable
≠ every question has a responsible answer
≠ continuation is always required
```

Recursion remains constrained by:

- available source material;
- Reference and Type Integrity;
- declared frame, granularity, level, and scope;
- PraxisPurchase and TraceableLoad;
- loss and irrecoverability;
- alternative transformations;
- Claim and Authority Ceilings;
- Stop and Non-Capture.

A new transformation is a new testable claim. It does not erase the status of the preceding claim and does not inherit its warrant automatically.

```text
new transformation
= new testable claim
```

```text
recursive continuation
≠ cumulative certainty
≠ automatic warrant inheritance
```

The distinction blocks two symmetric errors. The first is completion inflation: because another operation can be attempted, the analyst assumes that enough recursion will eventually produce a complete representation. The second is premature closure: because complete representation is unavailable, every further bounded transformation is dismissed as pointless.

A responsible recursive pass may still produce PraxisPurchase, reveal loss, distinguish rivals, or identify a Stop condition. Its legitimacy is local. Its availability does not impose an obligation to continue.

#### Invalid identity claim

```text
P_review can be decomposed,
its components can be recomposed,
and the result can be projected.
Therefore the transformation chain can in principle
recover the complete praxis structure.
```

The conclusion confuses operational availability with epistemic totality. Each pass remains selective. New frames and granularities can reveal relations while compressing others. Some source structures may be absent, uncertain, excluded, or irrecoverable. Some distinctions may fall below the Relevance Floor. Some abstraction may exceed the Traceability Ceiling. Rival reconstructions may remain genuinely unresolved.

Repeated operations can also amplify earlier errors. If an invalid type replacement, unsupported path claim, or source-indifferent projection becomes the input to later records, recursion compounds rather than repairs the defect.

```text
more recursive depth
≠ correction of an invalid premise
```

#### Admissible recursive relation

A bounded recursive claim may state:

```text
DECOMPOSE occurrence record R1
→ finer reconstruction R2

COMPOSE selected results from R2
→ revised composite R3

PROJECT_AS R3
→ bounded target function R4
```

Each occurrence requires:

- one operation kind;
- a separate source and target declaration;
- its own admissibility findings;
- its own five-part loss record;
- alternatives and governance;
- a local result;
- an explicit handoff into the next record.

The chain declaration preserves every local outcome.

```text
chain result
≠ replacement of occurrence results
```

A later admissible occurrence cannot rescue a failed earlier occurrence. A later failure cannot erase an earlier admissible result. A Stop at one link blocks only the continuations that depend on the failed or prohibited claim.

#### Stop as a positive recursive result

Recursion remains disciplined only if stopping is possible and informative.

An Optional Stop is appropriate when another transformation is available but unnecessary for the tested claim. A Mandatory Stop applies when continuation would depend on a known type error, failed traceability, prohibited authority transfer, grammar revision, missing reference continuity, or another load-bearing failure.

```text
available next operation
+ no additional warranted reconstruction
→ optional stop
```

```text
available next operation
+ failed load-bearing prerequisite
→ mandatory_stop
```

Stop does not assert that no future source or claim could justify re-entry. It states that the present continuation is not admissible under the declared conditions.

#### C8-REC-01 — recursive availability treated as completeness

An analyst opens `P_review` into finer meeting-level structures, recomposes selected residues into a revised path, and projects that path as an institutional-memory function. The analyst then claims that the chain is complete because every result can be opened again.

The individual occurrences may be inspectable, but the completion claim fails for three reasons:

1. the archive contains an undocumented interval;
2. rival selections remain plausible;
3. the target function is bounded to one later context.

The valid claims separate:

```text
recursive chain availability
→ retained

bounded local results
→ tested occurrence by occurrence

complete capture
→ claim_reduction_required
```

If the analyst continues transforming solely to protect the completeness claim, the chain reaches Mandatory Stop. Further work remains possible only under a new delimited question with declared PraxisPurchase and source support.

#### C8-STOP-01 — continued derivation from known collapse

A prior record has already established that the path's bounded frame-function does not change its origin type and does not create a `Frame` operator. A successor chain nevertheless uses the rejected operator identity to decompose the alleged new operator, assign it a permanent position in Δ–Ψ, and derive an authority-bearing participant classification.

The continuation depends on three known failures:

- origin-type replacement;
- primitive and grammar inflation;
- person typing and authority transfer.

The result is:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop preserves:

- the path as source object;
- any separately supported bounded frame-function;
- the documented failure of operator identity;
- the loss and alternatives already recorded.

It blocks only the downstream records that inherit the failed identity. Changing frame, granularity, level, composite, or target label cannot reopen the same claim without a new warrant.

```text
new analytical position
≠ erased failed claim
```

#### Recursion and Non-Capture

Non-Capture is not a defect that recursion is guaranteed to cure. It may identify a principled current limit: rival reconstructions remain source-supported, constitutive evidence is absent, or available transformations cannot responsibly select among them.

```text
more recursion
≠ forced capture
```

A Non-Capture record should state the rival claims, preserved material, limiting condition, prohibited inference, and re-entry condition. Further operations are admissible only when they address that condition rather than merely restating the rivals in a different form.

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- Chapter 0 for non-totality, claim boundaries, and anti-immunization;
- Chapter 1 for derived objects and recurrence without primitive inflation;
- Chapter 2 for frame, granularity, level, and scope changes;
- Chapter 4 for operations, chains, and non-invertibility;
- Chapter 5 for continuity across repeated transformations;
- Chapter 6 for the Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, non-compensation, Stop, and Non-Capture;
- Chapter 7 for occurrence records, chain declarations, local results, lineage, formal diagnostics, and re-entry;
- later LIMITS chapters for full recursion, anti-immunization, Stop, and Non-Capture procedures.

Chapter 8 does not establish a new recursion procedure. It blocks operational repeatability from becoming a completeness or mandatory-continuation claim.

### 8.13 Legibility Is Not Authority

```text
legibility
≠ authority
```

Legibility is the degree to which a reconstruction, transformation, record, or formal artifact can be inspected, compared, criticized, and traced. Tables, identifiers, schemas, explicit source paths, structured loss fields, machine validation, and reproducible checks can materially improve legibility.

They do not confer truth, semantic validity, causal proof, normative legitimacy, decision entitlement, or application authority.

```text
better legibility
≠ higher truth

formal precision
≠ empirical confirmation

technical implementation
≠ ontological evidence

successful transformation
≠ additional application authority
```

This pair closes the authority boundary running through all Foundations chapters. STRATA can make claims more explicit and auditable. That achievement changes the quality of inspection, not the rank of the analyst, the legitimacy of an institution, the status of a person, or the right to impose an outcome.

The distinction applies at several layers:

- polished prose can remain semantically wrong;
- a complete Shared Record can remain inadmissible;
- a schema-valid record can carry unsupported content;
- a passed package audit can coexist with an empirically false claim;
- a formally routed Output Class can remain bounded to one claim and context;
- an external warrant pointer can reference authority without creating it;
- a highly legible model can remain outside its Source or Claim Ceiling.

```text
machine-readable consistency
≠ truth proof
```

```text
audit success
≠ domain authorization
```

#### Invalid authority transfer

```text
The record is complete,
schema-valid,
linked,
and reproducible.
Therefore its transformation result
is authoritative for organizational action.
```

The premise establishes technical and documentary properties. It does not establish empirical truth, causal sufficiency, normative legitimacy, legal competence, organizational mandate, or safe application. Even an admissible structural claim remains governed by its Authority Ceiling and by external warrants.

The reverse collapse also fails. A result does not become false merely because it is difficult to formalize or because no machine-valid representation exists. Formal legibility is valuable but not the sole carrier of meaning or support.

```text
not machine-captured
≠ false automatically
```

#### Admissible legibility claim

A bounded claim may state:

```text
Record R is more auditable than Record R0
because source references, operation identity,
loss, alternatives, governance,
and routing are explicit.
```

This claim concerns inspectability. It may support review, error detection, comparison, reproducibility, or revision. It should not be expanded into a claim that R is truer, more legitimate, more complete, or entitled to govern action.

A technical implementation may also be judged fit for a declared validation purpose:

```text
Schema S detects explicit field-level inconsistency
within declared record syntax.
```

That statement remains separate from:

```text
Schema S decides semantic validity,
empirical truth,
or application authority.
```

#### C8-AUTH-01 — machine validity treated as entitlement

A transformation record is duplicate-key clean, schema-valid, linked to the declared sources, and routed to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. An implementation then uses this status to authorize an irreversible organizational classification and claims that the formal audit transfers authority to the decision.

The record may retain its bounded structural result. The application claim fails because:

- the Output Class is claim-relative;
- the Authority Ceiling remains external;
- no person-level or irreversible classification is authorized;
- schema and package validity are non-substantive;
- `authority_inheritance` is prohibited.

The unlicensed continuation receives:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

The Stop does not negate the value of legibility. It prevents documentary success from becoming authority inheritance.

#### Formal-model boundary

The Formal Model may validate:

- required fields;
- enumerated operation and Output-Class values;
- structural compatibility;
- declared provenance;
- loss-category presence;
- explicit authority-inheritance prohibition;
- package fingerprints and schema conformance.

It may not automatically decide:

- semantic identity or analogy quality;
- empirical truth or causality;
- legitimacy or moral rank;
- diagnosis or person classification;
- organizational competence;
- whether a bounded structural result should govern action.

```text
validation capability
≠ adjudicative authority
```

#### Definition return

This pair returns to:

- PMS Base for application guardrails and authority limits;
- Chapter 0 for No Meta-PMS, No Ontology of Layers, and Claim/Authority Boundaries;
- Chapter 2 for scope and comparison limits;
- Chapter 5 for Contextual Boundedness;
- Chapter 6 for Claim Ceiling, Authority Ceiling, non-compensation, and Mandatory Stop;
- Chapter 7 for Governance, formal diagnostics, schema boundaries, external warrant, and `authority_inheritance: prohibited`;
- the Formal-Model boundary and later Integrated Audit and LIMITS chapters.

Chapter 8 does not define a new authority doctrine. It consolidates the prohibition against transferring truth or entitlement from legibility, formal precision, technical implementation, or successful transformation.

#### Integrated thirteen-pair comparison matrix

| Non-equivalence | Invalid identity claim | Admissible relation or transformation | Primary definition return |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| finer granularity ≠ higher truth | more distinctions confer universal truth rank | a finer reconstruction may perform better for a declared claim under bounded sources and disclosed costs | Chapters 0, 2, 6 |
| relative level ≠ ontological layer | analytical height establishes a more real or fundamental stratum | an object may be located relatively within a declared frame and reference set | Chapters 0, 2 |
| composition ≠ lossless addition | a composite contains all source content without selection or loss | `COMPOSE` may form a real new analytical object with explicit formation and five-part loss | Chapters 1, 4, 6, 7 |
| decomposition ≠ discovery of final constituents | finer reconstruction reveals ultimate elements or essence | `DECOMPOSE` may produce a source-bound, revisable finer reconstruction of the same reference object | Chapters 1, 2, 4, 6, 7 |
| path ≠ sequence | supported order is already an actually traversed path | a sequence may provide selected inputs to a separately warranted path formation | Chapters 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 |
| path ≠ trajectory | every traversed path is a sedimented historical development | a path may support trajectory formation where historical load changes continuation possibilities | Chapters 3, 5, 6, 7 |
| trajectory ≠ path dependence | trajectory existence proves historical determination or inevitability | a trajectory may be separately tested for bounded path-dependence properties | Chapters 3, 5, 6, 7 |
| origin type ≠ target function | a path performing a frame-function becomes a `Frame` operator | `PROJECT_AS` preserves path typing while declaring a bounded function separately | Chapters 1, 4, 5, 6, 7 |
| projection ≠ operator identity | recurrent operator-like function creates a new PMS operator | a source-traceable contextual function remains derived and non-primitive | PMS Base; Chapters 0, 1, 4, 5, 7 |
| operator weighting ≠ operator replacement | local dominance deletes dependencies or creates a new type | dimension-specific weighting may describe a configuration while Δ–Ψ remains unchanged | PMS Base; Chapters 0, 1, 2, 5, 6, 7; later Chapter 35 |
| structural analogy ≠ valid projection | resemblance automatically establishes typed contextual function | a bounded analogy may remain `analogy_only`, or motivate a separately tested `PROJECT_AS` claim | Chapters 0, 1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7; later RETYPE |
| recursion ≠ completeness | repeatable operations guarantee total capture or require endless continuation | a new bounded transformation may be tested while Stop, loss, and Non-Capture remain constitutive | Chapters 0, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7; later LIMITS |
| legibility ≠ authority | formal, machine, or audit success confers truth or application entitlement | legibility may improve inspection and error detection while authority remains separately warranted | PMS Base; Chapters 0, 2, 5, 6, 7; later Integrated Audit and LIMITS |

The matrix is an audit return surface, not a substitute for the definitions, source tests, operation records, Admissibility Band, or Output routing it references.

#### Integrated catalogue use

The catalogue should be used in a controlled sequence:

```text
1. delimit the claim
2. identify the terms or statuses being equated
3. locate the primary definitions
4. name the proposed relation type
5. test the relation-specific burden
6. remove identity or authority transfer
7. preserve any weaker supported claim
8. apply continuity, admissibility, loss, alternatives, and governance
9. route the delimited claim
10. record Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry where applicable
```

This is an audit sequence, not a fourth operation and not an automatic decision tree.

A single claim may breach more than one pair. For example:

```text
finer granularity
→ higher truth
→ higher relative level
→ ontological layer
→ application authority
```

The audit must segment the transfers rather than assign one global label. One collapse may be repaired by claim reduction, another may preserve only analogy, and a third may require Mandatory Stop. The result remains claim-relative.

```text
multiple non-equivalence breaches
≠ one undifferentiated failure
```

The catalogue also prevents analytical laundering. A failed claim cannot be restored merely by changing its vocabulary:

```text
operator identity
→ renamed as projection

ontological layer
→ renamed as relative level

universal truth rank
→ renamed as granularity preference

authority transfer
→ renamed as legibility
```

```text
label substitution
≠ repaired distinction
```

A new frame, granularity, level, composite, target function, record, or recursive pass creates a new testable claim. It does not delete the earlier failure, Stop, or Non-Capture.

#### Integrated Stop and Non-Capture boundary

Mandatory Stop applies where continuation depends on a known load-bearing collapse, prohibited grammar change, person typing, or authority inheritance. Non-Capture applies where responsibly delimited rival claims remain unresolved after available distinctions and sources are exhausted.

```text
mandatory_stop
≠ non_capture
```

```text
known prohibited continuation
→ mandatory_stop

responsibly unresolved rival claims
→ non_capture
```

Neither result protects the original strong claim. Stop blocks it; Non-Capture withholds selection among bounded rivals.

#### WP3 boundary and handoff

Sections 8.11–8.13 establish:

- structural analogy versus valid projection;
- recursion versus completeness;
- legibility versus authority;
- the final three comparison-matrix rows and the integrated thirteen-pair matrix;
- five controlled pressure cases: `C8-ANALOGY-01`, `C8-REC-01`, `C8-AUTH-01`, `C8-STOP-01`, and `C8-NC-01`;
- bounded `analogy_only` without false projection passage;
- recursion with occurrence-level results, Optional Stop, Mandatory Stop, loss, and Non-Capture;
- formal and machine legibility without truth, legitimacy, person judgment, or application authority;
- integrated catalogue use without a fourth operation, automatic Output Class, or replacement of Chapters 0–7.

All eighteen prepared Chapter-8 pressure cases are now represented in canonical prose. Their later Case files, appendix expansions, Formal-Model synchronization, Foundations completion audit, and provisional-lock decision remain assigned to WP4 and downstream production.

The WP3 result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality is not a known local defect. It preserves the integrated synchronization, Formal-Model handoff, Foundations-completion, Case-registration, repository-audit, and provisional-lock duties of WP4.

#### Chapter 8 integrated completion and Foundations provisional lock

Sections 8.1–8.13 now form one integrated foundational non-equivalence audit layer. The completed chapter owns the audit meaning of non-equivalence and the closed thirteen-pair minimum catalogue. It does not acquire ownership of the object, coordinate, temporal, operation, continuity, admissibility, record, claim-boundary, or authority definitions to which the pairs return.

```text
primary definitions in Chapters 0–7
+ Chapter-8 non-equivalence audit returns
→ reusable category-collapse control
```

The integrated completion test confirms that every required pair has:

- one stable canonical statement;
- an explicit identity or authority collapse that it blocks;
- at least one admissible relation, comparison, property test, analogy, or transformation that remains possible;
- a primary Definition Return to Chapters 0–7 or PMS Base;
- a bounded pressure case;
- a route that preserves weaker supported claims rather than treating every breach as total failure.

The catalogue remains non-definitional and non-self-routing:

```text
non-equivalence finding
≠ automatic empirical counterclaim
≠ automatic canonical Output Class
≠ automatic Stop
```

A breach must still be segmented by claim, related to the governing definitions, tested through continuity and the Admissibility Band, recorded with loss, alternatives, governance, and status distinctions, and routed under the ten-class inventory. `mandatory_stop`, `non_capture`, `claim_reduction_required`, `analogy_only`, `failed_transformation`, and the other canonical classes retain their distinct burdens.

The eighteen Chapter-8 identifiers remain assigned production duties:

```text
C8-AUD-01      C8-AUD-02
C8-GRAN-01     C8-GRAN-02
C8-LEVEL-01    C8-COMP-01
C8-DEC-01      C8-PATH-01
C8-TRAJ-01     C8-PD-01
C8-TYPE-01     C8-PROJ-01
C8-WEIGHT-01   C8-ANALOGY-01
C8-REC-01      C8-AUTH-01
C8-STOP-01     C8-NC-01
```

Their presence in canonical prose demonstrates required pressure coverage. It does not convert them into produced `03_cases/*` files, empirical evidence, calibration data, or completed appendix material.

The bounded Formal-Model handoff mirrors only explicit catalogue identity, relation, and authority constraints. The Decision Tree may consume declared non-equivalence flags, require claim segmentation, preserve weaker claims, and prevent a flag from becoming an automatic route. It may not infer every semantic collapse from natural language, decide whether an analogy is meaningful, establish higher truth, determine path dependence, authorize projection, or transfer authority from validation success.

```text
machine-detectable explicit contradiction
≠ complete semantic non-equivalence audit
```

No new operation, Rule, Output Class, object primitive, PMS operator, universal scale, compensatory score, person type, or authority-bearing status is introduced. `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, and `PROJECT_AS` remain the only STRATA operations. Δ–Ψ remains unchanged in identity, order, dependency, and non-decomposability.

#### Foundations completion boundary

Chapters 0–8 now form a provisionally complete Foundations block:

```text
Chapter 0  → claim and authority boundary
Chapter 1  → object model
Chapter 2  → analytical coordinates
Chapter 3  → temporal object architecture
Chapter 4  → operation grammar
Chapter 5  → type, function, context, and continuity
Chapter 6  → Admissibility Band
Chapter 7  → Shared Transformation Record
Chapter 8  → non-equivalence audit layer
```

Provisionally complete means that the required foundational distinctions, operations, continuity duties, admissibility limits, record architecture, output boundaries, and audit returns are sufficiently explicit to begin PATH without an unresolved foundational type or operation question. It does not mean final freeze, empirical confirmation, exhaustive case production, completed appendices, completed Formal Model, or immunity from later corpus findings.

```text
Foundations completion
≠ Foundations finality
≠ higher authority
```

PATH may now operationalize temporal composition and trajectory formation under these controls. It may not redefine Foundations terms, create a fourth operation, weaken the Admissibility Band, convert relative level into ontology, treat sequence as path, treat recursion as completeness, or inherit authority from the fact that Foundations passed an internal audit.

PATH entry is therefore permission to begin bounded drafting, not pre-admission of any PATH claim. Each Chapter 9–17 occurrence must still declare source and target, operation identity, loss, alternatives, governance, continuity, admissibility, and its local result. A Foundations return supplies definitions and constraints; it does not supply empirical facts or preselected routes. If PATH exposes a Foundations ambiguity, the exact ownership conflict must be named rather than repaired through silent redefinition.

The block remains reopenable only for a documented contradiction, duplicated or competing definition, failed case burden, broken Definition Return, formal-model mismatch, source-supported correction, or later corpus audit finding that cannot be repaired locally without altering Foundations. Reopening one claim or chapter does not erase unaffected provisionally locked results.

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```
